# =============================================================================
# HTML WEBSITE CONFIGURATION
# =============================================================================
# This configuration is optimized for the interactive website build.
#
# Key Features:
# - Modern website navigation with organized sidebar sections
# - TikZ diagrams automatically converted to SVG for web browsers
# - Clean page layout without citation boxes or license footers
# - Interactive quizzes and cross-reference connections
# - Streamlined filters (no PDF-specific features like sidenotes)
# - No chapter numbering for cleaner web presentation
#
# Usage:
#   make build         # Uses this config automatically
#   make preview       # Uses this config for development
#
# Part of the dual-configuration system:
#   _quarto-html.yml   # This file (website optimized)
#   _quarto-pdf.yml    # Academic PDF optimized
# =============================================================================

project:
  type: website
  output-dir: _build/html

  post-render:
    - scripts/clean_svgs.py
    - scripts/fix_cross_references.py

  preview:
    browser: false
    navigate: false

website:

---
bibliography: introduction.bib
quiz: introduction_quizzes.json
concepts: introduction_concepts.yml
glossary: introduction_glossary.json
crossrefs: introduction_xrefs.json
---

# Introduction {#sec-introduction}
::: {layout-narrow}
::: {.column-margin}
_DALL·E 3 Prompt: A detailed, rectangular, flat 2D illustration depicting a roadmap of a book's chapters on machine learning systems, set on a crisp, clean white background. The image features a winding road traveling through various symbolic landmarks. Each landmark represents a chapter topic: Introduction, ML Systems, Deep Learning, AI Workflow, Data Engineering, AI Frameworks, AI Training, Efficient AI, Model Optimizations, AI Acceleration, Benchmarking AI, On-Device Learning, Embedded AIOps, Security & Privacy, Responsible AI, Sustainable AI, AI for Good, Robust AI, Generative AI. The style is clean, modern, and flat, suitable for a technical book, with each landmark clearly labeled with its chapter title._
:::

\noindent
![](images/png/cover_introduction.png)

:::

## Purpose {.unnumbered}

_Why must we master the engineering principles that govern systems capable of learning, adapting, and operating at massive scale?_

Machine learning represents the most significant transformation in computing since programmable computers, enabling systems whose behavior emerges from data rather than explicit instructions. This transformation requires new engineering foundations because traditional software engineering principles cannot address systems that learn and adapt based on experience. Every major technological challenge, from climate modeling and medical diagnosis to autonomous transportation, requires systems that process vast amounts of data and operate reliably despite uncertainty. Understanding ML systems engineering determines our ability to solve complex problems that exceed human cognitive capacity. This discipline provides the foundation for building systems that can scale across deployment environments, from massive data centers to resource-constrained edge devices, establishing the technical groundwork for technological progress in the 21st century.

::: {.callout-tip title="Learning Objectives"}

- Define machine learning systems as integrated computing systems comprising data, algorithms, and infrastructure

- Distinguish ML systems engineering from traditional software engineering through failure pattern analysis

- Analyze interdependencies between data, algorithms, and computing infrastructure using the AI Triangle framework

- Trace the historical evolution of AI paradigms from symbolic systems through statistical learning to deep learning

- Evaluate the implications of Sutton's "Bitter Lesson" for modern ML systems engineering priorities

- Compare silent performance degradation in ML systems with traditional software failure modes

- Contrast ML system lifecycle phases with traditional software development

- Classify real-world challenges in ML systems across data, model, system, and ethical categories

- Apply the five-pillar framework to evaluate ML system architectures

:::

## The Engineering Revolution in Artificial Intelligence {#sec-introduction-engineering-revolution-artificial-intelligence-a3eb}

Engineering practice today stands at an inflection point comparable to the most transformative periods in technological history. The Industrial Revolution established mechanical engineering as a discipline for managing physical forces, while the Digital Revolution formalized computational engineering to handle algorithmic complexity. Today, artificial intelligence systems require a new engineering paradigm for systems that exhibit learned behaviors, autonomous adaptation, and operational scales that exceed conventional software engineering methodologies.

This shift reconceptualizes the nature of engineered systems. Traditional deterministic software architectures operate according to explicitly programmed instructions, yielding predictable outputs for given inputs. In contrast, machine learning systems are probabilistic architectures whose behaviors emerge from statistical patterns extracted from training data. This transformation introduces engineering challenges that define the discipline of machine learning systems engineering: ensuring reliability in systems whose behaviors are learned rather than programmed, achieving scalability for systems processing petabyte-scale[^fn-petabyte-scale] datasets while serving billions of concurrent users, and maintaining robustness when operational data distributions diverge from training distributions.

These challenges establish the theoretical and practical foundations of ML systems engineering as a distinct academic discipline. This chapter provides the conceptual foundation for understanding both the historical evolution that created this field and the engineering principles that differentiate machine learning systems from traditional software architectures. The analysis synthesizes perspectives from computer science, systems engineering, and statistical learning theory to establish a framework for the systematic study of intelligent systems.

Our investigation begins with the relationship between artificial intelligence as a research objective and machine learning as the computational methodology for achieving intelligent behavior. We then establish what constitutes a machine learning system, the integrated computing systems comprising data, algorithms, and infrastructure that this discipline builds. Through historical analysis, we trace the evolution of AI paradigms from symbolic reasoning systems through statistical learning approaches to contemporary deep learning architectures, demonstrating how each transition required new engineering solutions. This progression illuminates Sutton's "bitter lesson" of AI research: that domain-general computational methods ultimately supersede hand-crafted knowledge representations, positioning systems engineering as central to AI advancement.

This historical and technical foundation enables us to formally define this discipline. Following the pattern established by Computer Engineering's emergence from Electrical Engineering and Computer Science, we establish it as a field focused on building reliable, efficient, and scalable machine learning systems across computational platforms. This formal definition addresses both the nomenclature used in practice and the technical scope of what practitioners actually build.

Building upon this foundation, we introduce the theoretical frameworks that structure the analysis of ML systems throughout this text. We develop the AI Triangle framework, which models ML systems as three interdependent components (data, algorithms, and infrastructure) whose interactions determine system capabilities. We examine the machine learning system lifecycle, contrasting it with traditional software development methodologies to highlight the unique phases of problem formulation, data curation, model development, validation, deployment, and continuous maintenance that characterize ML system engineering.

These theoretical frameworks are substantiated through examination of representative deployment scenarios that demonstrate the diversity of engineering requirements across application domains. From autonomous vehicles operating under stringent latency constraints at the network edge to recommendation systems serving billions of users through cloud infrastructure, these case studies illustrate how deployment context shapes system architecture and engineering trade-offs.

The analysis culminates by identifying the core challenges that establish ML systems engineering as both a necessary and complex discipline: silent performance degradation patterns that require specialized monitoring approaches, data quality issues and distribution shifts that compromise model validity, requirements for model robustness and interpretability in high-stakes applications, infrastructure scalability demands that exceed conventional distributed systems, and ethical considerations that impose new categories of system requirements. These challenges provide the foundation for the five-pillar organizational framework that structures this text, partitioning ML systems engineering into interconnected sub-disciplines that enable the development of robust, scalable, and responsible artificial intelligence systems.

This chapter establishes the theoretical foundation for Part I: Systems Foundations, introducing the principles that underlie all subsequent analysis of ML systems engineering. The conceptual frameworks introduced here provide the analytical tools that will be refined and applied throughout subsequent chapters, culminating in a methodology for engineering systems capable of reliably delivering artificial intelligence capabilities in production environments.

## From Artificial Intelligence Vision to Machine Learning Practice {#sec-introduction-artificial-intelligence-vision-machine-learning-practice-c45a}

Having established AI's transformative impact across society, a question emerges: How do we actually create these intelligent capabilities? Understanding the relationship between Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning provides the key to answering this question and is central to everything that follows in this book.

AI represents the broad goal of creating systems that can perform tasks requiring human-like intelligence: recognizing images, understanding language, making decisions, and solving problems. AI is the what, the vision of intelligent machines that can learn, reason, and adapt.

Machine Learning (ML) represents the methodological approach and practical discipline for creating systems that demonstrate intelligent behavior. Rather than implementing intelligence through predetermined rules, machine learning provides the computational techniques to automatically discover patterns in data through mathematical processes. This methodology transforms AI's theoretical insights into functioning systems.

Consider the evolution of chess-playing systems as an example of this shift. The AI goal remains constant: "Create a system that can play chess like a human." However, the approaches differ:

- **Symbolic AI Approach (Pre-ML)**: Program the computer with all chess rules and hand-craft strategies like "control the center" and "protect the king." This requires expert programmers to explicitly encode thousands of chess principles, creating brittle systems that struggle with novel positions.

- **Machine Learning Approach**: Have the computer analyze millions of chess games to learn winning strategies automatically from data. Rather than programming specific moves, the system discovers patterns that lead to victory through statistical analysis of game outcomes.

This transformation illustrates why ML has become the dominant approach: In rule-based systems, humans translate domain expertise directly into code. In ML systems, humans curate training data, design learning architectures, and define success metrics, allowing the system to extract its own operational logic from examples. Data-driven systems can adapt to situations that programmers never anticipated, while rule-based systems remain constrained by their original programming.

Machine learning systems acquire recognition capabilities through processes that parallel human learning patterns. Object recognition develops through exposure to numerous examples, while natural language processing systems acquire linguistic capabilities through extensive textual analysis. These learning approaches operationalize theories of intelligence developed in AI research, building on mathematical foundations that we establish systematically throughout this text.

The distinction between AI as research vision and ML as engineering methodology carries significant implications for system design. Rule-based AI systems scaled with programmer effort, requiring manual encoding of each new capability. Data-driven ML systems scale through computational and data infrastructure, achieving improved performance by expanding training datasets and computational resources rather than through additional programming effort. This transformation elevated systems engineering to a central role: advancement now depends on building infrastructure capable of collecting massive datasets, training models with billions of parameters, and serving predictions at scale. Machine learning emerged as a practical approach to artificial intelligence through this paradigm shift[^fn-paradigm-shift], transforming theoretical principles about intelligence into functioning systems that form the algorithmic foundation of today's intelligent capabilities.

[^fn-paradigm-shift]: **Paradigm Shift**: A term coined by philosopher Thomas Kuhn in 1962 [@kuhn1962structure] to describe major changes in scientific approach. In AI, the key paradigm shift was moving from symbolic reasoning (encoding human knowledge as rules) to statistical learning (discovering patterns from data). This transformation explains why ML systems engineering emerged as a discipline distinct from traditional software engineering.

[^fn-petabyte-scale]: **Petabyte-Scale Data**: One petabyte equals 1,000 terabytes or roughly 1 million gigabytes—enough to store 13.3 years of HD video or the entire written works of humanity 50 times over. Modern ML systems routinely process petabyte-scale datasets: Meta processes over 4 petabytes of data daily for its recommendation systems, while Google's search index contains hundreds of petabytes of web content. Managing this scale requires distributed storage systems (like HDFS or S3) that shard data across thousands of servers, parallel processing frameworks (like Apache Spark) that coordinate computation across clusters, and sophisticated data engineering pipelines that can validate, transform, and serve data at rates exceeding 100 GB/s. The engineering challenge isn't just storage capacity, but the bandwidth, fault tolerance, and consistency guarantees needed to make petabyte datasets useful for training and inference.

::: {.callout-definition title="Key Definitions"}
***Artificial Intelligence (AI)*** is the field of computer science focused on creating systems that perform tasks requiring human-like _intelligence_, including _learning_, _reasoning_, and _adaptation_.

***Machine Learning (ML)*** is the approach to AI that enables systems to automatically learn _patterns_ and make _decisions_ from _data_ rather than following explicit programmed rules.
:::

The evolution from rule-based AI to data-driven ML represents one of the most significant shifts in computing history. This transformation explains why ML systems engineering has emerged as a discipline: the path to intelligent systems now runs through the engineering challenge of building systems that can effectively learn from data at massive scale.

## Defining ML Systems {#sec-introduction-defining-ml-systems-bf7d}

<div align="center">

# Tiny🔥Torch

### Build Your Own ML Framework From Scratch

[![Version](https://img.shields.io/github/v/tag/harvard-edge/cs249r_book?filter=tinytorch-v*&label=version&color=D4740C&logo=fireship&logoColor=white)](https://github.com/harvard-edge/cs249r_book/releases?q=tinytorch)
[![Status](https://img.shields.io/badge/status-preview-orange?logo=github)](https://github.com/harvard-edge/cs249r_book/discussions/1076)
[![Docs](https://img.shields.io/badge/docs-mlsysbook.ai-blue?logo=readthedocs)](https://mlsysbook.ai/tinytorch)
[![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.8+-3776ab?logo=python&logoColor=white)](https://python.org)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-green.svg)](LICENSE)
[![Harvard](https://img.shields.io/badge/Harvard-CS249r-A51C30?logo=data:image/svg+xml;base64,REDACTED)](https://mlsysbook.ai)

**Most ML courses teach you to *use* frameworks. TinyTorch teaches you to *build* them.**

[The Vision](#why-tinytorch) · [20 Modules](#-20-progressive-modules) · [Share Feedback](https://github.com/harvard-edge/cs249r_book/discussions/1076)

</div>

---

> 🚧 **Preview Release** — TinyTorch is functional but evolving. We're sharing early to shape the direction with community input rather than building in isolation.
>
> 📅 **Classroom Ready**: Summer/Fall 2026 · **Right Now**: [We want your feedback](#-help-shape-tinytorch)

---

## Why TinyTorch?

Everyone wants to be an astronaut 🧑‍🚀. Very few want to be the rocket scientist 🚀.

In machine learning, we see the same pattern. Everyone wants to train models, run inference, deploy AI. Very few want to understand how the frameworks actually work. Even fewer want to build one.

**The world is full of users. We do not have enough builders.**

### The Solution: AI Bricks 🧱

TinyTorch teaches you the **AI bricks**—the stable engineering foundations you can use to build any AI system.

- **Small enough to learn from**: bite-sized code that runs even on a Raspberry Pi
- **Big enough to matter**: showing the real architecture of how frameworks are built

A Harvard University course that transforms you from framework user to systems engineer, giving you the deep understanding needed to optimize, debug, and innovate at the foundation of AI.

---

## What You'll Build

A **complete ML framework** capable of:

🎯 **North Star Achievement**: Train CNNs for image classification
- Real computer vision on standard benchmark datasets
- Built entirely from scratch using only NumPy
- Competitive performance with modern frameworks

**Additional Capabilities**:
- GPT-style language models with attention mechanisms
- Modern optimizers (Adam, SGD) with learning rate scheduling
- Performance profiling, optimization, and competitive benchmarking

**No dependencies on PyTorch or TensorFlow - everything is YOUR code!**

---

## 🛠 Help Shape TinyTorch

We're sharing TinyTorch early because we'd rather shape the direction with community input than build in isolation. Before diving into code, we want to hear from you:

**If you're a student:**
→ What hands-on labs or projects would help you learn ML systems?

**If you teach:**
→ What would make TinyTorch easy to bring into a course?

**If you're a practitioner:**
→ What real-world systems tasks should we simulate?

**For everyone:**
→ What natural extensions belong in this "AI bricks" model?

"""
# Module 01: Tensor Foundation - Building Blocks of ML

Welcome to Module 01! You're about to build the foundational Tensor class that powers all machine learning operations.

## 🔗 Prerequisites & Progress
**You've Built**: Nothing - this is our foundation!
**You'll Build**: A complete Tensor class with arithmetic, matrix operations, and shape manipulation
**You'll Enable**: Foundation for activations, layers, and all future neural network components

**Connection Map**:
```
NumPy Arrays → Tensor → Activations (Module 02)
(raw data)   (ML ops)  (intelligence)
```

## 🎯 Learning Objectives
By the end of this module, you will:
1. Implement a complete Tensor class with fundamental operations
2. Understand tensors as the universal data structure in ML
3. Master broadcasting, matrix multiplication, and shape manipulation
4. Test tensor operations with immediate validation

Let's get started!

## 📦 Where This Code Lives in the Final Package

**Learning Side:** You work in modules/01_tensor/tensor_dev.py
**Building Side:** Code exports to tinytorch.core.tensor

```python
# Final package structure:
# Other modules will import and use this Tensor
```

**Why this matters:**
- **Learning:** Complete tensor system in one focused module for deep understanding
- **Production:** Proper organization like PyTorch's torch.Tensor with all core operations together
- **Consistency:** All tensor operations and data manipulation in core.tensor
- **Integration:** Foundation that every other module will build upon
"""

     1→# SKILL: KNOWLEDGE_HARVESTING_PRIME
     2→
     3→## DOMAIN EXPERTISE
     4→**Agentic Archaeology & Data Recovery**. Specializes in recovering semantic value from "Ghost Files" (files deleted from disk but present in git index) and untracked artifacts before destruction.
     5→
     6→## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS
     7→- **Ghost Harvest**: Using `git ls-files -d --stage` and `git cat-file --batch` to read file content directly from the git object database without checking out the files to disk.
     8→- **Semantic Ingestion**: Classifying files as `Learning` (Insight/Retrospective) or `Journey` (Plan/Thought) and storing them in a Vector Database (SurrealDB).
     9→- **Safe Pruning**: Only deleting (or finalizing deletion of) files AFTER confirmed ingestion.
    10→
    11→## INSTRUCTION
    12→
    13→### 1. The Ghost Protocol (Recovering Deleted Files)
    14→If files are missing from disk (`D` in `git status`) but were tracked:
    15→
    16→```python
    17→import subprocess
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→
    20→def harvest_ghosts(root_dir="."):
    21→    # 1. Stream deleted files from Index
    22→    ls = subprocess.Popen(["git", "ls-files", "-d", "--stage"], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True)
    23→    
    24→    # 2. Pipe to cat-file for content
    25→    cat = subprocess.Popen(["git", "cat-file", "--batch"], stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE)
    26→    
    27→    for line in ls.stdout:
    28→        # line: "100644 <OID> 0\t<PATH>"
    29→        meta, path_str = line.split('\t', 1)
    30→        oid = meta.split()[1]
    31→        
    32→        # Request content
    33→        cat.stdin.write(f"{oid}\n".encode())
    34→        cat.stdin.flush()
    35→        
    36→        # Read Header: "<OID> <TYPE> <SIZE>"
    37→        header = cat.stdout.readline().decode()
    38→        if "missing" in header: continue
    39→        size = int(header.split()[2])
    40→        
    41→        # Read Content
    42→        content = cat.stdout.read(size)
    43→        cat.stdout.read(1) # trailing newline
    44→        
    45→        # ... Ingest content ...
    46→```
    47→
    48→### 2. The Living Harvest (Untracked Files)
    49→For massive untracked sets, avoid `git ls-files` if >1M files. Use `os.scandir`:
    50→```python
```

    63→  - Ingestion script: Follow `scripts/ingest_research.py` pattern (SurrealClient + KnowledgeMapper)
    64→  - Vault concepts: Follow existing format in `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/concepts/` (markdown with frontmatter)
    65→  - PRIME skills: Follow existing `src/cohezion/skills/*_PRIME.md` format (DOMAIN EXPERTISE, KEY TEXTS, INSTRUCTION sections)
    66→  - Python modules: Follow `src/cohezion/` package conventions (type hints, `__init__.py`, async where needed)
    67→
    68→- **Conventions:**
    69→  - All vault concept files use kebab-case: `ml-systems-engineering.md`
    70→  - PRIME skills use UPPER_SNAKE: `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME.md`
    71→  - Python modules use snake_case
    72→  - Every `src/` directory must have `__init__.py`
    73→
    74→- **Key files:**
    75→  - `scripts/ingest_research.py` - Existing ingestion pattern
    76→  - `src/cohezion/skills/RESEARCH_PATTERNS_PRIME.md` - Example PRIME skill
    77→  - `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/concepts/` - Where vault concepts live
    78→  - `tests/conftest.py` - Test isolation patterns
    79→
    80→- **Gotchas:**
    81→  - TinyTorch modules are Jupytext-formatted `.py` files (percent format with `# %%` cells) - need to extract pure Python
    82→  - TinyTorch depends only on NumPy - keep this constraint, don't add PyTorch deps
    83→  - Chapter concept files are YAML, glossary files are JSON - different parsers needed
    84→  - Some chapters may have missing or empty concept/glossary files
    85→  - The repo's default branch is `dev`, not `main`
    86→
    87→- **Domain context:**
    88→  - CS249R is Harvard's ML Systems course (Prof. Vijay Janapa Reddi)
    89→  - Book covers 6 parts: Systems Foundations, Design Principles, Performance Engineering, Robust Deployment, Trustworthy Systems, Frontiers
    90→  - TinyTorch is a from-scratch ML framework teaching implementation of tensor ops, autograd, transformers, quantization etc. using only NumPy
    91→  - Book site: https://mlsysbook.ai/
    92→

   319→
   320→**Objective:** Adapt TinyTorch's core modules (tensor, activations, layers, losses, dataloader, autograd, optimizers, training, convolutions) into Cohezion's codebase as `src/cohezion/tinytorch/`.

   322→**Dependencies:** Task 1

   327→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/activations.py` (from module 02)
   328→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/layers.py` (from module 03)
   329→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/losses.py` (from module 04)
   330→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/dataloader.py` (from module 05)

   333→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/training.py` (from module 08)
   334→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/convolutions.py` (from module 09)
   335→- Create: `tests/tinytorch/__init__.py`
   336→- Create: `tests/tinytorch/test_core.py`

   338→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   339→- TinyTorch source files are Jupytext percent-format `.py` files. Extract only the code cells (marked with `# %%`), skip markdown cells (`# %% [markdown]`)
   340→- TinyTorch depends only on NumPy - maintain this constraint
   341→- Each source file contains a complete module. Extract the exported functions/classes
   342→- The `tinytorch/` package in the original repo has `core/` and `perf/` subpackages with `__init__.py` only - the actual code is in `src/01_tensor/01_tensor.py` etc.
   343→- Adapt imports: TinyTorch internal imports like `from tinytorch.core.tensor import Tensor` become `from cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor`
   344→- Add type hints where TinyTorch doesn't have them (it targets Python 3.8+, we target 3.13+)
   345→- Keep files under 300 lines - split if needed
   346→

   430→
   431→## Testing Strategy
   432→
   433→- **Unit tests:** Test each ingestion function (YAML parsing, JSON parsing, vault note generation) in isolation
   434→- **Integration tests:** Test the full pipeline from repo access through vault write
   435→- **TinyTorch tests:** Test core operations (tensor math, autograd, training loop) and advanced features (attention, quantization)
   436→- **Regression tests:** Run full Cohezion test suite to ensure no existing tests break
   437→- **Manual verification:** Spot-check vault notes for content quality, query SurrealDB for cross-links
   438→
   439→## Risks and Mitigations
   440→
   441→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
   442→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
   443→| TinyTorch modules are too large for 300-line limit | High | Low | Split into submodules (e.g., `attention/multi_head.py`, `attention/scaled_dot.py`) |
   444→| Some chapter concept files are missing or malformed | Medium | Low | Graceful error handling in ingestion scripts: log warning, skip file, continue |
   445→| TinyTorch code depends on features not in NumPy 2.x | Low | Medium | Pin NumPy version or add compatibility shims during adaptation |
   446→| SurrealDB not running during indexing task | Medium | Low | Task 10 can be deferred; vault files work independently of SurrealDB |
   447→| Repository structure changes upstream | Low | Low | Pin to specific commit hash when cloning |
   448→| Full test suite fails due to new imports | Medium | Medium | Keep `tinytorch/` fully isolated with no imports from other Cohezion modules |
   449→
   450→## Open Questions
   451→
   452→- Should we also extract quiz data from chapters for use in Cohezion's evaluation/testing framework?

**Goal:** Ingest the full Harvard CS249R "Machine Learning Systems" textbook into Cohezion's knowledge ecosystem.

**10 Tasks across 3 layers:**

**Knowledge Layer (Tasks 1-4):**
1. Clone repo, create data access module, validate actual counts
2. Build chapter concept ingestion script (YAML parsing -> vault notes)
3. Ingest 656 glossary terms into vault + SurrealDB-ready JSON
4. Execute ingestion, create master index, cross-link with existing concepts

**Skills Layer (Tasks 5-7):**

6. Performance & Deployment skills: `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME`, `MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME`, `MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME`
7. Trustworthy & Frontiers skills: `RESPONSIBLE_AI_PRIME`, `EDGE_INTELLIGENCE_PRIME`

**Code Layer (Tasks 8-9):**
8. TinyTorch core modules (tensor, autograd, layers, optimizers, training, convolutions) - standalone, NumPy-only
9. TinyTorch advanced modules (attention, transformers, quantization, profiling, benchmarking)

**Validation (Task 10):**
10. End-to-end validation, SurrealDB indexing, full test suite regression check

   323→### Task 8: Integrate TinyTorch Core Modules (01-09)
   324→
   325→**Objective:** Adapt TinyTorch's core modules (tensor, activations, layers, losses, dataloader, autograd, optimizers, training, convolutions) into Cohezion's codebase as `src/cohezion/tinytorch/`.
   326→
   327→**Dependencies:** Task 1
   328→
   329→**Files:**
   330→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/__init__.py`
   331→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/tensor.py` (from module 01)
   332→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/activations.py` (from module 02)
   333→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/layers.py` (from module 03)
   334→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/losses.py` (from module 04)
   335→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/dataloader.py` (from module 05)
   336→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/autograd.py` (from module 06)
   337→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/optimizers.py` (from module 07)
   338→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/training.py` (from module 08)
   339→- Create: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/convolutions.py` (from module 09)
   340→- Create: `tests/tinytorch/__init__.py`
   341→- Create: `tests/tinytorch/test_core.py`
   342→
   343→**Key Decisions / Notes:**
   344→- **ISOLATION**: TinyTorch is a STANDALONE educational subpackage. NO imports from `cohezion.compound`, `cohezion.swarm`, or any other Cohezion module. Only numpy and stdlib. This preserves it as a self-contained learning resource and prevents test coupling
   345→- TinyTorch source files are Jupytext percent-format `.py` files. Extract code cells using regex `^# %%(?!\[markdown\])`, skip markdown cells. Test extraction on modules 01 and 06 first, verify output is valid Python before batch-processing
   346→- TinyTorch depends only on NumPy - maintain this constraint
   347→- Each source file contains a complete module. Extract the exported functions/classes
   348→- The `tinytorch/` package in the original repo has `core/` and `perf/` subpackages with `__init__.py` only - the actual code is in `src/01_tensor/01_tensor.py` etc.
   349→- Adapt imports: TinyTorch internal imports like `from tinytorch.core.tensor import Tensor` become `from cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor`
   350→- Add type hints where TinyTorch doesn't have them (it targets Python 3.8+, we target 3.13+)
   351→- Keep files under 300 lines - split if needed
   352→
   353→**Definition of Done:**
   354→- [ ] All 9 core modules adapted and importable from `cohezion.tinytorch`
   355→- [ ] Internal cross-imports work (e.g., `layers.py` imports from `tensor.py`)
   356→- [ ] Tests verify basic tensor operations, autograd backward pass, and a simple training loop
   357→- [ ] No diagnostics errors from `ruff check` on new files
   358→- [ ] All files under 300 lines
   359→

   362→- `uv run python -c "from cohezion.tinytorch import Tensor; t = Tensor([1,2,3]); print(t)"` - basic import works

   436→
   437→## Testing Strategy
   438→
   439→- **Unit tests:** Test each ingestion function (YAML parsing, JSON parsing, vault note generation) in isolation
   440→- **Integration tests:** Test the full pipeline from repo access through vault write
   441→- **TinyTorch tests:** Test core operations (tensor math, autograd, training loop) and advanced features (attention, quantization)
   442→- **Regression tests:** Run full Cohezion test suite to ensure no existing tests break
   443→- **Manual verification:** Spot-check vault notes for content quality, query SurrealDB for cross-links
   444→
   445→## Risks and Mitigations
   446→
   447→| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
   448→|------|-----------|--------|------------|
   449→| TinyTorch modules are too large for 300-line limit | High | Low | Split into submodules (e.g., `attention/multi_head.py`, `attention/scaled_dot.py`) |
   450→| Some chapter concept files are missing or malformed | Medium | Low | Graceful error handling in ingestion scripts: log warning, skip file, continue |
   451→| TinyTorch code depends on features not in NumPy 2.x | Low | Medium | Pin NumPy version or add compatibility shims during adaptation |
   452→| SurrealDB not running during indexing task | Medium | Low | Task 10 can be deferred; vault files work independently of SurrealDB |
   453→| Repository structure changes upstream | Low | Low | Pin to specific commit hash when cloning |
   454→| Full test suite fails due to new imports | Medium | Medium | Keep `tinytorch/` fully isolated with no imports from other Cohezion modules |

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/tests/cs249r/test_repo_access.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for CS249R repository access module.\"\"\"\n\nimport pytest\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scripts.cs249r.repo_access import CS249RRepo\n\n\ndef test_repo_initialization():\n    \"\"\"Test CS249RRepo initializes with correct path.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    assert repo.repo_path.exists()\n    assert repo.repo_path.name == \"cs249r_book\"\n\n\ndef test_list_core_chapters():\n    \"\"\"Test listing core chapters.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    core_chapters = repo.get_core_chapters()\n\n    assert len(core_chapters) == 21\n    assert all(isinstance(ch, dict) for ch in core_chapters)\n    assert all(\"name\" in ch and \"path\" in ch for ch in core_chapters)\n\n\ndef test_list_advanced_chapters():\n    \"\"\"Test listing advanced chapters.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    advanced_chapters = repo.get_advanced_chapters()\n\n    assert len(advanced_chapters) == 9\n    assert all(isinstance(ch, dict) for ch in advanced_chapters)\n\n\ndef test_all_chapters_property():\n    \"\"\"Test chapters property returns all chapters.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    all_chapters = repo.chapters\n\n    assert len(all_chapters) == 30  # 21 core + 9 advanced\n    assert all(\"type\" in ch for ch in all_chapters)  # Should have type: core or advanced\n\n\ndef test_load_chapter_concepts():\n    \"\"\"Test loading concept YAML for a chapter.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n\n    # Load introduction chapter concepts\n    concepts = repo.load_chapter_concepts(\"introduction\", chapter_type=\"core\")\n\n    assert concepts is not None\n    assert \"concept_map\" in concepts or \"primary_concepts\" in concepts.get(\"concept_map\", {})\n\n\ndef test_load_chapter_glossary():\n    \"\"\"Test loading glossary JSON for a chapter.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n\n    # Load introduction chapter glossary\n    glossary = repo.load_chapter_glossary(\"introduction\", chapter_type=\"core\")\n\n    # Glossary might be None or empty for some chapters - that's okay\n    assert glossary is None or isinstance(glossary, (list, dict))\n\n\ndef test_global_glossary_count():\n    \"\"\"Test global glossary has expected term count.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    glossary = repo.load_global_glossary()\n\n    assert glossary is not None\n    # Glossary structure has metadata and terms\n    terms = glossary.get(\"terms\", glossary) if isinstance(glossary, dict) else glossary\n    assert len(terms) >= 656  # At least 656 terms\n\n\ndef test_tinytorch_modules_count():\n    \"\"\"Test TinyTorch modules enumeration.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    modules = repo.get_tinytorch_modules()\n\n    assert len(modules) == 20\n    assert all(\"number\" in m and \"path\" in m for m in modules)\n    # Modules should be numbered 01-20\n    numbers = sorted([m[\"number\"] for m in modules])\n    assert numbers[0] == 1\n    assert numbers[-1] == 20\n\n\ndef test_glossary_term_count_property():\n    \"\"\"Test glossary_term_count property.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    count = repo.glossary_term_count\n\n    assert isinstance(count, int)\n    assert count == 656\n\n\ndef test_tinytorch_modules_property():\n    \"\"\"Test tinytorch_modules property.\"\"\"\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    modules = repo.tinytorch_modules\n\n    assert len(modules) == 20\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/scripts/cs249r/repo_access.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CS249R book repository access utilities.\n\nProvides structured access to the CS249R ML Systems book repository:\n- Chapter concepts (YAML files)\n- Glossary terms (JSON files)\n- TinyTorch modules (Jupytext .py files)\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport yaml\n\n\nclass CS249RRepo:\n    \"\"\"Interface to the CS249R book repository.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, repo_path: str | Path | None = None):\n        \"\"\"Initialize with path to cloned repository.\n\n        Args:\n            repo_path: Path to cs249r_book directory. If None, searches in standard location.\n        \"\"\"\n        if repo_path is None:\n            # Default location: sibling to cohezion\n            default_path = Path.home() / \"dev\" / \"cs249r_book\"\n            if not default_path.exists():\n                raise FileNotFoundError(\n                    f\"CS249R repo not found at {default_path}. \"\n                    \"Clone it first or pass explicit repo_path.\"\n                )\n            repo_path = default_path\n\n        self.repo_path = Path(repo_path)\n        if not self.repo_path.exists():\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Repository not found at {self.repo_path}\")\n\n        self._book_path = self.repo_path / \"book\" / \"quarto\" / \"contents\"\n        self._tinytorch_path = self.repo_path / \"tinytorch\" / \"src\"\n\n    def get_core_chapters(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"Get list of core chapter directories.\"\"\"\n        core_path = self._book_path / \"core\"\n        if not core_path.exists():\n            return []\n\n        chapters = []\n        for item in sorted(core_path.iterdir()):\n            if item.is_dir() and item.name not in (\"data\", \"frontmatter\", \"backmatter\", \"parts\"):\n                chapters.append({\n                    \"name\": item.name,\n                    \"path\": item,\n                    \"type\": \"core\"\n                })\n\n        return chapters\n\n    def get_advanced_chapters(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"Get list of advanced chapter directories.\"\"\"\n        advanced_path = self._book_path / \"advanced\"\n        if not advanced_path.exists():\n            return []\n\n        chapters = []\n        for item in sorted(advanced_path.iterdir()):\n            if item.is_dir() and item.name != \"README\":\n                chapters.append({\n                    \"name\": item.name,\n                    \"path\": item,\n                    \"type\": \"advanced\"\n                })\n\n        return chapters\n\n    @property\n    def chapters(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"Get all chapters (core + advanced).\"\"\"\n        return self.get_core_chapters() + self.get_advanced_chapters()\n\n    def load_chapter_concepts(self, chapter_name: str, chapter_type: str = \"core\") -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n        \"\"\"Load concept map YAML for a chapter.\n\n        Args:\n            chapter_name: Name of the chapter directory\n            chapter_type: \"core\" or \"advanced\"\n\n        Returns:\n            Parsed YAML dict or None if file doesn't exist\n        \"\"\"\n        base_path = self._book_path / chapter_type / chapter_name\n        concepts_file = base_path / f\"{chapter_name}_concepts.yml\"\n\n        if not concepts_file.exists():\n            return None\n\n        with concepts_file.open() as f:\n            return yaml.safe_load(f)\n\n    def load_chapter_glossary(self, chapter_name: str, chapter_type: str = \"core\") -> list[dict] | None:\n        \"\"\"Load glossary JSON for a chapter.\n\n        Args:\n            chapter_name: Name of the chapter directory\n            chapter_type: \"core\" or \"advanced\"\n\n        Returns:\n            Parsed JSON list/dict or None if file doesn't exist\n        \"\"\"\n        base_path = self._book_path / chapter_type / chapter_name\n        glossary_file = base_path / f\"{chapter_name}_glossary.json\"\n\n        if not glossary_file.exists():\n            return None\n\n        with glossary_file.open() as f:\n            return json.load(f)\n\n    def load_global_glossary(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Load the global glossary JSON.\n\n        Returns:\n            Parsed JSON with structure: {\"metadata\": {...}, \"terms\": [...]}\n        \"\"\"\n        glossary_file = self._book_path / \"data\" / \"global_glossary.json\"\n\n        if not glossary_file.exists():\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Global glossary not found at {glossary_file}\")\n\n        with glossary_file.open() as f:\n            return json.load(f)\n\n    @property\n    def glossary_term_count(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Get count of terms in global glossary.\"\"\"\n        glossary = self.load_global_glossary()\n        # Glossary structure: {\"metadata\": {...}, \"terms\": [...]}\n        terms = glossary.get(\"terms\", glossary) if isinstance(glossary, dict) else glossary\n        return len(terms)\n\n    def get_tinytorch_modules(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"Get list of TinyTorch module directories.\n\n        Returns:\n            List of dicts with keys: number, name, path\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self._tinytorch_path.exists():\n            return []\n\n        modules = []\n        for item in sorted(self._tinytorch_path.iterdir()):\n            if item.is_dir() and re.match(r\"^\\d{2}_\", item.name):\n                # Extract module number from name like \"01_tensor\"\n                match = re.match(r\"^(\\d{2})_(.+)$\", item.name)\n                if match:\n                    modules.append({\n                        \"number\": int(match.group(1)),\n                        \"name\": match.group(2),\n                        \"path\": item,\n                        \"main_file\": item / f\"{item.name}.py\"\n                    })\n\n        return sorted(modules, key=lambda m: m[\"number\"])\n\n    @property\n    def tinytorch_modules(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"Get all TinyTorch modules.\"\"\"\n        return self.get_tinytorch_modules()\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for CS249R chapter ingestion script.\"\"\"\n\nimport pytest\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scripts.cs249r.ingest_chapters import (\n    parse_concept_yaml,\n    create_vault_note,\n    ingest_all_chapters,\n)\n\n\ndef test_parse_concept_yaml_with_valid_file():\n    \"\"\"Test parsing a valid concept YAML file.\"\"\"\n    # This will test against actual file from cloned repo\n    from scripts.cs249r.repo_access import CS249RRepo\n\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    concepts = repo.load_chapter_concepts(\"introduction\", chapter_type=\"core\")\n\n    # Parse the concepts\n    parsed = parse_concept_yaml(concepts, chapter_name=\"introduction\")\n\n    assert parsed is not None\n    assert \"primary_concepts\" in parsed or \"concept_map\" in parsed\n    assert \"domain\" in parsed  # Should assign a domain tag\n\n\ndef test_parse_concept_yaml_with_missing_file():\n    \"\"\"Test handling of missing concept file.\"\"\"\n    result = parse_concept_yaml(None, chapter_name=\"missing\")\n\n    # Should return None gracefully\n    assert result is None\n\n\ndef test_create_vault_note_structure():\n    \"\"\"Test vault note creation with correct structure.\"\"\"\n    concept_data = {\n        \"chapter_name\": \"introduction\",\n        \"chapter_type\": \"core\",\n        \"domain\": \"foundations\",\n        \"primary_concepts\": [\"ML Systems\", \"AI Triangle\"],\n        \"secondary_concepts\": [\"Production Systems\"],\n        \"technical_terms\": [\"Perceptron\", \"ELIZA\"],\n        \"methodologies\": [\"System Version Management\"],\n        \"applications\": [\"Medical Image Analysis\"],\n    }\n\n    note_content = create_vault_note(concept_data)\n\n    # Check frontmatter tags\n    assert \"tags: [concept, ml-systems, cs249r, foundations]\" in note_content\n    assert \"source: cs249r/core/introduction\" in note_content\n\n    # Check sections\n    assert \"## Primary Concepts\" in note_content\n    assert \"## Technical Terms\" in note_content\n    assert \"ML Systems\" in note_content\n    assert \"Perceptron\" in note_content\n\n\ndef test_create_vault_note_with_minimal_data():\n    \"\"\"Test vault note creation with minimal concept data.\"\"\"\n    concept_data = {\n        \"chapter_name\": \"test\",\n        \"chapter_type\": \"core\",\n        \"domain\": \"foundations\",\n        \"primary_concepts\": [\"Concept A\"],\n    }\n\n    note_content = create_vault_note(concept_data)\n\n    # Should not crash with minimal data\n    assert \"Concept A\" in note_content\n    assert \"tags: [concept, ml-systems, cs249r, foundations]\" in note_content\n\n\ndef test_ingest_all_chapters_dry_run(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Test dry-run mode doesn't write files.\"\"\"\n    # Dry run should return stats without writing\n    stats = ingest_all_chapters(output_dir=tmp_path, dry_run=True)\n\n    assert \"core_chapters\" in stats\n    assert \"advanced_chapters\" in stats\n    assert \"total\" in stats\n\n    # No files should be written in dry-run mode\n    files = list(tmp_path.glob(\"*.md\"))\n    assert len(files) == 0\n\n\ndef test_ingest_all_chapters_creates_files(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Test actual file creation.\"\"\"\n    stats = ingest_all_chapters(output_dir=tmp_path, dry_run=False)\n\n    # Should create files\n    files = list(tmp_path.glob(\"*.md\"))\n    assert len(files) > 0\n    assert stats[\"total\"] == len(files)\n\n    # Check one file has correct structure\n    sample_file = files[0]\n    content = sample_file.read_text()\n\n    assert \"---\" in content  # YAML frontmatter delimiters\n    assert \"tags:\" in content\n    assert \"cs249r\" in content\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/scripts/cs249r/ingest_chapters.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CS249R chapter concept ingestion.\n\nParses concept YAML files from the book and creates vault-compatible markdown notes.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport logging\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom scripts.cs249r.repo_access import CS249RRepo\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n# Domain mapping based on chapter names\nCHAPTER_DOMAINS = {\n    # Systems Foundations\n    \"introduction\": \"foundations\",\n    \"ml_systems\": \"foundations\",\n    \"dl_primer\": \"foundations\",\n    \"dnn_architectures\": \"architectures\",\n    # Design Principles\n    \"workflow\": \"foundations\",\n    \"data_engineering\": \"data-eng\",\n    \"frameworks\": \"foundations\",\n    \"training\": \"foundations\",\n    # Performance Engineering\n    \"efficient_ai\": \"performance\",\n    \"optimizations\": \"performance\",\n    \"hw_acceleration\": \"performance\",\n    \"benchmarking\": \"performance\",\n    # Robust Deployment\n    \"ops\": \"deployment\",\n    \"ondevice_learning\": \"deployment\",\n    \"privacy_security\": \"deployment\",\n    \"robust_ai\": \"trustworthy\",\n    # Trustworthy Systems\n    \"responsible_ai\": \"trustworthy\",\n    \"sustainable_ai\": \"trustworthy\",\n    \"ai_for_good\": \"trustworthy\",\n    # Frontiers\n    \"frontiers\": \"frontiers\",\n    \"conclusion\": \"frontiers\",\n    # Advanced chapters\n    \"edge_intelligence\": \"edge\",\n    \"distributed_training\": \"edge\",\n    \"fault_tolerance\": \"edge\",\n    \"inference\": \"edge\",\n    \"communication\": \"edge\",\n    \"storage\": \"edge\",\n    \"infrastructure\": \"edge\",\n}\n\n\ndef parse_concept_yaml(concepts: dict[str, Any] | None, chapter_name: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Parse concept YAML and extract structured data.\n\n    Args:\n        concepts: Parsed YAML dict from chapter concepts file\n        chapter_name: Name of the chapter (for domain mapping)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict with parsed concept data or None if concepts is None\n    \"\"\"\n    if concepts is None:\n        return None\n\n    # Extract concept_map if present\n    concept_map = concepts.get(\"concept_map\", concepts)\n\n    # Determine domain from chapter name\n    domain = CHAPTER_DOMAINS.get(chapter_name, \"foundations\")\n\n    return {\n        \"chapter_name\": chapter_name,\n        \"domain\": domain,\n        \"primary_concepts\": concept_map.get(\"primary_concepts\", []),\n        \"secondary_concepts\": concept_map.get(\"secondary_concepts\", []),\n        \"technical_terms\": concept_map.get(\"technical_terms\", []),\n        \"methodologies\": concept_map.get(\"methodologies\", []),\n        \"applications\": concept_map.get(\"applications\", []),\n    }\n\n\ndef create_vault_note(concept_data: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Create vault-formatted markdown note from concept data.\n\n    Args:\n        concept_data: Parsed concept data dict\n\n    Returns:\n        Formatted markdown content with YAML frontmatter\n    \"\"\"\n    chapter_name = concept_data[\"chapter_name\"]\n    chapter_type = concept_data.get(\"chapter_type\", \"core\")\n    domain = concept_data[\"domain\"]\n    date = datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\n\n    # Build YAML frontmatter\n    frontmatter = f\"\"\"---\ntags: [concept, ml-systems, cs249r, {domain}]\nsource: cs249r/{chapter_type}/{chapter_name}\ndate: {date}\n---\n\n\"\"\"\n\n    # Build content sections\n    content = f\"# {chapter_name.replace('_', ' ').title()}\\n\\n\"\n    content += f\"**Source:** CS249R ML Systems Book - {chapter_type.title()} Chapter\\n\\n\"\n\n    # Primary concepts\n    if concept_data.get(\"primary_concepts\"):\n        content += \"## Primary Concepts\\n\\n\"\n        for concept in concept_data[\"primary_concepts\"]:\n            content += f\"- {concept}\\n\"\n        content += \"\\n\"\n\n    # Secondary concepts\n    if concept_data.get(\"secondary_concepts\"):\n        content += \"## Secondary Concepts\\n\\n\"\n        for concept in concept_data[\"secondary_concepts\"]:\n            content += f\"- {concept}\\n\"\n        content += \"\\n\"\n\n    # Technical terms\n    if concept_data.get(\"technical_terms\"):\n        content += \"## Technical Terms\\n\\n\"\n        for term in concept_data[\"technical_terms\"]:\n            content += f\"- {term}\\n\"\n        content += \"\\n\"\n\n    # Methodologies\n    if concept_data.get(\"methodologies\"):\n        content += \"## Methodologies\\n\\n\"\n        for method in concept_data[\"methodologies\"]:\n            content += f\"- {method}\\n\"\n        content += \"\\n\"\n\n    # Applications\n    if concept_data.get(\"applications\"):\n        content += \"## Applications\\n\\n\"\n        for app in concept_data[\"applications\"]:\n            content += f\"- {app}\\n\"\n        content += \"\\n\"\n\n    return frontmatter + content\n\n\ndef ingest_all_chapters(output_dir: Path | str | None = None, dry_run: bool = False) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Ingest all chapter concepts and create vault notes.\n\n    Args:\n        output_dir: Directory to write vault notes. Defaults to standard vault path.\n        dry_run: If True, don't write files, just return stats\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict with ingestion statistics\n    \"\"\"\n    if output_dir is None:\n        output_dir = Path.home() / \"vaults\" / \"cohezion-vault\" / \"concepts\" / \"cs249r\"\n    else:\n        output_dir = Path(output_dir)\n\n    if not dry_run:\n        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    stats = {\n        \"core_chapters\": 0,\n        \"advanced_chapters\": 0,\n        \"skipped\": 0,\n        \"total\": 0,\n    }\n\n    # Process all chapters\n    for chapter in repo.chapters:\n        chapter_name = chapter[\"name\"]\n        chapter_type = chapter[\"type\"]\n\n        # Load concepts\n        concepts = repo.load_chapter_concepts(chapter_name, chapter_type=chapter_type)\n\n        if concepts is None:\n            logger.warning(f\"No concepts file for {chapter_type}/{chapter_name}, skipping\")\n            stats[\"skipped\"] += 1\n            continue\n\n        # Parse concepts\n        try:\n            concept_data = parse_concept_yaml(concepts, chapter_name)\n            if concept_data is None:\n                stats[\"skipped\"] += 1\n                continue\n\n            concept_data[\"chapter_type\"] = chapter_type\n\n            # Create vault note\n            note_content = create_vault_note(concept_data)\n\n            # Write to file (if not dry run)\n            if not dry_run:\n                filename = f\"{chapter_name}.md\"\n                filepath = output_dir / filename\n                filepath.write_text(note_content)\n                logger.info(f\"Created vault note: {filepath}\")\n\n            # Update stats\n            if chapter_type == \"core\":\n                stats[\"core_chapters\"] += 1\n            else:\n                stats[\"advanced_chapters\"] += 1\n            stats[\"total\"] += 1\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.error(f\"Error processing {chapter_type}/{chapter_name}: {e}\")\n            stats[\"skipped\"] += 1\n\n    return stats\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    import sys\n\n    # Simple CLI for dry-run testing\n    dry_run = \"--dry-run\" in sys.argv\n\n    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)\n    stats = ingest_all_chapters(dry_run=dry_run)\n\n    print(f\"\\nIngestion {'DRY RUN ' if dry_run else ''}complete:\")\n    print(f\"  Core chapters: {stats['core_chapters']}\")\n    print(f\"  Advanced chapters: {stats['advanced_chapters']}\")\n    print(f\"  Skipped: {stats['skipped']}\")\n    print(f\"  Total: {stats['total']}\")\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"Tests for CS249R glossary ingestion.\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport pytest\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom scripts.cs249r.ingest_glossary import (\n    parse_glossary_terms,\n    create_glossary_vault_note,\n    create_surreal_json,\n    ingest_glossary,\n)\n\n\ndef test_parse_glossary_terms_from_repo():\n    \"\"\"Test parsing glossary terms from actual repository.\"\"\"\n    from scripts.cs249r.repo_access import CS249RRepo\n\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    glossary_data = repo.load_global_glossary()\n\n    terms = parse_glossary_terms(glossary_data)\n\n    assert isinstance(terms, list)\n    assert len(terms) >= 656  # Should have at least 656 terms\n    assert all(\"term\" in t for t in terms)  # All should have term field\n\n\ndef test_create_glossary_vault_note():\n    \"\"\"Test vault note generation from glossary terms.\"\"\"\n    sample_terms = [\n        {\"term\": \"Machine Learning\", \"definition\": \"A method of data analysis\"},\n        {\"term\": \"Neural Network\", \"definition\": \"A computational model\"},\n    ]\n\n    note_content = create_glossary_vault_note(sample_terms)\n\n    # Check structure\n    assert \"---\" in note_content  # YAML frontmatter\n    assert \"tags: [glossary, ml-systems, cs249r]\" in note_content\n    assert \"Machine Learning\" in note_content\n    assert \"Neural Network\" in note_content\n    assert \"## M\" in note_content or \"###\" in note_content  # Alphabetical grouping\n\n\ndef test_create_surreal_json():\n    \"\"\"Test SurrealDB JSON format creation.\"\"\"\n    sample_terms = [\n        {\"term\": \"API\", \"definition\": \"Application Programming Interface\"},\n        {\"term\": \"ML\", \"definition\": \"Machine Learning\"},\n    ]\n\n    surreal_data = create_surreal_json(sample_terms)\n\n    assert isinstance(surreal_data, list)\n    assert len(surreal_data) == 2\n    assert all(\"id\" in t for t in surreal_data)\n    assert all(\"term\" in t for t in surreal_data)\n    assert all(\"definition\" in t for t in surreal_data)\n    # Check ID format: glossary:term-slug\n    assert surreal_data[0][\"id\"].startswith(\"glossary:\")\n\n\ndef test_ingest_glossary_dry_run(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Test dry-run mode doesn't write files.\"\"\"\n    stats = ingest_glossary(output_dir=tmp_path, dry_run=True)\n\n    assert \"term_count\" in stats\n    assert stats[\"term_count\"] >= 656\n\n    # No files should be written\n    files = list(tmp_path.glob(\"*.md\"))\n    assert len(files) == 0\n\n\ndef test_ingest_glossary_creates_files(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Test actual file creation.\"\"\"\n    stats = ingest_glossary(output_dir=tmp_path, dry_run=False)\n\n    # Should create glossary markdown\n    glossary_file = tmp_path / \"ml-systems-glossary.md\"\n    assert glossary_file.exists()\n\n    content = glossary_file.read_text()\n    assert \"tags: [glossary, ml-systems, cs249r]\" in content\n    assert len(content) > 1000  # Should have substantial content\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/scripts/cs249r/ingest_glossary.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"CS249R glossary ingestion.\n\nParses the global glossary and creates vault note + SurrealDB JSON.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport logging\nimport re\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom scripts.cs249r.repo_access import CS249RRepo\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\ndef parse_glossary_terms(glossary_data: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Parse glossary data and extract terms.\n\n    Args:\n        glossary_data: Parsed global glossary JSON\n\n    Returns:\n        List of term dicts with term, definition, etc.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Glossary structure: {\"metadata\": {...}, \"terms\": [...]}\n    if isinstance(glossary_data, dict):\n        terms = glossary_data.get(\"terms\", [])\n    else:\n        terms = glossary_data\n\n    return terms\n\n\ndef create_glossary_vault_note(terms: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Create vault-formatted glossary markdown.\n\n    Args:\n        terms: List of glossary term dicts\n\n    Returns:\n        Formatted markdown with frontmatter\n    \"\"\"\n    date = datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\n\n    frontmatter = f\"\"\"---\ntags: [glossary, ml-systems, cs249r]\nsource: cs249r/global_glossary\ndate: {date}\nterm_count: {len(terms)}\n---\n\n\"\"\"\n\n    content = \"# ML Systems Glossary\\n\\n\"\n    content += f\"**Source:** CS249R ML Systems Book - Global Glossary\\n\"\n    content += f\"**Terms:** {len(terms)}\\n\\n\"\n\n    # Group terms alphabetically\n    terms_by_letter: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}\n    for term in terms:\n        term_name = term.get(\"term\", \"Unknown\")\n        first_letter = term_name[0].upper() if term_name else \"?\"\n\n        if first_letter not in terms_by_letter:\n            terms_by_letter[first_letter] = []\n\n        terms_by_letter[first_letter].append(term)\n\n    # Write grouped terms\n    for letter in sorted(terms_by_letter.keys()):\n        content += f\"## {letter}\\n\\n\"\n\n        for term_dict in sorted(terms_by_letter[letter], key=lambda t: t.get(\"term\", \"\")):\n            term_name = term_dict.get(\"term\", \"\")\n            definition = term_dict.get(\"definition\", \"No definition available\")\n\n            content += f\"**{term_name}**: {definition}\\n\\n\"\n\n    return frontmatter + content\n\n\ndef create_surreal_json(terms: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Create SurrealDB-compatible JSON from glossary terms.\n\n    Args:\n        terms: List of glossary term dicts\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SurrealDB-ready term records\n    \"\"\"\n    surreal_records = []\n\n    for term_dict in terms:\n        term_name = term_dict.get(\"term\", \"\")\n        # Create slug for ID\n        slug = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]+\", \"-\", term_name.lower()).strip(\"-\")\n\n        record = {\n            \"id\": f\"glossary:{slug}\",\n            \"term\": term_name,\n            \"definition\": term_dict.get(\"definition\", \"\"),\n            \"tags\": [\"glossary\", \"cs249r\", \"ml-systems\"],\n        }\n\n        # Add optional fields if present\n        if \"chapter\" in term_dict:\n            record[\"chapter\"] = term_dict[\"chapter\"]\n\n        surreal_records.append(record)\n\n    return surreal_records\n\n\ndef ingest_glossary(output_dir: Path | str | None = None, dry_run: bool = False) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Ingest glossary and create vault note + SurrealDB JSON.\n\n    Args:\n        output_dir: Directory to write files. Defaults to vault concepts/cs249r/\n        dry_run: If True, don't write files, just return stats\n\n    Returns:\n        Dict with ingestion statistics\n    \"\"\"\n    if output_dir is None:\n        output_dir = Path.home() / \"vaults\" / \"cohezion-vault\" / \"concepts\" / \"cs249r\"\n    else:\n        output_dir = Path(output_dir)\n\n    if not dry_run:\n        output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Load glossary from repo\n    repo = CS249RRepo()\n    glossary_data = repo.load_global_glossary()\n\n    # Parse terms\n    terms = parse_glossary_terms(glossary_data)\n\n    # Create vault note\n    vault_note = create_glossary_vault_note(terms)\n\n    # Create SurrealDB JSON\n    surreal_data = create_surreal_json(terms)\n\n    # Write files\n    if not dry_run:\n        # Write vault note\n        vault_file = output_dir / \"ml-systems-glossary.md\"\n        vault_file.write_text(vault_note)\n        logger.info(f\"Created glossary vault note: {vault_file}\")\n\n        # Write SurrealDB JSON (in data/ directory)\n        data_dir = Path(\"data\")\n        data_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n        surreal_file = data_dir / \"cs249r_glossary_surreal.json\"\n        surreal_file.write_text(json.dumps(surreal_data, indent=2))\n        logger.info(f\"Created SurrealDB glossary JSON: {surreal_file}\")\n\n    stats = {\n        \"term_count\": len(terms),\n        \"vault_file\": \"ml-systems-glossary.md\",\n        \"surreal_file\": \"data/cs249r_glossary_surreal.json\",\n    }\n\n    return stats\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    import sys\n\n    dry_run = \"--dry-run\" in sys.argv\n\n    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)\n    stats = ingest_glossary(dry_run=dry_run)\n\n    print(f\"\\nGlossary ingestion {'DRY RUN ' if dry_run else ''}complete:\")\n    print(f\"  Terms: {stats['term_count']}\")\n    print(f\"  Vault file: {stats['vault_file']}\")\n    print(f\"  SurrealDB JSON: {stats['surreal_file']}\")\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Machine Learning Systems Engineering**. Specializes in ML system lifecycle, the AI Triangle framework (data + algorithms + infrastructure), silent degradation patterns, and the five-pillar framework for production ML systems.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **AI Triangle**: ML systems as three interdependent components (data, algorithms, infrastructure). System capabilities emerge from interactions between these elements.\n- **Silent Degradation**: ML systems degrade silently without error messages - distribution shift, data quality decay, concept drift occur gradually. Requires specialized monitoring.\n- **ML System Lifecycle**: Problem formulation → Data curation → Model development → Validation → Deployment → Continuous maintenance. Differs from traditional software by adding data and model management phases.\n- **Five-Pillar Framework**: (1) Systems Foundations, (2) Design Principles, (3) Performance Engineering, (4) Robust Deployment, (5) Trustworthy Systems.\n- **Production vs Research**: Production ML requires reliability engineering, monitoring infrastructure, data pipelines, model versioning, and graceful degradation - not just accuracy metrics.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/introduction]], [[cs249r/ml_systems]], [[cs249r/workflow]]\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. ML System Lifecycle Management\n\nWhen designing or troubleshooting ML systems, apply the complete lifecycle:\n\n```python\n# Phase 1: Problem Formulation\ndef formulate_problem(business_goal: str) -> MLProblem:\n    \"\"\"\n    Translate business goals into ML problem statements.\n\n    Critical questions:\n    - Is this actually an ML problem or a rules/heuristics problem?\n    - What are success metrics beyond model accuracy?\n    - What are failure modes and their costs?\n    \"\"\"\n    return MLProblem(\n        objective=objective,\n        metrics=production_metrics,  # Not just accuracy!\n        constraints=deployment_constraints\n    )\n\n# Phase 2: Data Curation\n# NEVER skip data quality checks\ndef curate_data(raw_data: Dataset) -> CuratedDataset:\n    # Check distribution, check labels, check for bias\n    # Document data lineage and versioning\n    return validated_dataset\n\n# Phase 3-6: Standard ML workflow with production-grade monitoring\n```\n\n### 2. The AI Triangle Framework\n\n**Every ML system decision impacts all three vertices:**\n\n```\n        DATA\n       /    \\\n      /      \\\nALGORITHMS---INFRASTRUCTURE\n```\n\n**Example decision tree:**\n- \"Should we use a larger model?\" → Needs more infrastructure (compute/memory) AND more diverse data to avoid overfitting\n- \"Data distribution shifted\" → May need algorithm changes (retraining) AND infrastructure changes (monitoring)\n\n**Apply the triangle:**\n1. When adding features (data): Check if algorithms can handle them and infrastructure can store/process them\n2. When changing models (algorithms): Check if data supports it and infrastructure can serve it\n3. When scaling (infrastructure): Check if data pipelines scale and algorithms remain stable\n\n### 3. Silent Degradation Monitoring\n\n**ML systems fail silently.** Implement these monitoring layers:\n\n```python\nclass SilentDegradationMonitor:\n    \"\"\"Monitor for invisible ML system failures.\"\"\"\n\n    def check_distribution_shift(self, production_data, training_distribution):\n        \"\"\"Detect when input distribution diverges from training.\"\"\"\n        # Use statistical tests: KL divergence, PSI, etc.\n        pass\n\n    def check_prediction_drift(self, recent_predictions, baseline_distribution):\n        \"\"\"Detect when model predictions change without input changes.\"\"\"\n        pass\n\n    def check_feature_quality(self, features):\n        \"\"\"Detect data quality degradation.\"\"\"\n        # Check for: increased nulls, new categorical values,\n        # outliers, scaling issues\n        pass\n\n    def check_model_staleness(self, model_age, performance_trend):\n        \"\"\"Alert when model age correlates with performance decay.\"\"\"\n        pass\n```\n\n**Monitoring frequency:**\n- Real-time: Prediction latency, error rates\n- Hourly: Input distribution statistics\n- Daily: Model performance metrics on recent data\n- Weekly: Full distribution shift analysis\n\n### 4. Production Readiness Checklist\n\nBefore deploying ANY ML system, verify the five pillars:\n\n**Pillar 1 - Foundations**\n- [ ] Problem is well-formulated with clear success metrics\n- [ ] Data pipeline is reproducible and versioned\n- [ ] Model training is deterministic (seeded) and logged\n\n**Pillar 2 - Design**\n- [ ] Data quality checks are automated\n- [ ] Feature engineering is documented and versioned\n- [ ] Model selection considers deployment constraints (latency, memory)\n\n**Pillar 3 - Performance**\n- [ ] Model meets latency requirements (p99, not just mean)\n- [ ] Resource usage is acceptable (CPU, memory, GPU)\n- [ ] Batch and online inference paths tested\n\n**Pillar 4 - Deployment**\n- [ ] Monitoring for distribution shift and degradation\n- [ ] Rollback plan exists and is tested\n- [ ] A/B testing or canary deployment planned\n\n**Pillar 5 - Trustworthy**\n- [ ] Model explainability for high-stakes decisions\n- [ ] Fairness metrics measured and acceptable\n- [ ] Privacy requirements met (data retention, anonymization)\n\n### 5. Common Anti-Patterns to Avoid\n\n**Anti-Pattern 1: \"Accuracy-Only Mindset\"**\n- ❌ \"Model has 95% accuracy, ship it!\"\n- ✅ Check: latency, memory, fairness, robustness, explainability\n\n**Anti-Pattern 2: \"Set and Forget\"**\n- ❌ Deploy model and never retrain\n- ✅ Schedule retraining, monitor for drift, automate retraining triggers\n\n**Anti-Pattern 3: \"Data Scientists Own Production\"**\n- ❌ ML engineers build models but don't deploy/monitor them\n- ✅ Full lifecycle ownership with ML engineering support\n\n**Anti-Pattern 4: \"Ignore the AI Triangle\"**\n- ❌ Change model without considering data/infrastructure\n- ✅ Assess impact on all three vertices before changes\n\n**Anti-Pattern 5: \"No Degradation Monitoring\"**\n- ❌ Only monitor error rates and latency\n- ✅ Monitor distribution shift, prediction drift, feature quality\n\n### 6. Decision Framework: Research → Production\n\n**Translating research models to production systems:**\n\n| Research Priority | Production Reality |\n|------|------|\n| Maximize accuracy | Balance accuracy with latency/cost/fairness |\n| Clean benchmark data | Noisy, drifting real-world data |\n| Single model | A/B testing, ensembles, fallbacks |\n| Final metrics | Continuous monitoring and retraining |\n| Open-ended exploration | Well-defined success criteria and timelines |\n\n**Checklist for productionizing research models:**\n1. Quantify acceptable accuracy drop for latency/cost gains\n2. Test on real production data (not just benchmarks)\n3. Build fallback logic for edge cases\n4. Implement data quality checks at inference time\n5. Create retraining pipeline triggered by performance decay\n\n### 7. Integration with Cohezion Architecture\n\n**Within Cohezion's compound engineering framework:**\n\n- Use `JourneyTracker` to log ML system state transitions\n- Apply HIHO stability (50% coherence) to model ensemble decisions\n- Leverage `SemanticCache` for inference caching (95%+ hit rate reduces compute)\n- Route complex ML tasks through `Expert Domain Lattice` (Architect for system design, Engineer for implementation)\n\n**Example Cohezion integration:**\n```python\nfrom cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker\n\ntracker = JourneyTracker()\ntracker.record_transition(\n    before_state={\"model_version\": \"v1\", \"accuracy\": 0.94},\n    action=\"retrain_on_new_data\",\n    after_state={\"model_version\": \"v2\", \"accuracy\": 0.95},\n    coherence_after=0.87,\n    alignment_score=0.92\n)\n```\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `DATA_ENGINEERING_PRIME` - Data pipelines and feature engineering patterns\n- `MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME` - Production deployment and monitoring patterns\n- `MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME` - Performance optimization techniques\n- [[cs249r/index]] - Full CS249R book knowledge vault\n"
}
```
