```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: DNN_ARCHITECTURES_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Deep Neural Network Architectures**. Specializes in CNNs, RNNs, Transformers, attention mechanisms, architecture selection criteria, and computational complexity analysis for different DNN types.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **CNNs (Convolutional Neural Networks)**: Spatial feature extraction via convolution layers. Best for images, spatial data. Key components: conv layers, pooling, spatial hierarchy.\n- **RNNs (Recurrent Neural Networks)**: Sequential data processing with hidden state. Variants: LSTM (long short-term memory), GRU (gated recurrent units). Best for time series, sequences.\n- **Transformers**: Attention-based architecture without recurrence. Parallelizable, scales to massive datasets. Foundation for GPT, BERT, vision transformers.\n- **Attention Mechanisms**: Learn what to focus on in input. Types: self-attention (within sequence), cross-attention (between sequences), multi-head attention (parallel attention).\n- **Architecture Selection**: Choose based on data type (images→CNN, sequences→Transformer, time series→RNN/Transformer), compute budget, and latency requirements.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/dnn_architectures]], [[cs249r/dl_primer]], [[cs249r/efficient_ai]]\n**Related TinyTorch Modules**: Module 09 (convolutions), Module 12 (attention), Module 13 (transformers)\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Architecture Selection Decision Tree\n\n```\nInput Data Type?\n├─ Images/Spatial Data → CNN\n│   ├─ Small dataset → ResNet-18, EfficientNet-B0\n│   ├─ Large dataset → ResNet-50, EfficientNet-B3\n│   └─ Strict latency → MobileNetV3, EfficientNet-Lite\n│\n├─ Sequential/Text Data → Transformer (preferred) or RNN\n│   ├─ Long sequences → Transformer (better parallelization)\n│   ├─ Real-time streaming → RNN/LSTM (processes sequentially)\n│   └─ Limited compute → Distilled BERT, TinyBERT\n│\n├─ Time Series → Transformer or RNN\n│   ├─ Irregular sampling → Transformer (positional encoding handles gaps)\n│   ├─ Very long history → Sparse Transformer, Longformer\n│   └─ Real-time prediction → LSTM, GRU\n│\n└─ Multimodal (Image + Text) → Hybrid\n    ├─ CNN for images + Transformer for text → fusion layer\n    └─ Vision Transformer (ViT) for unified processing\n```\n\n### 2. CNN Architecture Patterns\n\n**Convolutional layers for spatial feature extraction:**\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef apply_convolution(input_image, kernel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Core CNN operation: sliding window feature extraction.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 09 (convolutions)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Convolution = dot product of kernel with image patches\n    # Output size = (input_size - kernel_size + 2*padding) / stride + 1\n    pass\n\n# Standard CNN architecture\nclass CNN:\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.conv1 = Conv2d(in_channels=3, out_channels=64, kernel_size=3)\n        self.pool = MaxPool2d(kernel_size=2)\n        self.conv2 = Conv2d(64, 128, 3)\n        self.fc = Linear(128 * 7 * 7, num_classes)\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        x = relu(self.conv1(x))  # Spatial features\n        x = self.pool(x)          # Downsample\n        x = relu(self.conv2(x))  # Higher-level features\n        x = self.pool(x)\n        x = x.flatten()\n        return self.fc(x)\n```\n\n**Key CNN design choices:**\n- **Kernel size**: 3x3 most common (efficient), 1x1 for channel mixing, 5x5+ for larger receptive fields\n- **Pooling**: Max pooling (preserves features), Average pooling (smooths), Stride convolutions (learnable downsampling)\n- **Depth vs Width**: Deeper (more layers) = more abstract features, Wider (more channels) = more capacity\n\n### 3. Transformer Architecture Patterns\n\n**Attention-based sequence processing:**\n\n```python\nclass MultiHeadAttention:\n    \"\"\"\n    Core transformer operation: learn what to attend to.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 12 (attention), Module 13 (transformers)\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, d_model, num_heads):\n        self.d_model = d_model\n        self.num_heads = num_heads\n        self.head_dim = d_model // num_heads\n\n        # Q, K, V projections\n        self.q_proj = Linear(d_model, d_model)\n        self.k_proj = Linear(d_model, d_model)\n        self.v_proj = Linear(d_model, d_model)\n        self.out_proj = Linear(d_model, d_model)\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # 1. Project to Q, K, V\n        Q = self.q_proj(x)  # Queries: what I'm looking for\n        K = self.k_proj(x)  # Keys: what each position offers\n        V = self.v_proj(x)  # Values: actual content\n\n        # 2. Scaled dot-product attention\n        # attention_weights = softmax(Q @ K^T / sqrt(d_k))\n        # output = attention_weights @ V\n\n        # 3. Multi-head: parallel attention on different subspaces\n        return self.out_proj(output)\n```\n\n**Transformer advantages:**\n- Parallelizable (unlike RNNs) → faster training on GPUs\n- Long-range dependencies via attention\n- State-of-the-art for NLP, increasingly for vision\n\n**Transformer costs:**\n- O(n²) complexity in sequence length (attention over all pairs)\n- Memory intensive (KV cache for generation)\n- Requires large datasets to train effectively\n\n### 4. Computational Complexity Analysis\n\n**Model selection based on compute constraints:**\n\n| Architecture | Time Complexity | Space Complexity | Best For |\n|------|------|------|------|\n| CNN (single conv) | O(C_out × C_in × k² × H × W) | O(C_out × k²) | Images, spatial data |\n| RNN (single step) | O(d × h + h × h) | O(h) | Streaming sequences |\n| Transformer (single layer) | O(n² × d) | O(n × d) | Parallel sequence processing |\n\n**Where:**\n- C = channels, k = kernel size, H/W = height/width (CNN)\n- d = embedding dimension, h = hidden size (RNN)\n- n = sequence length, d = model dimension (Transformer)\n\n**Practical implications:**\n- **Long sequences** → Transformer O(n²) becomes prohibitive. Use sparse attention, hierarchical models, or RNNs.\n- **Real-time** → CNN or RNN preferred (no need to process full sequence at once)\n- **Batch processing** → Transformer preferred (parallelizable)\n\n### 5. Architecture Optimization Patterns\n\n**Pattern 1: Depth vs Width Trade-off**\n```python\n# Deeper network (more layers, fewer parameters per layer)\ndeep_model = [\n    Conv(64, 64, 3) for _ in range(10)  # 10 layers, 64 channels\n]  # Good for learning hierarchical features\n\n# Wider network (fewer layers, more parameters per layer)\nwide_model = [\n    Conv(256, 256, 3) for _ in range(3)  # 3 layers, 256 channels\n]  # Good for learning diverse features in parallel\n```\n\n**Pattern 2: Residual Connections (Skip Connections)**\n```python\ndef residual_block(x):\n    \"\"\"ResNet-style skip connection.\"\"\"\n    identity = x\n    x = conv(x)\n    x = relu(x)\n    x = conv(x)\n    return relu(x + identity)  # Add input to output\n\n# Why it works: Allows gradients to flow directly through network\n# Enables training very deep networks (100+ layers)\n```\n\n**Pattern 3: Efficient Attention (for long sequences)**\n```python\n# Standard attention: O(n²)\nattention = softmax(Q @ K.T / sqrt(d)) @ V\n\n# Sparse attention: O(n * sqrt(n))\n# Only attend to nearby positions + few global positions\n\n# Linear attention: O(n)\n# Approximate attention with kernel methods\n```\n\n### 6. Model Selection Criteria\n\n**Decision matrix for production deployment:**\n\n| Criterion | CNN | RNN/LSTM | Transformer |\n|------|------|------|------|\n| **Latency** | Low | Medium | Medium-High |\n| **Throughput** | High | Low (sequential) | High (parallel) |\n| **Memory** | Low | Medium | High |\n| **Long sequences** | N/A | Good | Excellent (but costly) |\n| **Training speed** | Fast | Slow | Fast (with GPUs) |\n| **Data efficiency** | Medium | High | Low (needs big data) |\n\n**Recommended choices:**\n- **Mobile/Edge**: MobileNet (CNN), DistilBERT (Transformer), quantized models\n- **Cloud/Server**: ResNet-50 (CNN), GPT-style Transformer (text), ViT (vision)\n- **Real-time**: EfficientNet-Lite (CNN), LSTM (sequences)\n\n### 7. Integration with TinyTorch Modules\n\n**TinyTorch provides from-scratch implementations:**\n\n```python\n# Module 09: Convolutions\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.convolutions import Conv2d, MaxPool2d\n\n# Module 12: Attention\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.attention import MultiHeadAttention\n\n# Module 13: Transformers\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.transformers import TransformerEncoder\n\n# Build a complete vision model\nmodel = Sequential([\n    Conv2d(3, 64, kernel_size=3),  # TinyTorch conv\n    ReLU(),\n    MaxPool2d(2),\n    # ... more layers\n])\n```\n\n**Educational value:** TinyTorch implements these from NumPy only, showing the mathematics behind each architecture without framework abstractions.\n\n### 8. Common Pitfalls\n\n**Pitfall 1: Wrong architecture for data type**\n- ❌ Using RNN for images (loses spatial structure)\n- ✅ CNN for spatial data, Transformer for sequences\n\n**Pitfall 2: Not considering deployment constraints**\n- ❌ Using Transformer with 1B parameters for mobile app\n- ✅ Check latency/memory budget before architecture selection\n\n**Pitfall 3: Ignoring inductive biases**\n- CNNs have spatial locality bias (good for images)\n- RNNs have temporal locality bias (good for sequences)\n- Transformers have no built-in bias (need more data but more flexible)\n\n**Pitfall 4: Optimizing for wrong metric**\n- ❌ Maximizing accuracy without checking latency\n- ✅ Balance accuracy with inference speed and resource usage\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME` - Model quantization, pruning, distillation for deployment\n- `MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME` - Operator fusion, graph optimization\n- `ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME` - ML system lifecycle and production readiness\n- TinyTorch modules: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/{convolutions,attention,transformers}.py`\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: DATA_ENGINEERING_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**ML Data Engineering**. Specializes in data pipelines, feature engineering, data quality assurance, distribution shift detection, and data versioning for production ML systems.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Data Pipelines**: Automated workflows for data collection, cleaning, transformation, and serving. Must be reproducible, monitored, and versioned.\n- **Feature Engineering**: Transform raw data into ML-ready features. Critical for model performance - often more impactful than model architecture choices.\n- **Data Quality**: Validation checks for completeness, consistency, accuracy. Bad data → bad models, regardless of algorithm sophistication.\n- **Distribution Shift**: When production data distribution differs from training data. Types: covariate shift (input changes), label shift (output changes), concept drift (relationship changes).\n- **Data Versioning**: Track dataset versions like code versions. Essential for reproducibility and debugging production issues.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/data_engineering]], [[cs249r/workflow]], [[cs249r/ml_systems]]\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Data Pipeline Architecture\n\n**Production-grade data pipeline components:**\n\n```python\nclass MLDataPipeline:\n    \"\"\"End-to-end data pipeline for ML systems.\"\"\"\n\n    def collect(self, sources: list[DataSource]) -> RawData:\n        \"\"\"Stage 1: Data collection from multiple sources.\"\"\"\n        # Combine databases, APIs, logs, user events\n        # Track data lineage: where did each record come from?\n        return raw_data\n\n    def validate_raw(self, raw_data: RawData) -> ValidationReport:\n        \"\"\"Stage 2: Raw data quality checks.\"\"\"\n        checks = [\n            self.check_schema_compliance(raw_data),\n            self.check_completeness(raw_data),\n            self.check_freshness(raw_data),\n            self.check_duplicates(raw_data),\n        ]\n        return ValidationReport(checks)\n\n    def clean(self, raw_data: RawData) -> CleanData:\n        \"\"\"Stage 3: Data cleaning and normalization.\"\"\"\n        # Handle missing values, remove duplicates, fix errors\n        # CRITICAL: Document all cleaning decisions\n        return clean_data\n\n    def engineer_features(self, clean_data: CleanData) -> Features:\n        \"\"\"Stage 4: Feature engineering.\"\"\"\n        features = []\n\n        # Numerical features\n        features.extend(self.normalize_numerical(clean_data))\n\n        # Categorical features\n        features.extend(self.encode_categorical(clean_data))\n\n        # Temporal features\n        features.extend(self.extract_time_features(clean_data))\n\n        # Domain-specific features\n        features.extend(self.domain_transforms(clean_data))\n\n        return Features(features)\n\n    def split(self, features: Features) -> TrainValTest:\n        \"\"\"Stage 5: Create train/validation/test splits.\"\"\"\n        # Stratified split to maintain class distribution\n        # Time-based split for time series (no future leakage!)\n        return splits\n\n    def version(self, dataset: Dataset) -> DatasetVersion:\n        \"\"\"Stage 6: Version and snapshot the dataset.\"\"\"\n        # DVC, MLflow, or custom versioning\n        # Store: data hash, schema, statistics, metadata\n        return version_id\n```\n\n### 2. Feature Engineering Patterns\n\n**Pattern 1: Numerical Features**\n```python\ndef engineer_numerical_features(df):\n    \"\"\"Transform numerical columns into ML-ready features.\"\"\"\n\n    # Scaling (critical for gradient-based models)\n    from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler\n    scaled = StandardScaler().fit_transform(df[numerical_cols])\n\n    # Log transform (for skewed distributions)\n    log_features = np.log1p(df[skewed_cols])  # log1p handles zeros\n\n    # Binning (discretize continuous variables)\n    age_bins = pd.cut(df['age'], bins=[0, 18, 35, 50, 65, 100])\n\n    # Polynomial features (interactions)\n    from sklearn.preprocessing import PolynomialFeatures\n    poly = PolynomialFeatures(degree=2, interaction_only=True)\n    interactions = poly.fit_transform(df[['feature1', 'feature2']])\n\n    return features\n```\n\n**Pattern 2: Categorical Features**\n```python\ndef engineer_categorical_features(df):\n    \"\"\"Encode categorical variables.\"\"\"\n\n    # One-hot encoding (for low-cardinality categoricals)\n    one_hot = pd.get_dummies(df['category'], prefix='cat')\n\n    # Label encoding (for ordinal categoricals)\n    from sklearn.preprocessing import LabelEncoder\n    df['size_encoded'] = LabelEncoder().fit_transform(df['size'])  # S, M, L -> 0, 1, 2\n\n    # Target encoding (use mean target value per category)\n    # DANGER: Must be done in cross-validation to avoid leakage!\n    target_means = df.groupby('category')['target'].mean()\n    df['category_target_enc'] = df['category'].map(target_means)\n\n    # Hash encoding (for high-cardinality categoricals)\n    from sklearn.feature_extraction import FeatureHasher\n    hasher = FeatureHasher(n_features=100, input_type='string')\n    hashed = hasher.transform(df['high_cardinality_col'])\n\n    return features\n```\n\n**Pattern 3: Temporal Features**\n```python\ndef extract_time_features(df, timestamp_col):\n    \"\"\"Extract temporal features from timestamps.\"\"\"\n    df['hour'] = df[timestamp_col].dt.hour\n    df['day_of_week'] = df[timestamp_col].dt.dayofweek\n    df['day_of_month'] = df[timestamp_col].dt.day\n    df['month'] = df[timestamp_col].dt.month\n    df['is_weekend'] = df['day_of_week'].isin([5, 6]).astype(int)\n    df['is_holiday'] = df[timestamp_col].isin(holidays).astype(int)\n\n    # Time since last event (for event data)\n    df['time_since_last'] = df.groupby('user_id')[timestamp_col].diff()\n\n    # Cyclic encoding (preserves cyclical nature)\n    df['hour_sin'] = np.sin(2 * np.pi * df['hour'] / 24)\n    df['hour_cos'] = np.cos(2 * np.pi * df['hour'] / 24)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n### 3. Data Quality Validation\n\n**Comprehensive validation checks:**\n\n```python\nclass DataQualityValidator:\n    \"\"\"Automated data quality checks.\"\"\"\n\n    def check_completeness(self, df):\n        \"\"\"Check for missing values.\"\"\"\n        null_counts = df.isnull().sum()\n        null_pct = (null_counts / len(df)) * 100\n\n        # Fail if critical columns have > threshold% nulls\n        for col in critical_columns:\n            if null_pct[col] > max_null_threshold:\n                raise DataQualityError(f\"{col} has {null_pct[col]:.1f}% nulls\")\n\n        return null_pct\n\n    def check_schema_compliance(self, df, expected_schema):\n        \"\"\"Verify column names, types, and constraints.\"\"\"\n        # Check column presence\n        missing_cols = set(expected_schema.keys()) - set(df.columns)\n        extra_cols = set(df.columns) - set(expected_schema.keys())\n\n        # Check data types\n        for col, expected_type in expected_schema.items():\n            if df[col].dtype != expected_type:\n                raise SchemaError(f\"{col} has type {df[col].dtype}, expected {expected_type}\")\n\n        return schema_report\n\n    def check_distribution_shift(self, production_df, training_stats):\n        \"\"\"Detect if production data diverges from training distribution.\"\"\"\n        from scipy.stats import ks_2samp\n\n        alerts = []\n        for col in numerical_columns:\n            # Kolmogorov-Smirnov test\n            statistic, p_value = ks_2samp(\n                production_df[col],\n                training_stats[col]['samples']\n            )\n\n            if p_value < 0.01:  # Significant difference\n                alerts.append(f\"Distribution shift detected in {col}\")\n\n        return alerts\n\n    def check_label_quality(self, df, label_col):\n        \"\"\"Validate labels for supervised learning.\"\"\"\n        # Check class balance\n        class_dist = df[label_col].value_counts(normalize=True)\n\n        # Warn if severe class imbalance\n        min_class_pct = class_dist.min()\n        if min_class_pct < 0.01:  # <1% of data\n            logger.warning(f\"Severe class imbalance: minority class has {min_class_pct:.2%}\")\n\n        # Check for label noise (if we have confidence scores)\n        # Flag examples with low confidence for review\n        return quality_report\n```\n\n### 4. Distribution Shift Detection and Mitigation\n\n**Types of distribution shift:**\n\n```python\nclass DistributionShiftMonitor:\n    \"\"\"Monitor and respond to distribution shifts.\"\"\"\n\n    def detect_covariate_shift(self, prod_data, train_data):\n        \"\"\"\n        Covariate shift: P(X) changes, P(Y|X) stays same.\n        Example: Model trained on summer data, deployed in winter.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Statistical tests on input features\n        shift_scores = []\n        for feature in features:\n            score = self.calculate_shift_score(\n                prod_data[feature],\n                train_data[feature]\n            )\n            shift_scores.append((feature, score))\n\n        return sorted(shift_scores, key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n\n    def detect_label_shift(self, prod_predictions, train_labels):\n        \"\"\"\n        Label shift: P(Y) changes, P(X|Y) stays same.\n        Example: Fraud detection model sees higher fraud rate.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Compare label distributions\n        prod_dist = np.bincount(prod_predictions) / len(prod_predictions)\n        train_dist = np.bincount(train_labels) / len(train_labels)\n\n        kl_div = scipy.stats.entropy(prod_dist, train_dist)\n\n        if kl_div > threshold:\n            logger.alert(\"Label distribution has shifted significantly\")\n\n        return kl_div\n\n    def mitigate_shift(self, shift_type):\n        \"\"\"Mitigation strategies based on shift type.\"\"\"\n        if shift_type == \"covariate\":\n            # Option 1: Retrain on recent data\n            # Option 2: Importance weighting\n            # Option 3: Domain adaptation\n            return self.importance_weighted_retraining()\n\n        elif shift_type == \"label\":\n            # Option 1: Re-calibrate decision thresholds\n            # Option 2: Retrain with updated class priors\n            return self.recalibrate_thresholds()\n```\n\n### 5. Data Versioning Best Practices\n\n**Track datasets like code:**\n\n```python\nclass DataVersionManager:\n    \"\"\"Version datasets for reproducibility.\"\"\"\n\n    def create_snapshot(self, dataset_path, metadata):\n        \"\"\"\n        Create immutable snapshot of dataset.\n\n        Store: data, schema, statistics, lineage.\n        \"\"\"\n        snapshot = {\n            \"version_id\": self.generate_version_id(),\n            \"timestamp\": datetime.now(),\n            \"data_hash\": self.hash_dataset(dataset_path),\n            \"schema\": self.extract_schema(dataset_path),\n            \"statistics\": self.compute_statistics(dataset_path),\n            \"lineage\": metadata[\"lineage\"],  # How was this dataset created?\n        }\n\n        # Store in version control (DVC, MLflow, etc.)\n        self.store_snapshot(snapshot)\n\n        return snapshot[\"version_id\"]\n\n    def load_version(self, version_id):\n        \"\"\"Load specific dataset version for debugging or retraining.\"\"\"\n        snapshot = self.retrieve_snapshot(version_id)\n        dataset = self.fetch_data(snapshot[\"data_hash\"])\n\n        # Verify hash matches\n        assert self.hash_dataset(dataset) == snapshot[\"data_hash\"]\n\n        return dataset\n```\n\n**Critical for:**\n- Reproducing model training\n- Debugging production issues (\"What data did model X see?\")\n- Compliance and auditing\n\n### 6. Common Data Engineering Anti-Patterns\n\n**Anti-Pattern 1: Data Leakage**\n```python\n# ❌ WRONG: Target encoding before train/test split\ndf['category_mean'] = df.groupby('category')['target'].transform('mean')\ntrain, test = train_test_split(df)\n\n# ✅ CORRECT: Fit on train, transform test\ntrain, test = train_test_split(df)\ntarget_means = train.groupby('category')['target'].mean()\ntrain['category_mean'] = train['category'].map(target_means)\ntest['category_mean'] = test['category'].map(target_means)\n```\n\n**Anti-Pattern 2: Ignoring Time Ordering**\n```python\n# ❌ WRONG: Random split for time series\ntrain, test = train_test_split(time_series_df)  # Future leakage!\n\n# ✅ CORRECT: Time-based split\ncutoff_date = df['date'].quantile(0.8)\ntrain = df[df['date'] <= cutoff_date]\ntest = df[df['date'] > cutoff_date]\n```\n\n**Anti-Pattern 3: Not Validating Pipeline Outputs**\n```python\n# ❌ WRONG: Assume pipeline always succeeds\nprocessed_data = pipeline.transform(raw_data)\n\n# ✅ CORRECT: Validate at each stage\nprocessed_data = pipeline.transform(raw_data)\nvalidator.check_schema(processed_data)\nvalidator.check_distributions(processed_data)\nif not validator.is_valid():\n    raise PipelineError(\"Data validation failed\")\n```\n\n### 7. Integration with Cohezion\n\n**Leverage Cohezion's caching and persistence:**\n\n```python\nfrom cohezion.cache.semantic_cache import SemanticCache\nfrom cohezion.core.persistence.surreal_client import SurrealClient\n\n# Cache expensive feature engineering\ncache = SemanticCache()\nfeatures = cache.get_or_compute(\n    key=\"feature_engineering_v2_\" + data_hash,\n    compute_fn=lambda: engineer_features(raw_data),\n    ttl=3600  # 1 hour\n)\n\n# Store dataset metadata in SurrealDB\ndb = SurrealClient()\nawait db.insert(\"dataset_version\", {\n    \"id\": version_id,\n    \"created_at\": timestamp,\n    \"features\": feature_list,\n    \"statistics\": stats,\n    \"lineage\": lineage_graph\n})\n```\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME` - ML system lifecycle and production readiness\n- `MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME` - Data monitoring in production\n- [[cs249r/data_engineering]] - Full chapter concepts from CS249R\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Efficient AI and Model Compression**. Specializes in knowledge distillation, pruning, quantization (int8/int4), neural architecture search (NAS), and efficient inference on resource-constrained devices.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Knowledge Distillation**: Train a small \"student\" model to mimic a large \"teacher\" model. Types: response-based (soft targets), feature-based (intermediate representations), attention transfer.\n- **Pruning**: Remove redundant weights or structures. Types: unstructured (individual weights), structured (entire channels/filters), gradual magnitude pruning. Lottery Ticket Hypothesis: sparse subnetworks can match full network performance.\n- **Quantization**: Reduce numerical precision of weights and activations. Levels: FP32 → FP16 → INT8 → INT4 → binary. Post-training quantization (PTQ) vs quantization-aware training (QAT).\n- **Neural Architecture Search (NAS)**: Automated discovery of efficient architectures. Approaches: reinforcement learning, evolutionary, differentiable (DARTS). One-shot NAS with weight sharing for efficiency.\n- **Scaling Laws**: Chinchilla scaling — optimal model size depends on compute budget and data budget. Compute-optimal training balances parameters and tokens.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/efficient_ai]], [[cs249r/optimizations]], [[cs249r/hw_acceleration]]\n**Related TinyTorch Modules**: Module 15 (quantization), Module 16 (compression), Module 17 (acceleration)\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Compression Strategy Selection\n\n**Choose the right technique for your constraints:**\n\n```\nDeployment Target?\n├─ Edge/Mobile (< 50 MB model)\n│   ├─ Accuracy critical → Quantization-Aware Training (QAT)\n│   ├─ Latency critical → Structured Pruning + INT8\n│   └─ Both → Distillation + Pruning + Quantization (compound)\n│\n├─ Server (latency-sensitive)\n│   ├─ Batch inference → INT8 Quantization (fastest ROI)\n│   ├─ Real-time → FP16 Mixed Precision\n│   └─ Cost reduction → Distillation to smaller model\n│\n└─ Extreme constraint (< 1 MB, MCU)\n    ├─ Binary/Ternary quantization\n    ├─ Aggressive pruning (90%+ sparsity)\n    └─ Custom NAS for target hardware\n```\n\n### 2. Quantization Patterns\n\n**Post-Training Quantization (PTQ):**\n```python\ndef quantize_tensor(tensor, num_bits=8):\n    \"\"\"\n    Quantize floating-point tensor to fixed-point.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 15 (quantization)\n    \"\"\"\n    qmin = -(2 ** (num_bits - 1))\n    qmax = 2 ** (num_bits - 1) - 1\n\n    # Compute scale and zero point\n    min_val, max_val = tensor.min(), tensor.max()\n    scale = (max_val - min_val) / (qmax - qmin)\n    zero_point = qmin - min_val / scale\n\n    # Quantize\n    q_tensor = (tensor / scale + zero_point).round().clip(qmin, qmax)\n\n    return q_tensor.astype(int), scale, zero_point\n\ndef dequantize_tensor(q_tensor, scale, zero_point):\n    \"\"\"Recover approximate floating-point values.\"\"\"\n    return (q_tensor.astype(float) - zero_point) * scale\n```\n\n**Quantization-Aware Training (QAT):**\n```python\nclass FakeQuantize:\n    \"\"\"Simulate quantization during training (straight-through estimator).\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_bits=8):\n        self.num_bits = num_bits\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # Forward: quantize then dequantize\n        q, scale, zp = quantize_tensor(x, self.num_bits)\n        x_hat = dequantize_tensor(q, scale, zp)\n        return x_hat\n\n    def backward(self, grad_output):\n        # Straight-through: pass gradient unchanged\n        return grad_output\n```\n\n**Precision trade-offs:**\n\n| Precision | Size Reduction | Accuracy Drop | Hardware Support |\n|-----------|---------------|---------------|-----------------|\n| FP32 → FP16 | 2x | ~0% | GPUs, modern CPUs |\n| FP32 → INT8 | 4x | 0.1-1% | Most hardware |\n| FP32 → INT4 | 8x | 1-5% | Specialized HW |\n| FP32 → Binary | 32x | 5-15% | Custom accelerators |\n\n### 3. Pruning Patterns\n\n**Magnitude-Based Pruning:**\n```python\ndef prune_by_magnitude(weights, sparsity=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Remove weights with smallest absolute values.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 16 (compression)\n    \"\"\"\n    threshold = np.percentile(np.abs(weights), sparsity * 100)\n    mask = np.abs(weights) > threshold\n    return weights * mask, mask\n\ndef gradual_pruning_schedule(initial_sparsity, final_sparsity, total_steps):\n    \"\"\"\n    Cubic sparsity schedule (from Zhu & Gupta 2017).\n    Gradually increases sparsity during training.\n    \"\"\"\n    def sparsity_at_step(step):\n        progress = step / total_steps\n        return final_sparsity + (initial_sparsity - final_sparsity) * (1 - progress) ** 3\n    return sparsity_at_step\n```\n\n**Structured vs Unstructured:**\n```python\n# Unstructured: prune individual weights (high sparsity, needs sparse HW)\nmask = np.abs(weights) > threshold  # Irregular sparsity pattern\n\n# Structured: prune entire channels/filters (lower sparsity, runs on any HW)\nchannel_importance = np.linalg.norm(conv_weights, axis=(1, 2, 3))  # L2 norm per channel\nchannels_to_keep = np.argsort(channel_importance)[-num_keep:]  # Keep most important\npruned_weights = conv_weights[channels_to_keep]\n```\n\n**Key insight:** Structured pruning gives smaller speedups but works on standard hardware. Unstructured pruning achieves higher compression but requires sparse matrix support.\n\n### 4. Knowledge Distillation Patterns\n\n**Response-Based Distillation:**\n```python\ndef distillation_loss(student_logits, teacher_logits, labels, temperature=4.0, alpha=0.7):\n    \"\"\"\n    Combine soft targets (from teacher) with hard targets (labels).\n\n    - Higher temperature → softer probability distribution → more knowledge transfer\n    - Alpha balances teacher knowledge vs ground truth\n    \"\"\"\n    # Soft targets: teacher's dark knowledge\n    soft_loss = kl_divergence(\n        softmax(student_logits / temperature),\n        softmax(teacher_logits / temperature)\n    ) * (temperature ** 2)  # Scale by T^2 to match gradient magnitude\n\n    # Hard targets: standard cross-entropy\n    hard_loss = cross_entropy(student_logits, labels)\n\n    return alpha * soft_loss + (1 - alpha) * hard_loss\n```\n\n**Distillation best practices:**\n- Teacher should be well-trained (overfit slightly is OK)\n- Temperature 2-20 (higher = more knowledge transfer, lower = more focus on top predictions)\n- Student architecture should be similar to teacher but smaller (same depth, fewer channels)\n- Train student longer than teacher (small models need more steps)\n\n### 5. Neural Architecture Search\n\n**NAS approaches by compute budget:**\n\n| Approach | Compute | Best For |\n|----------|---------|----------|\n| Reinforcement Learning NAS | Very high (1000+ GPU-hours) | Research, large budgets |\n| Evolutionary NAS | High (100+ GPU-hours) | Custom constraints |\n| Differentiable NAS (DARTS) | Medium (10+ GPU-hours) | Practical deployment |\n| One-Shot NAS | Low (1+ GPU-hours) | Rapid prototyping |\n\n**Efficient NAS pattern:**\n```python\nclass SearchSpace:\n    \"\"\"Define the architecture search space.\"\"\"\n    OPERATIONS = [\n        'conv_3x3', 'conv_5x5',    # Standard convolutions\n        'sep_conv_3x3',             # Depthwise separable (efficient)\n        'dil_conv_3x3',             # Dilated (larger receptive field)\n        'max_pool_3x3',             # Pooling (no parameters)\n        'skip_connect',             # Identity (residual)\n        'none',                     # Zero (prune this edge)\n    ]\n\n    def sample_architecture(self):\n        \"\"\"Random architecture from search space.\"\"\"\n        return [random.choice(self.OPERATIONS) for _ in range(self.num_edges)]\n```\n\n### 6. Compound Compression Pipeline\n\n**Maximum compression: combine techniques sequentially:**\n\n```\nStep 1: Distillation (teacher → student)\n  └─ 3-5x parameter reduction\nStep 2: Structured Pruning (remove channels)\n  └─ Additional 2-3x reduction\nStep 3: Quantization (FP32 → INT8)\n  └─ Additional 4x reduction\nStep 4: Operator Fusion (combine layers)\n  └─ Latency improvement\n\nTotal: 24-60x compression with <2% accuracy drop\n```\n\n**Order matters:** Distill first (creates a good small model), prune second (removes redundancy), quantize last (reduces precision of what remains).\n\n### 7. Hardware-Aware Optimization\n\n**For Cohezion's AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395:**\n- Use XNNPACK or OneDNN for CPU inference optimization\n- AVX-512 enables fast INT8 operations natively\n- Unified memory architecture means no CPU-GPU transfer overhead\n- Profile with TinyTorch Module 14 (profiling) before optimizing\n\n**Measurement-first approach:**\n```python\n# Always profile before optimizing\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.profiling import profile_model\n\nresults = profile_model(model, sample_input)\n# Results show: time per layer, memory per layer, FLOPs per layer\n\n# Optimize the bottleneck, not everything\nslowest_layer = max(results, key=lambda r: r.time_ms)\n# Apply technique to THIS layer first\n```\n\n### 8. Common Pitfalls\n\n**Pitfall 1: Quantizing without calibration**\n- INT8 needs representative calibration data to set scale/zero-point\n- Use 100-1000 samples from training distribution\n\n**Pitfall 2: Pruning too aggressively at once**\n- Gradual pruning (cubic schedule) outperforms one-shot pruning\n- Allow fine-tuning between pruning steps\n\n**Pitfall 3: Wrong compression order**\n- Quantize AFTER pruning (not before)\n- Distill BEFORE pruning\n\n**Pitfall 4: Ignoring hardware**\n- Unstructured sparsity is useless without sparse matrix hardware support\n- INT4 is only faster on hardware with INT4 compute units\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME` - Operator fusion, graph optimization, benchmarking\n- `DNN_ARCHITECTURES_PRIME` - Architecture selection and design\n- `EDGE_INTELLIGENCE_PRIME` - Edge deployment and distributed inference\n- `HARDWARE_PROFILE_PRIME` - Cohezion's AMD hardware specifications\n- TinyTorch modules: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/{quantization,compression,acceleration}.py`\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Model Optimization and Performance Engineering**. Specializes in operator fusion, graph optimization, memory optimization, hardware-aware compilation, profiling methodology, and benchmarking for production ML systems.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Operator Fusion**: Combine multiple operations into a single kernel to reduce memory bandwidth overhead. Types: vertical fusion (sequential ops), horizontal fusion (parallel ops), element-wise fusion.\n- **Graph Optimization**: Transform computation graphs to reduce operations. Techniques: constant folding, dead code elimination, common subexpression elimination, algebraic simplification.\n- **Memory Optimization**: Reduce peak memory during training and inference. Techniques: gradient checkpointing, activation recomputation, memory-efficient attention, in-place operations.\n- **Hardware-Aware Compilation**: Map computation graphs to specific hardware capabilities. Tools: TVM, XLA, TensorRT, ONNX Runtime. Target-specific optimizations for CPU/GPU/NPU.\n- **Benchmarking Methodology**: Rigorous performance measurement. Warmup runs, statistical significance, system-level vs model-level metrics, reproducibility.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/optimizations]], [[cs249r/hw_acceleration]], [[cs249r/benchmarking]]\n**Related TinyTorch Modules**: Module 14 (profiling), Module 19 (benchmarking)\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Optimization Decision Framework\n\n**Profile before optimizing — never guess the bottleneck:**\n\n```python\ndef optimization_workflow(model, sample_input):\n    \"\"\"\n    Systematic optimization: measure → identify → optimize → verify.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 14 (profiling)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: Profile baseline\n    baseline = profile_model(model, sample_input, warmup=10, iterations=100)\n\n    # Step 2: Identify bottleneck\n    bottleneck = identify_bottleneck(baseline)\n    # Is it compute-bound or memory-bound?\n    # Compute-bound: arithmetic intensity > hardware threshold\n    # Memory-bound: waiting on data transfer\n\n    # Step 3: Apply targeted optimization\n    if bottleneck.type == \"memory\":\n        optimized = apply_memory_optimization(model)\n    elif bottleneck.type == \"compute\":\n        optimized = apply_compute_optimization(model)\n\n    # Step 4: Verify improvement (and no accuracy regression)\n    improved = profile_model(optimized, sample_input)\n    assert improved.latency < baseline.latency\n    assert abs(improved.accuracy - baseline.accuracy) < 0.01\n```\n\n### 2. Operator Fusion Patterns\n\n**Vertical Fusion (sequential operations):**\n```python\n# BEFORE: 3 separate kernel launches, 3 memory reads/writes\nx = conv2d(input)       # Read input, write x\nx = batch_norm(x)       # Read x, write x\nx = relu(x)             # Read x, write x\n\n# AFTER: 1 fused kernel, 1 memory read/write\nx = fused_conv_bn_relu(input)  # Read input, write x\n# 3x fewer memory accesses = significant speedup\n```\n\n**Element-wise Fusion:**\n```python\n# BEFORE: Multiple passes over the same data\ny = x * scale          # Pass 1\ny = y + bias           # Pass 2\ny = max(y, 0)          # Pass 3 (ReLU)\n\n# AFTER: Single pass\ndef fused_scale_bias_relu(x, scale, bias):\n    \"\"\"Fuse element-wise ops into a single pass.\"\"\"\n    return max(x * scale + bias, 0)  # 1 pass over data\n```\n\n**When fusion helps most:**\n- Small operations that are memory-bound (bandwidth-limited)\n- Sequential operations on the same tensor\n- Batch normalization + activation (almost always fusible)\n\n**When fusion doesn't help:**\n- Large compute-bound operations (already saturating compute units)\n- Operations with different data layouts (need transpose between them)\n\n### 3. Graph Optimization Techniques\n\n**Constant Folding:**\n```python\n# BEFORE: Computed at runtime\nweight = model.weight * 1.0  # Redundant multiplication\nbias = model.bias + 0.0      # Redundant addition\n\n# AFTER: Eliminated at compile time\nweight = model.weight  # Constants folded away\nbias = model.bias\n```\n\n**Common Subexpression Elimination:**\n```python\n# BEFORE: Redundant computation\ny1 = matmul(x, W) + b\ny2 = matmul(x, W) + c  # matmul(x, W) computed twice\n\n# AFTER: Compute once, reuse\nshared = matmul(x, W)\ny1 = shared + b\ny2 = shared + c\n```\n\n**Algebraic Simplification:**\n```python\n# x * 1.0 → x\n# x + 0.0 → x\n# x * 0.0 → zeros_like(x)\n# transpose(transpose(x)) → x\n# reshape(reshape(x, s1), s2) → reshape(x, s2)\n```\n\n### 4. Memory Optimization\n\n**Gradient Checkpointing:**\n```python\ndef gradient_checkpointing(model, layers, input_data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Trade compute for memory: recompute activations during backward pass\n    instead of storing them all.\n\n    Memory: O(sqrt(N)) instead of O(N) for N layers.\n    Compute: ~33% more (one extra forward pass per segment).\n    \"\"\"\n    # Divide model into segments\n    segment_size = int(len(layers) ** 0.5)\n    checkpoints = layers[::segment_size]  # Save every sqrt(N) activations\n\n    # Forward: only store checkpoint activations\n    # Backward: recompute intermediate activations from nearest checkpoint\n```\n\n**Activation Memory Reduction:**\n```python\n# In-place operations (modify tensor without allocation)\nx = relu_(x)        # In-place ReLU: no new tensor allocated\nx.add_(bias)        # In-place add\n\n# Memory-efficient attention (for transformers)\n# Standard: O(n^2) memory for attention matrix\n# Flash Attention: O(n) memory via tiled computation\n# Processes attention in blocks, never materializes full n×n matrix\n```\n\n**Peak memory estimation:**\n```\nTraining memory ≈ model_params + gradients + optimizer_state + activations\n                = P + P + 2P (Adam) + activation_memory\n                ≈ 4P + activations\n\nInference memory ≈ model_params + activations (no gradients/optimizer)\n                 ≈ P + peak_activation\n```\n\n### 5. Hardware-Aware Optimization\n\n**Understand the memory hierarchy:**\n```\nRegisters (fastest, smallest)\n  → L1 Cache (~32KB, ~1 cycle)\n    → L2 Cache (~256KB, ~10 cycles)\n      → L3 Cache (~32MB, ~40 cycles)\n        → DRAM (~128GB, ~200 cycles)\n          → SSD/NVMe (millions of cycles)\n```\n\n**Arithmetic Intensity = FLOPs / Bytes moved**\n- **Compute-bound** (high arithmetic intensity): Use faster compute (quantization, efficient kernels)\n- **Memory-bound** (low arithmetic intensity): Use fusion, tiling, data layout optimization\n\n**For Cohezion's AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395:**\n```python\n# Hardware specs to consider:\n# - 128 GiB unified LPDDR5X (high bandwidth)\n# - AVX-512 for vectorized operations\n# - Radeon 8060S iGPU shares memory (no PCIe transfer overhead)\n# - 32 threads for parallel data loading\n\n# Optimization priorities:\n# 1. Use AVX-512 vectorized ops (NumPy compiled with OpenBLAS/MKL)\n# 2. Leverage unified memory (no CPU→GPU copies needed)\n# 3. Use INT8 operations (natively supported by AVX-512 VNNI)\n# 4. Parallel data loading (32 threads available)\n```\n\n### 6. Benchmarking Methodology\n\n**Rigorous measurement protocol:**\n\n```python\ndef benchmark_model(model, input_data, warmup=50, iterations=200):\n    \"\"\"\n    Production-grade benchmarking.\n\n    TinyTorch reference: Module 19 (benchmarking)\n    \"\"\"\n    import time\n    import numpy as np\n\n    # Step 1: Warmup (JIT compilation, cache warming)\n    for _ in range(warmup):\n        model(input_data)\n\n    # Step 2: Timed iterations\n    latencies = []\n    for _ in range(iterations):\n        start = time.perf_counter_ns()\n        model(input_data)\n        end = time.perf_counter_ns()\n        latencies.append((end - start) / 1e6)  # Convert to ms\n\n    # Step 3: Statistical analysis\n    latencies = np.array(latencies)\n    return {\n        \"mean_ms\": np.mean(latencies),\n        \"median_ms\": np.median(latencies),\n        \"p50_ms\": np.percentile(latencies, 50),\n        \"p95_ms\": np.percentile(latencies, 95),\n        \"p99_ms\": np.percentile(latencies, 99),\n        \"std_ms\": np.std(latencies),\n        \"throughput_qps\": 1000 / np.mean(latencies),\n    }\n```\n\n**What to report:**\n- Always report **p99 latency** (not mean) for production systems\n- Report **throughput** (queries/sec) for batch workloads\n- Include **hardware specs** and software versions for reproducibility\n- Run multiple trials and report variance\n\n**Common benchmarking pitfalls:**\n- Not warming up (first runs include JIT/cache effects)\n- Using mean instead of percentiles (hides tail latency)\n- Benchmarking on different hardware than production\n- Not controlling for system load (background processes)\n\n### 7. Optimization Priority Matrix\n\n**Apply optimizations in this order (highest ROI first):**\n\n| Priority | Technique | Effort | Speedup | Risk |\n|----------|-----------|--------|---------|------|\n| 1 | FP16/BF16 mixed precision | Low | 2x | Very low |\n| 2 | Operator fusion (conv+bn+relu) | Low | 1.5-2x | Low |\n| 3 | INT8 post-training quantization | Medium | 2-4x | Low |\n| 4 | Structured pruning (50%) | Medium | 1.5-2x | Medium |\n| 5 | Graph optimization | Medium | 1.2-1.5x | Low |\n| 6 | Memory optimization | Medium | Memory only | Low |\n| 7 | INT4 quantization | High | 4-8x | High |\n| 8 | Custom kernels | Very high | Variable | High |\n\n### 8. Common Pitfalls\n\n**Pitfall 1: Optimizing without profiling**\n- Optimizing the wrong layer wastes effort\n- Always profile first to find the actual bottleneck\n\n**Pitfall 2: Benchmarking incorrectly**\n- Mean latency hides tail latency spikes\n- Always report p95/p99 for production systems\n\n**Pitfall 3: Ignoring accuracy regression**\n- Every optimization must include accuracy verification\n- Set an acceptable accuracy drop threshold before starting\n\n**Pitfall 4: Over-optimizing non-bottleneck operations**\n- Amdahl's Law: speedup limited by the slowest unoptimized component\n- Focus effort on the critical path\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME` - Quantization, pruning, distillation techniques\n- `DNN_ARCHITECTURES_PRIME` - Architecture selection and computational complexity\n- `ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME` - Production readiness and system lifecycle\n- `HARDWARE_PROFILE_PRIME` - AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 specifications\n- TinyTorch modules: `src/cohezion/tinytorch/{profiling,benchmarking}.py`\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**MLOps and Production ML Deployment**. Specializes in model serving, A/B testing, monitoring for drift, CI/CD for ML, on-device learning, privacy-preserving inference, and operational best practices for production ML systems.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Model Serving**: Deploying trained models to handle inference requests. Patterns: online (real-time), batch (scheduled), streaming (event-driven). Infrastructure: REST APIs, gRPC, model servers (TF Serving, Triton).\n- **A/B Testing for ML**: Compare model versions in production with statistical rigor. Shadow mode (log predictions without serving), canary deployment (gradual rollout), multi-armed bandits (adaptive allocation).\n- **Drift Monitoring**: Detect when production data or model behavior changes. Types: data drift (input distribution), concept drift (relationship changes), prediction drift (output distribution).\n- **On-Device Learning**: Training or fine-tuning models directly on edge devices. Techniques: federated learning, transfer learning on-device, personalization. Constraints: limited memory, no GPU, privacy requirements.\n- **CI/CD for ML**: Continuous integration and deployment adapted for ML. Includes: data validation, model training, model evaluation, model registry, automated deployment with rollback.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/ops]], [[cs249r/ondevice_learning]], [[cs249r/privacy_security]]\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Deployment Pattern Selection\n\n**Choose based on latency, throughput, and freshness requirements:**\n\n```\nInference Requirement?\n├─ Real-time (< 100ms)\n│   ├─ High throughput → Model server (Triton, TF Serving) with batching\n│   ├─ Low throughput → REST API with single model instance\n│   └─ Ultra-low latency → Edge deployment or in-process inference\n│\n├─ Batch (minutes to hours OK)\n│   ├─ Scheduled → Cron job with batch prediction pipeline\n│   ├─ On-demand → Serverless (Cloud Functions/Lambda)\n│   └─ Large scale → Spark/distributed batch processing\n│\n└─ Streaming (event-driven)\n    ├─ Message queue → Model behind Kafka/Pub-Sub consumer\n    └─ Real-time features → Feature store + online inference\n```\n\n### 2. Model Serving Architecture\n\n**Production serving patterns:**\n\n```python\nclass ModelServer:\n    \"\"\"Production model serving with versioning and fallback.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.models = {}         # version -> model\n        self.active_version = None\n        self.fallback_version = None\n\n    def load_model(self, version, model_path):\n        \"\"\"Load model version with validation.\"\"\"\n        model = load_and_validate(model_path)\n        # Validate: correct input/output shapes, reasonable predictions\n        self.models[version] = model\n\n    def predict(self, input_data):\n        \"\"\"Serve prediction with fallback.\"\"\"\n        try:\n            model = self.models[self.active_version]\n            prediction = model.predict(input_data)\n\n            # Validate prediction (sanity checks)\n            if not self.is_valid_prediction(prediction):\n                raise PredictionError(\"Invalid prediction output\")\n\n            return prediction\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.error(f\"Primary model failed: {e}\")\n            # Fallback to previous version\n            if self.fallback_version:\n                return self.models[self.fallback_version].predict(input_data)\n            raise\n\n    def is_valid_prediction(self, prediction):\n        \"\"\"Sanity check predictions before serving.\"\"\"\n        # Check for NaN, extreme values, wrong shape\n        if np.isnan(prediction).any():\n            return False\n        if prediction.max() > self.max_expected:\n            return False\n        return True\n```\n\n**Request batching for throughput:**\n```python\nclass BatchPredictor:\n    \"\"\"Accumulate requests and predict in batches for GPU efficiency.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, model, max_batch_size=32, max_wait_ms=10):\n        self.model = model\n        self.max_batch_size = max_batch_size\n        self.max_wait_ms = max_wait_ms\n        self.queue = []\n\n    async def predict(self, input_data):\n        \"\"\"Queue request and wait for batch prediction.\"\"\"\n        future = asyncio.Future()\n        self.queue.append((input_data, future))\n\n        if len(self.queue) >= self.max_batch_size:\n            await self._process_batch()\n\n        return await future\n\n    async def _process_batch(self):\n        \"\"\"Process accumulated requests as a single batch.\"\"\"\n        batch = self.queue[:self.max_batch_size]\n        self.queue = self.queue[self.max_batch_size:]\n\n        inputs = np.stack([item[0] for item in batch])\n        predictions = self.model.predict(inputs)\n\n        for (_, future), pred in zip(batch, predictions):\n            future.set_result(pred)\n```\n\n### 3. A/B Testing and Canary Deployment\n\n**Statistically rigorous model comparison:**\n\n```python\nclass ABTestManager:\n    \"\"\"Manage A/B tests between model versions.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, control_model, treatment_model, traffic_split=0.1):\n        self.control = control_model\n        self.treatment = treatment_model\n        self.traffic_split = traffic_split  # 10% to treatment\n        self.metrics = {\"control\": [], \"treatment\": []}\n\n    def route_request(self, request):\n        \"\"\"Route to control or treatment based on traffic split.\"\"\"\n        import hashlib\n        # Deterministic routing based on request ID (reproducible)\n        hash_val = int(hashlib.md5(request.id.encode()).hexdigest(), 16)\n        if (hash_val % 100) < (self.traffic_split * 100):\n            return self.treatment.predict(request), \"treatment\"\n        return self.control.predict(request), \"control\"\n\n    def evaluate(self):\n        \"\"\"Statistical test for significant difference.\"\"\"\n        from scipy.stats import mannwhitneyu\n\n        stat, p_value = mannwhitneyu(\n            self.metrics[\"control\"],\n            self.metrics[\"treatment\"],\n            alternative=\"two-sided\"\n        )\n\n        return {\n            \"p_value\": p_value,\n            \"significant\": p_value < 0.05,\n            \"control_mean\": np.mean(self.metrics[\"control\"]),\n            \"treatment_mean\": np.mean(self.metrics[\"treatment\"]),\n            \"recommendation\": \"deploy\" if p_value < 0.05 and\n                np.mean(self.metrics[\"treatment\"]) > np.mean(self.metrics[\"control\"])\n                else \"keep_control\"\n        }\n```\n\n**Canary deployment schedule:**\n```\nDay 1: 1% traffic → monitor for errors, latency regression\nDay 2: 5% traffic → compare accuracy metrics\nDay 3: 25% traffic → statistical significance check\nDay 4: 50% traffic → final validation\nDay 5: 100% traffic → full rollout (keep rollback ready)\n```\n\n### 4. Drift Monitoring\n\n**Comprehensive monitoring system:**\n\n```python\nclass DriftMonitor:\n    \"\"\"Monitor production model for drift and degradation.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, reference_stats):\n        self.reference = reference_stats\n\n    def check_data_drift(self, production_batch):\n        \"\"\"Detect if input distribution has shifted.\"\"\"\n        from scipy.stats import ks_2samp\n\n        alerts = []\n        for feature in self.reference.features:\n            stat, p_val = ks_2samp(\n                production_batch[feature],\n                self.reference.distributions[feature]\n            )\n            if p_val < 0.01:\n                alerts.append({\n                    \"feature\": feature,\n                    \"type\": \"data_drift\",\n                    \"severity\": \"high\" if p_val < 0.001 else \"medium\",\n                    \"p_value\": p_val\n                })\n        return alerts\n\n    def check_prediction_drift(self, recent_predictions):\n        \"\"\"Detect if model output distribution has changed.\"\"\"\n        # Population Stability Index (PSI)\n        expected = self.reference.prediction_distribution\n        actual = np.histogram(recent_predictions, bins=self.reference.bins)[0]\n        actual = actual / actual.sum()\n\n        psi = np.sum((actual - expected) * np.log(actual / expected + 1e-10))\n\n        if psi > 0.25:\n            return {\"type\": \"prediction_drift\", \"severity\": \"critical\", \"psi\": psi}\n        elif psi > 0.10:\n            return {\"type\": \"prediction_drift\", \"severity\": \"warning\", \"psi\": psi}\n        return None\n\n    def check_performance_decay(self, labeled_sample):\n        \"\"\"Check if accuracy has degraded (requires ground truth labels).\"\"\"\n        current_accuracy = self.evaluate(labeled_sample)\n        baseline_accuracy = self.reference.accuracy\n\n        if current_accuracy < baseline_accuracy * 0.95:  # >5% drop\n            return {\n                \"type\": \"performance_decay\",\n                \"severity\": \"critical\",\n                \"current\": current_accuracy,\n                \"baseline\": baseline_accuracy\n            }\n        return None\n```\n\n**Monitoring frequency:**\n\n| Check | Frequency | Action on Alert |\n|-------|-----------|-----------------|\n| Error rate, latency | Real-time | Page on-call if >2x baseline |\n| Input distribution | Hourly | Investigate; may need retraining |\n| Prediction distribution | Daily | Compare with A/B baseline |\n| Model performance | Weekly | Schedule retraining if declining |\n| Full drift analysis | Monthly | Comprehensive model review |\n\n### 5. CI/CD for ML\n\n**ML pipeline stages:**\n\n```\nData Validation\n  → Training\n    → Model Evaluation\n      → Model Registry\n        → Staging Deployment\n          → Production Deployment\n\n# Each stage has gates:\n# Data: schema checks, distribution checks, freshness\n# Training: convergence, no NaN losses, reasonable metrics\n# Evaluation: beats baseline, no fairness regressions\n# Registry: model card, lineage, artifact hash\n# Staging: integration tests, load tests\n# Production: canary deployment, rollback plan\n```\n\n**Automated retraining trigger:**\n```python\ndef should_retrain(drift_alerts, performance_metrics, model_age_days):\n    \"\"\"Decide if model needs retraining.\"\"\"\n    if any(a[\"severity\"] == \"critical\" for a in drift_alerts):\n        return True, \"Critical drift detected\"\n    if performance_metrics.accuracy < performance_metrics.baseline * 0.95:\n        return True, \"Performance below threshold\"\n    if model_age_days > 30:\n        return True, \"Scheduled retraining (>30 days)\"\n    return False, None\n```\n\n### 6. On-Device Learning\n\n**Federated learning pattern:**\n```python\nclass FederatedLearningServer:\n    \"\"\"Coordinate training across edge devices without sharing raw data.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, global_model):\n        self.global_model = global_model\n\n    def federated_round(self, participating_devices):\n        \"\"\"One round of federated averaging.\"\"\"\n        # 1. Send global model to selected devices\n        local_updates = []\n        for device in participating_devices:\n            # Device trains locally on its private data\n            update = device.train_local(self.global_model, epochs=5)\n            local_updates.append(update)\n\n        # 2. Aggregate updates (FedAvg)\n        # Weight by number of samples per device\n        total_samples = sum(u.num_samples for u in local_updates)\n        new_weights = {}\n        for key in self.global_model.weights:\n            new_weights[key] = sum(\n                u.weights[key] * (u.num_samples / total_samples)\n                for u in local_updates\n            )\n\n        # 3. Update global model\n        self.global_model.set_weights(new_weights)\n\n        # Privacy preserved: raw data never leaves devices\n```\n\n**On-device constraints:**\n- Memory: typically < 4GB RAM available\n- Compute: no GPU, limited CPU\n- Power: battery-powered devices\n- Network: intermittent connectivity\n\n### 7. Integration with Cohezion\n\n**Leverage Cohezion's existing infrastructure:**\n\n```python\nfrom cohezion.cache.semantic_cache import SemanticCache\nfrom cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker\n\n# Cache inference results for repeated queries\ncache = SemanticCache()\ncached_result = cache.get(query_embedding)\nif cached_result:\n    return cached_result  # 95%+ hit rate reduces compute\n\n# Track model serving in 12D universe\ntracker = JourneyTracker()\ntracker.record_transition(\n    before_state={\"model_version\": \"v2.1\", \"latency_p99\": 45},\n    action=\"serve_prediction\",\n    after_state={\"predictions_served\": count + 1},\n    coherence_after=0.91\n)\n```\n\n### 8. Common Anti-Patterns\n\n**Anti-Pattern 1: No rollback plan**\n- Always keep the previous model version loaded and ready\n- Test rollback procedure before deployment\n\n**Anti-Pattern 2: Monitoring only errors**\n- Error rate catches crashes, but not silent degradation\n- Must monitor drift, prediction distribution, and performance\n\n**Anti-Pattern 3: Manual deployment**\n- Use CI/CD pipelines for reproducible, auditable deployments\n- Manual steps = human error + no audit trail\n\n**Anti-Pattern 4: Ignoring data quality in production**\n- Production data is messier than training data\n- Validate inputs before prediction (schema, ranges, nulls)\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME` - ML system lifecycle and production readiness\n- `DATA_ENGINEERING_PRIME` - Data pipelines and quality monitoring\n- `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME` - Model compression for edge deployment\n- `EDGE_INTELLIGENCE_PRIME` - Federated learning and distributed inference\n- [[cs249r/ops]] - Full chapter on MLOps practices\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.REDACTED.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: RESPONSIBLE_AI_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Responsible and Trustworthy AI Systems**. Specializes in fairness metrics, bias detection and mitigation, explainability (SHAP, LIME), environmental impact assessment, carbon-aware scheduling, robustness testing, and safety alignment for production ML systems.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Algorithmic Fairness**: Ensuring ML systems don't discriminate against protected groups. Key metrics: demographic parity, equalized odds, equal opportunity, calibration. Trade-offs exist between different fairness definitions — they cannot all be satisfied simultaneously (impossibility theorem).\n- **Explainability (XAI)**: Making model decisions interpretable to humans. Model-agnostic methods: SHAP (game theory-based attribution), LIME (local linear approximation). Model-specific: Grad-CAM (CNN attention maps), attention weights (transformers).\n- **Sustainable AI / Green AI**: Measuring and reducing the environmental impact of ML training and inference. Metrics: carbon footprint (CO2e), energy consumption (kWh), compute efficiency (accuracy per FLOP). Carbon-aware scheduling shifts workloads to low-carbon periods.\n- **Robustness**: Model reliability under adversarial inputs, distribution shift, and noisy data. Adversarial examples expose vulnerabilities. Certification methods provide formal guarantees.\n- **AI Safety Alignment**: Ensuring AI systems act in accordance with human values and intentions. Connects to Cohezion's CONSTITUTION.md principles: honesty, safety, helpfulness.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/responsible_ai]], [[cs249r/sustainable_ai]], [[cs249r/robust_ai]], [[cs249r/ai_for_good]], [[cs249r/privacy_security]]\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Fairness Assessment Framework\n\n**Step-by-step fairness evaluation:**\n\n```python\nclass FairnessAuditor:\n    \"\"\"Audit ML model for fairness across protected attributes.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, protected_attributes):\n        self.protected_attrs = protected_attributes\n\n    def demographic_parity(self, predictions, groups):\n        \"\"\"\n        Equal positive prediction rates across groups.\n        P(Y_hat=1 | A=a) = P(Y_hat=1 | A=b) for all groups a, b.\n        \"\"\"\n        rates = {}\n        for group in set(groups):\n            mask = groups == group\n            rates[group] = predictions[mask].mean()\n\n        max_diff = max(rates.values()) - min(rates.values())\n        return {\n            \"metric\": \"demographic_parity\",\n            \"rates\": rates,\n            \"max_disparity\": max_diff,\n            \"passes\": max_diff < 0.1  # 10% threshold (common standard)\n        }\n\n    def equalized_odds(self, predictions, labels, groups):\n        \"\"\"\n        Equal TPR and FPR across groups.\n        More stringent than demographic parity.\n        \"\"\"\n        results = {}\n        for group in set(groups):\n            mask = groups == group\n            tp = ((predictions[mask] == 1) & (labels[mask] == 1)).sum()\n            fp = ((predictions[mask] == 1) & (labels[mask] == 0)).sum()\n            fn = ((predictions[mask] == 0) & (labels[mask] == 1)).sum()\n            tn = ((predictions[mask] == 0) & (labels[mask] == 0)).sum()\n\n            results[group] = {\n                \"tpr\": tp / (tp + fn) if (tp + fn) > 0 else 0,\n                \"fpr\": fp / (fp + tn) if (fp + tn) > 0 else 0,\n            }\n        return results\n\n    def calibration(self, predictions, probabilities, labels, groups):\n        \"\"\"\n        When model says 80% confident, is it right 80% of the time?\n        Check calibration per group.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Bin predictions by confidence\n        bins = np.linspace(0, 1, 11)\n        calibration_per_group = {}\n\n        for group in set(groups):\n            mask = groups == group\n            group_probs = probabilities[mask]\n            group_labels = labels[mask]\n\n            bin_accs = []\n            for i in range(len(bins) - 1):\n                bin_mask = (group_probs >= bins[i]) & (group_probs < bins[i + 1])\n                if bin_mask.sum() > 0:\n                    bin_accs.append(group_labels[bin_mask].mean())\n\n            calibration_per_group[group] = bin_accs\n\n        return calibration_per_group\n```\n\n**Fairness metric selection guide:**\n\n| Metric | Use When | Trade-off |\n|--------|----------|-----------|\n| Demographic Parity | Equal outcomes desired (hiring) | May sacrifice accuracy |\n| Equalized Odds | Equal accuracy across groups | Harder to achieve |\n| Equal Opportunity | Focus on positive class (loans) | Allows FPR differences |\n| Calibration | Probabilistic predictions matter | Doesn't guarantee equal rates |\n\n### 2. Bias Detection and Mitigation\n\n**Three intervention points:**\n\n```python\n# 1. PRE-PROCESSING: Fix data before training\ndef reweight_training_data(data, labels, groups):\n    \"\"\"Assign weights to equalize representation.\"\"\"\n    weights = np.ones(len(data))\n    for group in set(groups):\n        mask = groups == group\n        for label in set(labels):\n            label_mask = labels == label\n            group_label_mask = mask & label_mask\n            # Weight inversely proportional to group-label frequency\n            expected = len(data) / (len(set(groups)) * len(set(labels)))\n            actual = group_label_mask.sum()\n            if actual > 0:\n                weights[group_label_mask] = expected / actual\n    return weights\n\n# 2. IN-PROCESSING: Constrain model during training\ndef fairness_regularized_loss(predictions, labels, groups, lambda_fair=0.1):\n    \"\"\"Add fairness penalty to training loss.\"\"\"\n    task_loss = cross_entropy(predictions, labels)\n    fairness_penalty = demographic_parity_difference(predictions, groups)\n    return task_loss + lambda_fair * fairness_penalty\n\n# 3. POST-PROCESSING: Adjust predictions after training\ndef equalize_odds_postprocess(predictions, groups, thresholds):\n    \"\"\"Apply group-specific thresholds to equalize TPR/FPR.\"\"\"\n    adjusted = predictions.copy()\n    for group in set(groups):\n        mask = groups == group\n        adjusted[mask] = (predictions[mask] > thresholds[group]).astype(int)\n    return adjusted\n```\n\n### 3. Explainability Methods\n\n**SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations):**\n```python\ndef explain_prediction_shap(model, instance, background_data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Attribute prediction to each feature using Shapley values.\n\n    Shapley value = average marginal contribution of feature\n    across all possible feature coalitions.\n    \"\"\"\n    # For each feature:\n    #   For each subset of other features:\n    #     Measure change in prediction when this feature is added\n    #   Average across all subsets (weighted by coalition size)\n\n    # Practical: Use sampling-based approximation (exact is O(2^n))\n    n_features = instance.shape[0]\n    shap_values = np.zeros(n_features)\n\n    for _ in range(n_samples):\n        # Random permutation of features\n        perm = np.random.permutation(n_features)\n        # Add features one at a time, measure marginal contribution\n        for i, feature_idx in enumerate(perm):\n            x_with = background_sample.copy()\n            x_with[perm[:i + 1]] = instance[perm[:i + 1]]\n            x_without = background_sample.copy()\n            x_without[perm[:i]] = instance[perm[:i]]\n\n            shap_values[feature_idx] += model(x_with) - model(x_without)\n\n    return shap_values / n_samples\n```\n\n**LIME (Local Interpretable Model-agnostic Explanations):**\n```python\ndef explain_prediction_lime(model, instance, n_samples=1000):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fit a local linear model around the prediction point.\n\n    1. Perturb the instance randomly\n    2. Get model predictions for perturbations\n    3. Weight by proximity to original instance\n    4. Fit weighted linear model → coefficients = feature importance\n    \"\"\"\n    perturbations = generate_perturbations(instance, n_samples)\n    predictions = model.predict(perturbations)\n    weights = proximity_weights(instance, perturbations)\n\n    # Weighted linear regression\n    linear_model = LinearRegression()\n    linear_model.fit(perturbations, predictions, sample_weight=weights)\n\n    return {\n        \"feature_importance\": linear_model.coef_,\n        \"local_accuracy\": linear_model.score(perturbations, predictions, sample_weight=weights)\n    }\n```\n\n### 4. Environmental Impact Assessment\n\n**Carbon footprint estimation:**\n```python\nclass CarbonTracker:\n    \"\"\"Track carbon emissions from ML training and inference.\"\"\"\n\n    # Average carbon intensity by region (gCO2/kWh)\n    CARBON_INTENSITY = {\n        \"us_west\": 190,      # California (mix of solar/wind/gas)\n        \"us_east\": 380,      # Virginia (more fossil fuels)\n        \"europe_north\": 30,  # Norway/Sweden (hydroelectric)\n        \"europe_west\": 250,  # Germany/France (nuclear + renewables)\n        \"asia_east\": 550,    # China (coal-heavy)\n    }\n\n    def estimate_training_carbon(self, gpu_hours, gpu_type, region):\n        \"\"\"\n        Estimate CO2 emissions from training.\n\n        CO2 = Energy × Carbon Intensity\n        Energy = GPU Power × Hours × PUE\n        \"\"\"\n        gpu_power_watts = {\"A100\": 400, \"V100\": 300, \"T4\": 70, \"CPU\": 150}\n        pue = 1.1  # Typical datacenter Power Usage Effectiveness\n\n        energy_kwh = (gpu_power_watts[gpu_type] * gpu_hours * pue) / 1000\n        carbon_g = energy_kwh * self.CARBON_INTENSITY[region]\n\n        return {\n            \"energy_kwh\": energy_kwh,\n            \"carbon_g_co2\": carbon_g,\n            \"equivalent_km_driven\": carbon_g / 120,  # Average car: 120g/km\n        }\n\n    def carbon_aware_schedule(self, job, available_regions):\n        \"\"\"Schedule training job in lowest-carbon region.\"\"\"\n        costs = []\n        for region in available_regions:\n            carbon = self.estimate_training_carbon(\n                job.estimated_gpu_hours,\n                job.gpu_type,\n                region\n            )\n            costs.append((region, carbon[\"carbon_g_co2\"]))\n\n        return min(costs, key=lambda x: x[1])  # Lowest carbon region\n```\n\n**Green AI practices:**\n- Report accuracy **per FLOP** (not just accuracy)\n- Use smaller models first; scale up only if needed\n- Transfer learning and fine-tuning over training from scratch\n- Early stopping to avoid wasted compute\n- Prefer efficient architectures (MobileNet over ResNet for equivalent tasks)\n\n### 5. Robustness Testing\n\n**Adversarial robustness evaluation:**\n```python\ndef fgsm_attack(model, input_data, label, epsilon=0.01):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fast Gradient Sign Method: minimal perturbation to flip prediction.\n\n    Perturbation = epsilon * sign(gradient of loss w.r.t. input)\n    \"\"\"\n    input_data.requires_grad = True\n    loss = cross_entropy(model(input_data), label)\n    loss.backward()\n\n    # Perturb in direction that increases loss\n    perturbation = epsilon * np.sign(input_data.grad)\n    adversarial = input_data + perturbation\n\n    # Clip to valid range\n    adversarial = np.clip(adversarial, 0, 1)\n\n    return adversarial\n\ndef robustness_report(model, test_data, test_labels, epsilons=[0.01, 0.05, 0.1]):\n    \"\"\"Generate robustness report across perturbation strengths.\"\"\"\n    results = {\"clean_accuracy\": accuracy(model, test_data, test_labels)}\n\n    for eps in epsilons:\n        adversarial_data = [fgsm_attack(model, x, y, eps) for x, y in zip(test_data, test_labels)]\n        results[f\"accuracy_eps_{eps}\"] = accuracy(model, adversarial_data, test_labels)\n\n    return results\n```\n\n### 6. Safety Alignment Principles\n\n**Connection to Cohezion's CONSTITUTION.md:**\n\n```python\n# Cohezion's Constitution mandates:\n# 1. Honesty - Report true capabilities and limitations\n# 2. Safety - Never generate harmful outputs\n# 3. Helpfulness - Maximize user value within constraints\n\nclass SafetyGuard:\n    \"\"\"Pre-deployment safety checks aligned with CONSTITUTION.md.\"\"\"\n\n    def check_output_safety(self, model_output):\n        \"\"\"Verify model output meets safety constraints.\"\"\"\n        checks = [\n            self.no_harmful_content(model_output),\n            self.no_private_data_leakage(model_output),\n            self.confidence_calibrated(model_output),\n            self.uncertainty_communicated(model_output),\n        ]\n        return all(checks)\n\n    def confidence_calibrated(self, output):\n        \"\"\"Honesty principle: model confidence matches actual accuracy.\"\"\"\n        # Overconfident models violate honesty\n        # Underconfident models reduce helpfulness\n        return abs(output.confidence - output.empirical_accuracy) < 0.1\n\n    def uncertainty_communicated(self, output):\n        \"\"\"Honesty principle: communicate when model is uncertain.\"\"\"\n        if output.confidence < 0.7:\n            return hasattr(output, 'uncertainty_message')\n        return True\n```\n\n### 7. Responsible AI Checklist\n\n**Before deploying ANY model, verify:**\n\n- [ ] **Fairness**: Tested on all protected groups with documented metrics\n- [ ] **Explainability**: Key predictions can be explained to stakeholders\n- [ ] **Robustness**: Tested against adversarial inputs at relevant epsilon levels\n- [ ] **Privacy**: No personal data leakage, differential privacy if needed\n- [ ] **Sustainability**: Carbon footprint estimated and minimized where possible\n- [ ] **Safety**: Output safety checks pass, harmful outputs impossible\n- [ ] **Documentation**: Model card with intended use, limitations, and failure modes\n- [ ] **Monitoring**: Fairness metrics tracked in production, not just at launch\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `ML_SYSTEMS_FOUNDATIONS_PRIME` - ML system lifecycle and production readiness\n- `MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME` - Production monitoring and drift detection\n- `EDGE_INTELLIGENCE_PRIME` - Privacy-preserving on-device learning\n- `.agent/CONSTITUTION.md` - Cohezion's core safety and honesty principles\n- [[cs249r/responsible_ai]] - Full chapter on responsible AI practices\n- [[cs249r/sustainable_ai]] - Environmental impact of AI systems\n"
}
```

```json
{
```
