  "content": "# SKILL: EDGE_INTELLIGENCE_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\n**Edge Intelligence and Distributed ML**. Specializes in federated learning, split computing, communication-efficient training, fault-tolerant distributed systems, on-device inference, and edge-cloud collaboration for resource-constrained environments.\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n- **Federated Learning**: Train models collaboratively across devices without sharing raw data. Server aggregates model updates (FedAvg, FedProx). Privacy by design — data never leaves the device.\n- **Split Computing**: Partition a model between edge device and cloud. Early layers run on-device (feature extraction), later layers in cloud (classification). Reduces latency and bandwidth.\n- **Communication-Efficient Training**: Minimize data transferred during distributed training. Techniques: gradient compression, quantized gradients, sparse updates, local SGD (multiple local steps before sync).\n- **Edge-Cloud Collaboration**: Dynamic workload splitting between edge and cloud based on network conditions, device capabilities, and latency requirements. Adaptive inference routing.\n- **TinyML**: ML inference on microcontrollers (MCUs) with kilobytes of memory. Requires extreme model compression, fixed-point arithmetic, and hardware-aware design.\n\n**Related Vault Concepts**: [[cs249r/ondevice_learning]], [[cs249r/privacy_security]], [[cs249r/hw_acceleration]]\n\n## INSTRUCTION\n\n### 1. Edge Deployment Decision Framework\n\n**Choose the right edge strategy:**\n\n```\nWhere does inference run?\n├─ Fully On-Device\n│   ├─ Privacy critical (medical, personal data) → Federated Learning\n│   ├─ No network available → Standalone edge model\n│   └─ Ultra-low latency (<10ms) → Quantized on-device model\n│\n├─ Split Computing (Edge + Cloud)\n│   ├─ Large model, good network → Early exit on-device, full model in cloud\n│   ├─ Variable network → Adaptive splitting based on bandwidth\n│   └─ Bandwidth limited → Feature extraction on-device, cloud classification\n│\n└─ Cloud with Edge Cache\n    ├─ Latency tolerant → Cloud inference with edge caching\n    └─ Personalization needed → Cloud base model + edge fine-tuning\n```\n\n### 2. Federated Learning Patterns\n\n**Federated Averaging (FedAvg):**\n```python\nclass FederatedServer:\n    \"\"\"Coordinate federated learning across edge devices.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, global_model, min_clients=10):\n        self.global_model = global_model\n        self.min_clients = min_clients\n        self.round_number = 0\n\n    def run_round(self, available_clients):\n        \"\"\"Execute one round of federated averaging.\"\"\"\n        # 1. Select subset of clients (don't require all devices)\n        selected = self.select_clients(available_clients)\n        if len(selected) < self.min_clients:\n            return None  # Not enough clients\n\n        # 2. Send global model weights to selected clients\n        global_weights = self.global_model.get_weights()\n\n        # 3. Clients train locally and send back updates\n        client_updates = []\n        for client in selected:\n            update = client.train_local(\n                global_weights,\n                local_epochs=5,     # Multiple local epochs (efficiency)\n                learning_rate=0.01\n            )\n            client_updates.append(update)\n\n        # 4. Aggregate using weighted average\n        self.aggregate_updates(client_updates)\n        self.round_number += 1\n\n    def aggregate_updates(self, updates):\n        \"\"\"FedAvg: weighted average by number of samples.\"\"\"\n        total_samples = sum(u.num_samples for u in updates)\n        new_weights = {}\n\n        for key in self.global_model.get_weights():\n            new_weights[key] = sum(\n                u.weights[key] * (u.num_samples / total_samples)\n                for u in updates\n            )\n\n        self.global_model.set_weights(new_weights)\n\n    def select_clients(self, available, fraction=0.1):\n        \"\"\"Select random subset of available clients.\"\"\"\n        n_select = max(self.min_clients, int(len(available) * fraction))\n        return random.sample(available, min(n_select, len(available)))\n```\n\n**FedProx (handles heterogeneous devices):**\n```python\ndef fedprox_local_loss(local_model, global_weights, mu=0.01):\n    \"\"\"\n    Add proximal term to prevent client drift.\n    Handles non-IID data better than FedAvg.\n\n    Loss = task_loss + (mu/2) * ||local_weights - global_weights||^2\n    \"\"\"\n    task_loss = compute_task_loss(local_model)\n    proximal_term = sum(\n        np.sum((local - global_) ** 2)\n        for local, global_ in zip(local_model.get_weights().values(),\n                                   global_weights.values())\n    )\n    return task_loss + (mu / 2) * proximal_term\n```\n\n### 3. Communication-Efficient Training\n\n**Gradient Compression:**\n```python\ndef top_k_sparsification(gradients, k_ratio=0.01):\n    \"\"\"\n    Send only top-k% of gradient values.\n    Reduces communication by 100x with minimal accuracy loss.\n    \"\"\"\n    flat = gradients.flatten()\n    k = max(1, int(len(flat) * k_ratio))\n    top_k_indices = np.argpartition(np.abs(flat), -k)[-k:]\n    top_k_values = flat[top_k_indices]\n\n    # Accumulate residual (error feedback) for next round\n    residual = gradients.copy()\n    sparse_grad = np.zeros_like(flat)\n    sparse_grad[top_k_indices] = top_k_values\n    residual -= sparse_grad.reshape(gradients.shape)\n\n    return top_k_indices, top_k_values, residual\n\ndef quantize_gradients(gradients, num_bits=2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Quantize gradients to low-bit representation.\n    2-bit: send only sign + magnitude bucket.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sign quantization (1-bit: positive or negative)\n    signs = np.sign(gradients)\n\n    # Magnitude quantization\n    abs_grad = np.abs(gradients)\n    threshold = np.mean(abs_grad)\n\n    # 2-bit: {0, +threshold, -threshold, +2*threshold}\n    quantized = signs * np.where(abs_grad > threshold, threshold, 0)\n\n    return quantized\n```\n\n**Communication budget comparison:**\n\n| Technique | Compression Ratio | Accuracy Impact |\n|-----------|------------------|-----------------|\n| No compression | 1x | Baseline |\n| FP16 gradients | 2x | ~0% |\n| Top-1% sparsification | 100x | <0.5% |\n| 2-bit quantization | 16x | <1% |\n| Local SGD (H=10 steps) | 10x | <0.5% |\n| Combined (sparse + quantized) | 1000x+ | 1-3% |\n\n### 4. Split Computing Architecture\n\n**Dynamic model splitting:**\n```python\nclass SplitComputeManager:\n    \"\"\"\n    Split model execution between edge and cloud.\n    Adapt split point based on network conditions.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, model, split_points):\n        self.model = model\n        self.split_points = split_points  # Candidate layer indices\n\n    def choose_split_point(self, network_bandwidth_mbps, device_compute_gflops):\n        \"\"\"\n        Optimal split minimizes total latency:\n        Total = edge_compute + transfer + cloud_compute\n        \"\"\"\n        best_split = None\n        best_latency = float('inf')\n\n        for split_idx in self.split_points:\n            # Edge computation time\n            edge_flops = self.model.flops_before(split_idx)\n            edge_time = edge_flops / (device_compute_gflops * 1e9)\n\n            # Transfer time\n            intermediate_size = self.model.output_size_at(split_idx)\n            transfer_time = intermediate_size / (network_bandwidth_mbps * 1e6 / 8)\n\n            # Cloud computation time\n            cloud_flops = self.model.flops_after(split_idx)\n            cloud_time = cloud_flops / (100 * 1e9)  # Assume 100 GFLOPS cloud\n\n            total = edge_time + transfer_time + cloud_time\n\n            if total < best_latency:\n                best_latency = total\n                best_split = split_idx\n\n        return best_split\n\n    def infer(self, input_data, split_point):\n        \"\"\"Execute split inference.\"\"\"\n        # Run early layers on edge\n        intermediate = self.model.forward_to(input_data, split_point)\n\n        # Transfer intermediate representation\n        compressed = self.compress_intermediate(intermediate)\n\n        # Run remaining layers on cloud\n        result = self.model.forward_from(compressed, split_point)\n\n        return result\n```\n\n### 5. TinyML Patterns\n\n**Microcontroller deployment constraints:**\n\n| Resource | Typical MCU | What It Means |\n|----------|------------|---------------|\n| RAM | 256 KB | Model + activations must fit |\n| Flash | 1 MB | Model weights storage |\n| Clock | 100 MHz | Limited compute throughput |\n| Power | 1-10 mW | Battery/energy harvesting |\n\n**Fixed-point inference for MCU:**\n```python\ndef fixed_point_inference(weights_int8, input_int8, scale_w, scale_x, scale_y):\n    \"\"\"\n    INT8 inference without floating point hardware.\n    Output = (Input * Weights) * (scale_x * scale_w / scale_y)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Integer matrix multiply (no FPU needed)\n    accumulator_int32 = np.dot(input_int8.astype(np.int32),\n                                weights_int8.astype(np.int32))\n\n    # Rescale to output range\n    multiplier = (scale_x * scale_w) / scale_y\n    # Approximate multiplier as fixed-point: M * 2^(-shift)\n    shift = 15\n    fixed_multiplier = int(multiplier * (1 << shift))\n\n    output_int8 = (accumulator_int32 * fixed_multiplier) >> shift\n    return output_int8.clip(-128, 127).astype(np.int8)\n```\n\n### 6. Fault Tolerance in Distributed Training\n\n**Handle device failures gracefully:**\n```python\nclass FaultTolerantTrainer:\n    \"\"\"Distributed training with fault tolerance.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, model, num_workers):\n        self.model = model\n        self.num_workers = num_workers\n        self.checkpoints = {}\n\n    def train_step_with_recovery(self, data_shards):\n        \"\"\"Training step that tolerates worker failures.\"\"\"\n        gradients = []\n        failed_workers = []\n\n        for worker_id, shard in enumerate(data_shards):\n            try:\n                grad = self.compute_gradient(worker_id, shard, timeout=30)\n                gradients.append(grad)\n            except (TimeoutError, ConnectionError) as e:\n                failed_workers.append(worker_id)\n                # Use stale gradient from checkpoint if available\n                if worker_id in self.checkpoints:\n                    gradients.append(self.checkpoints[worker_id])\n\n        if len(gradients) < self.num_workers * 0.5:\n            raise TrainingError(\"Too many worker failures (>50%)\")\n\n        # Average available gradients (robust to missing workers)\n        avg_gradient = np.mean(gradients, axis=0)\n        self.model.update(avg_gradient)\n\n        # Checkpoint successful gradients\n        for worker_id, grad in zip(range(len(gradients)), gradients):\n            if worker_id not in failed_workers:\n                self.checkpoints[worker_id] = grad\n```\n\n### 7. Integration with Cohezion's Local-First Architecture\n\n**Cohezion runs on AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395 with local Ollama models:**\n\n```python\n# Cohezion's local-first approach aligns with edge intelligence:\n# - Ollama models run locally (no cloud dependency)\n# - 128 GiB unified memory enables large local models\n# - SemanticCache provides inference caching (95%+ hit rate)\n# - Cost savings from local inference vs API calls\n\n# Edge intelligence patterns applicable to Cohezion:\n# 1. Model selection based on query complexity\n#    (small model for simple queries, large for complex)\n# 2. Caching as a form of edge intelligence\n#    (semantic cache = learned index of common queries)\n# 3. Federated knowledge aggregation\n#    (multiple Cohezion instances could share learned skills)\n```\n\n### 8. Common Pitfalls\n\n**Pitfall 1: Assuming IID data in federated learning**\n- Real-world devices have non-IID data distributions\n- Use FedProx or FedBN to handle heterogeneity\n\n**Pitfall 2: Ignoring communication costs**\n- Network transfers dominate training time in distributed settings\n- Always compress gradients and minimize sync frequency\n\n**Pitfall 3: Not testing on actual edge hardware**\n- Emulators miss real-world constraints (thermal throttling, memory fragmentation)\n- Profile on target hardware before deployment\n\n**Pitfall 4: Synchronous training with heterogeneous devices**\n- Slow devices bottleneck synchronous training\n- Use asynchronous SGD or tolerate stragglers\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `EFFICIENT_AI_PRIME` - Model compression for edge deployment\n- `MLOPS_DEPLOYMENT_PRIME` - Production deployment and on-device learning\n- `MODEL_OPTIMIZATION_PRIME` - Hardware-aware optimization techniques\n- `HARDWARE_PROFILE_PRIME` - Cohezion's AMD hardware specifications\n- [[cs249r/ondevice_learning]] - On-device learning chapter concepts\n"
}

    94→# %% [markdown]
    95→"""
    96→## 💡 Introduction: What is a Tensor?
    97→
    98→A tensor is a multi-dimensional array that serves as the fundamental data structure in machine learning. Think of it as a universal container that can hold data in different dimensions:
    99→
   100→```
   101→Tensor Dimensions:
   102→┌─────────────┐
   103→│ 0D: Scalar  │  5.0          (just a number)
   104→│ 1D: Vector  │  [1, 2, 3]    (list of numbers)
   105→│ 2D: Matrix  │  [[1, 2]      (grid of numbers)
   106→│             │   [3, 4]]
   107→│ 3D: Cube    │  [[[...       (stack of matrices)
   108→└─────────────┘
   109→```
   110→
   111→In computation, tensors flow through operations like water through pipes:
   112→
   113→```
   114→Data Processing Flow:
   115→Input Data → Transform 1 → Transform 2 → ... → Result
   116→  [rows,       [rows,        [rows,              [rows,
   117→   columns]     new_cols]     new_cols2]           final_cols]
   118→```
   119→
   120→From simple statistics to large-scale scientific computing, tensors are the universal data container. Understanding tensors means understanding the foundation of numerical computation.
   121→
   122→### Why Tensors Matter in ML Systems
   123→
   124→In production ML systems, tensors carry more than just data - they carry operation history, memory layout information, and execution context:
   125→
   126→```
   127→Real ML Pipeline:
   128→Raw Data → Preprocessing → Tensor Creation → Computation → Result
   129→   ↓           ↓              ↓               ↓              ↓
   130→ Files     NumPy Arrays    Tensors        GPU Tensors     Output Tensor
   131→```
   132→
   133→**Key Insight**: Tensors bridge the gap between mathematical concepts and efficient computation on modern hardware.
   134→"""
   135→
   136→# %% [markdown]
   137→"""
   138→## 📐 Foundations: Mathematical Background
   139→
   140→### Core Operations We'll Implement
   141→
   142→Our Tensor class will support all fundamental operations that neural networks need:
   143→
   144→```
   145→Operation Types:
   146→┌─────────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────┐
   147→│ Element-wise    │ Matrix Ops      │ Shape Ops       │
   148→├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┤
   149→│ + Addition      │ @ Matrix Mult   │ .reshape()      │
   150→│ - Subtraction   │ .transpose()    │ .sum()          │
   151→│ * Multiplication│                 │ .mean()         │
   152→│ / Division      │                 │ .max()          │
   153→└─────────────────┴─────────────────┴─────────────────┘
   154→```
   155→
   156→### Broadcasting: Making Tensors Work Together
   157→
   158→Broadcasting automatically aligns tensors of different shapes for operations:
   159→
   160→```
   161→Broadcasting Examples:
   162→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   163→│ Scalar + Vector:                                        │
   164→│    5    + [1, 2, 3] → [5, 5, 5] + [1, 2, 3] = [6, 7, 8] │
   165→│                                                         │
   166→│ Matrix + Vector (row-wise):                             │
   167→│ [[1, 2]]   [10]   [[1, 2]]   [[10, 10]]   [[11, 12]]    │
   168→│ [[3, 4]] + [10] = [[3, 4]] + [[10, 10]] = [[13, 14]]    │
   169→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   170→```
   171→
   172→**Memory Layout**: NumPy uses row-major (C-style) storage where elements are stored row by row in memory for cache efficiency:
   173→
   174→```
   175→Memory Layout (2×3 matrix):
   176→Matrix:     Memory:
   177→[[1, 2, 3]  [1][2][3][4][5][6]
   178→ [4, 5, 6]]  ↑  Row 1   ↑  Row 2
   179→

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/scripts/cs249r/extract_tinytorch.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Extract code cells from TinyTorch Jupytext files into clean Python modules.\n\nReads Jupytext percent-format .py files and extracts only the code cells,\nskipping markdown cells, nbgrader metadata, and jupytext headers.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n\nTINYTORCH_SRC = Path.home() / \"dev\" / \"cs249r_book\" / \"tinytorch\" / \"src\"\n\n# Map module number to (source_dir, output_name)\nCORE_MODULES = {\n    \"01\": (\"01_tensor\", \"tensor\"),\n    \"02\": (\"02_activations\", \"activations\"),\n    \"03\": (\"03_layers\", \"layers\"),\n    \"04\": (\"04_losses\", \"losses\"),\n    \"05\": (\"05_dataloader\", \"dataloader\"),\n    \"06\": (\"06_autograd\", \"autograd\"),\n    \"07\": (\"07_optimizers\", \"optimizers\"),\n    \"08\": (\"08_training\", \"training\"),\n    \"09\": (\"09_convolutions\", \"convolutions\"),\n}\n\nADVANCED_MODULES = {\n    \"10\": (\"10_tokenization\", \"tokenization\"),\n    \"11\": (\"11_embeddings\", \"embeddings\"),\n    \"12\": (\"12_attention\", \"attention\"),\n    \"13\": (\"13_transformers\", \"transformers\"),\n    \"14\": (\"14_profiling\", \"profiling\"),\n    \"15\": (\"15_quantization\", \"quantization\"),\n    \"16\": (\"16_compression\", \"compression\"),\n    \"17\": (\"17_acceleration\", \"acceleration\"),\n    \"18\": (\"18_memoization\", \"memoization\"),\n    \"19\": (\"19_benchmarking\", \"benchmarking\"),\n}\n\n\ndef extract_code_cells(source_path: Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract code cells from a Jupytext percent-format file.\n\n    Returns list of code blocks (strings), skipping:\n    - Jupytext header (# --- block)\n    - Markdown cells (# %% [markdown])\n    - nbgrader directives (#| lines)\n    - Inline test/exercise blocks\n    \"\"\"\n    lines = source_path.read_text().splitlines()\n    code_blocks: list[str] = []\n    current_block: list[str] = []\n    in_code = False\n    in_markdown = False\n    in_header = False\n    in_docstring = False\n\n    for line in lines:\n        # Skip jupytext header\n        if line.strip() == \"# ---\":\n            in_header = not in_header\n            continue\n        if in_header:\n            continue\n\n        # Cell boundary\n        if line.startswith(\"# %%\"):\n            # Save previous code block\n            if in_code and current_block:\n                code_blocks.append(\"\\n\".join(current_block))\n                current_block = []\n\n            if \"[markdown]\" in line:\n                in_code = False\n                in_markdown = True\n            else:\n                in_code = True\n                in_markdown = False\n            continue\n\n        if in_markdown:\n            continue\n\n        if not in_code:\n            continue\n\n        # Skip nbgrader/export directives\n        if line.startswith(\"#|\"):\n            continue\n\n        # Track docstrings in markdown cells that leak into code cells\n        stripped = line.strip()\n        if stripped == '\"\"\"' or stripped == \"'''\":\n            in_docstring = not in_docstring\n            # If this is a standalone docstring delimiter in a code cell,\n            # check if the previous/next line suggests it's a markdown block\n            if not current_block and not in_docstring:\n                continue  # Skip closing of empty docstring\n            if in_docstring and not current_block:\n                # Opening of docstring at start of code cell - likely markdown\n                in_docstring = True\n                continue\n\n        if in_docstring:\n            continue\n\n        current_block.append(line)\n\n    # Don't forget the last block\n    if in_code and current_block:\n        code_blocks.append(\"\\n\".join(current_block))\n\n    return code_blocks\n\n\ndef clean_code_block(block: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Clean a code block: remove test assertions, print demos, etc.\"\"\"\n    lines = block.splitlines()\n    cleaned = []\n    skip_block = False\n\n    for line in lines:\n        stripped = line.strip()\n\n        # Skip standalone test/demo blocks\n        if stripped.startswith(\"# Test\") or stripped.startswith(\"# Quick test\"):\n            skip_block = True\n            continue\n        if stripped.startswith(\"# Demo\") or stripped.startswith(\"# Let's\"):\n            skip_block = True\n            continue\n        if stripped.startswith(\"print(\") and \"test\" in stripped.lower():\n            continue\n\n        # Resume on next function/class definition\n        if skip_block and (stripped.startswith(\"def \") or stripped.startswith(\"class \")):\n            skip_block = False\n\n        if skip_block:\n            # Keep blank lines (could be between test blocks)\n            if not stripped:\n                continue\n            # Skip assertion lines\n            if stripped.startswith(\"assert \") or stripped.startswith(\"print(\"):\n                continue\n            # Non-test code = end of skip\n            if not stripped.startswith(\"#\"):\n                skip_block = False\n\n        if not skip_block:\n            cleaned.append(line)\n\n    return \"\\n\".join(cleaned)\n\n\ndef rewrite_imports(code: str, module_name: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Rewrite tinytorch imports to use cohezion.tinytorch namespace.\"\"\"\n    # Replace tinytorch.core.X imports\n    code = re.sub(\n        r\"from tinytorch\\.core\\.(\\w+)\",\n        r\"from cohezion.tinytorch.\\1\",\n        code,\n    )\n    code = re.sub(\n        r\"from tinytorch\\.perf\\.(\\w+)\",\n        r\"from cohezion.tinytorch.\\1\",\n        code,\n    )\n    code = re.sub(\n        r\"import tinytorch\\.core\\.(\\w+)\",\n        r\"import cohezion.tinytorch.\\1\",\n        code,\n    )\n    return code\n\n\ndef build_module(\n    module_num: str,\n    source_dir: str,\n    output_name: str,\n    output_base: Path,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Extract and build a single TinyTorch module.\n\n    Returns dict with stats about the extraction.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Find the source file\n    src_dir = TINYTORCH_SRC / source_dir\n    src_files = list(src_dir.glob(\"*.py\"))\n    if not src_files:\n        return {\"module\": output_name, \"status\": \"no_source\", \"lines\": 0}\n\n    src_file = src_files[0]\n\n    # Extract code cells\n    blocks = extract_code_cells(src_file)\n\n    # Clean and combine\n    cleaned_blocks = []\n    for block in blocks:\n        cleaned = clean_code_block(block)\n        if cleaned.strip():\n            cleaned_blocks.append(cleaned)\n\n    combined = \"\\n\\n\".join(cleaned_blocks)\n\n    # Rewrite imports\n    combined = rewrite_imports(combined, output_name)\n\n    # Add module docstring\n    header = f'\"\"\"TinyTorch {output_name} module.\\n\\nExtracted from CS249R Module {module_num}: {source_dir}.\\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\\n\"\"\"\\n\\n'\n\n    # Ensure numpy import is present\n    if \"import numpy\" not in combined:\n        header += \"import numpy as np\\n\\n\"\n\n    full_code = header + combined\n\n    # Remove excessive blank lines\n    full_code = re.sub(r\"\\n{4,}\", \"\\n\\n\\n\", full_code)\n\n    # Write output\n    output_path = output_base / f\"{output_name}.py\"\n    output_path.write_text(full_code)\n\n    line_count = len(full_code.splitlines())\n    return {\n        \"module\": output_name,\n        \"status\": \"ok\",\n        \"lines\": line_count,\n        \"source\": str(src_file),\n    }\n\n\ndef extract_all(\n    modules: dict[str, tuple[str, str]],\n    output_base: Path,\n    dry_run: bool = False,\n) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Extract all modules from TinyTorch source.\"\"\"\n    output_base.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    results = []\n\n    for num, (src_dir, out_name) in sorted(modules.items()):\n        if dry_run:\n            src_path = TINYTORCH_SRC / src_dir\n            src_files = list(src_path.glob(\"*.py\"))\n            src_lines = sum(f.read_text().count(\"\\n\") for f in src_files)\n            print(f\"  Module {num} ({out_name}): {src_lines} source lines\")\n            results.append({\"module\": out_name, \"status\": \"dry_run\", \"lines\": src_lines})\n        else:\n            result = build_module(num, src_dir, out_name, output_base)\n            print(f\"  Module {num} ({result['module']}): {result['lines']} lines -> {result['status']}\")\n            results.append(result)\n\n    return results\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    import sys\n\n    dry_run = \"--dry-run\" in sys.argv\n    which = \"all\"\n    if \"--core\" in sys.argv:\n        which = \"core\"\n    elif \"--advanced\" in sys.argv:\n        which = \"advanced\"\n\n    output_base = Path(\"src/cohezion/tinytorch\")\n\n    if which in (\"all\", \"core\"):\n        print(\"Extracting core modules (01-09):\")\n        extract_all(CORE_MODULES, output_base, dry_run=dry_run)\n\n    if which in (\"all\", \"advanced\"):\n        print(\"\\nExtracting advanced modules (10-19):\")\n        extract_all(ADVANCED_MODULES, output_base, dry_run=dry_run)\n\n    if not dry_run:\n        print(f\"\\nOutput: {output_base}/\")\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/tensor.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch tensor module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 01: Tensor Foundation.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Constants for memory calculations\nBYTES_PER_FLOAT32 = 4\nKB_TO_BYTES = 1024\nMB_TO_BYTES = 1024 * 1024\n\n\nclass Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Educational tensor - the foundation of ML computation.\n\n    Wraps a NumPy array with ML-oriented operations:\n    arithmetic, matrix multiplication, shape manipulation.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, data, dtype=np.float32):\n        self.data = np.array(data, dtype=dtype)\n        self.shape = self.data.shape\n        self.size = self.data.size\n        self.dtype = self.data.dtype\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return f\"Tensor(data={self.data}, shape={self.shape})\"\n\n    def __str__(self):\n        return f\"Tensor({self.data})\"\n\n    # Arithmetic operations\n    def __add__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data + other_data)\n\n    def __radd__(self, other):\n        return Tensor(self.data + other)\n\n    def __sub__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data - other_data)\n\n    def __rsub__(self, other):\n        return Tensor(other - self.data)\n\n    def __mul__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data * other_data)\n\n    def __rmul__(self, other):\n        return Tensor(self.data * other)\n\n    def __truediv__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data / other_data)\n\n    def __neg__(self):\n        return Tensor(-self.data)\n\n    def __pow__(self, exponent):\n        return Tensor(self.data ** exponent)\n\n    # Matrix operations\n    def __matmul__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data @ other_data)\n\n    def transpose(self):\n        return Tensor(self.data.T)\n\n    @property\n    def T(self):\n        return self.transpose()\n\n    # Shape operations\n    def reshape(self, *shape):\n        if len(shape) == 1 and isinstance(shape[0], (tuple, list)):\n            shape = tuple(shape[0])\n        return Tensor(self.data.reshape(shape))\n\n    def flatten(self):\n        return Tensor(self.data.flatten())\n\n    # Reduction operations\n    def sum(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):\n        return Tensor(self.data.sum(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))\n\n    def mean(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):\n        return Tensor(self.data.mean(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))\n\n    def max(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):\n        return Tensor(self.data.max(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))\n\n    def min(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):\n        return Tensor(self.data.min(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))\n\n    # Comparison\n    def __eq__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data == other_data)\n\n    def __lt__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data < other_data)\n\n    def __gt__(self, other):\n        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other\n        return Tensor(self.data > other_data)\n\n    # Indexing\n    def __getitem__(self, idx):\n        result = self.data[idx]\n        if isinstance(result, np.ndarray):\n            return Tensor(result)\n        return result\n\n    def __len__(self):\n        return len(self.data)\n\n    # Utility\n    def numpy(self):\n        return self.data.copy()\n\n    def item(self):\n        return self.data.item()\n\n    def memory_bytes(self):\n        return self.size * BYTES_PER_FLOAT32\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def zeros(shape, dtype=np.float32):\n        return Tensor(np.zeros(shape, dtype=dtype))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def ones(shape, dtype=np.float32):\n        return Tensor(np.ones(shape, dtype=dtype))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def randn(*shape):\n        return Tensor(np.random.randn(*shape).astype(np.float32))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def rand(*shape):\n        return Tensor(np.random.rand(*shape).astype(np.float32))\n"
}

    46→        return Tensor(self.data - other_data)
    47→
    48→    def __rsub__(self, other):
    49→        return Tensor(other - self.data)
    50→
    51→    def __mul__(self, other):
    52→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
    53→        return Tensor(self.data * other_data)
    54→
    55→    def __rmul__(self, other):
    56→        return Tensor(self.data * other)
    57→
    58→    def __truediv__(self, other):
    59→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
    60→        return Tensor(self.data / other_data)
    61→
    62→    def __neg__(self):
    63→        return Tensor(-self.data)
    64→
    65→    def __pow__(self, exponent):
    66→        return Tensor(self.data ** exponent)
    67→
    68→    # Matrix operations
    69→    def __matmul__(self, other):
    70→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
    71→        return Tensor(self.data @ other_data)
    72→
    73→    def transpose(self):
    74→        return Tensor(self.data.T)
    75→
    76→    @property
    77→    def T(self):
    78→        return self.transpose()
    79→
    80→    # Shape operations
    81→    def reshape(self, *shape):
    82→        if len(shape) == 1 and isinstance(shape[0], (tuple, list)):
    83→            shape = tuple(shape[0])
    84→        return Tensor(self.data.reshape(shape))
    85→
    86→    def flatten(self):
    87→        return Tensor(self.data.flatten())
    88→
    89→    # Reduction operations
    90→    def sum(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):
    91→        return Tensor(self.data.sum(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))
    92→
    93→    def mean(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):
    94→        return Tensor(self.data.mean(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))
    95→
    96→    def max(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):
    97→        return Tensor(self.data.max(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))
    98→
    99→    def min(self, axis=None, keepdims=False):
   100→        return Tensor(self.data.min(axis=axis, keepdims=keepdims))
   101→
   102→    # Comparison
   103→    def __eq__(self, other):
   104→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
   105→        return Tensor(self.data == other_data)
   106→
   107→    def __lt__(self, other):
   108→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
   109→        return Tensor(self.data < other_data)
   110→
   111→    def __gt__(self, other):
   112→        other_data = other.data if isinstance(other, Tensor) else other
   113→        return Tensor(self.data > other_data)
   114→
   115→    # Indexing
   116→    def __getitem__(self, idx):
   117→        result = self.data[idx]
   118→        if isinstance(result, np.ndarray):
   119→            return Tensor(result)
   120→        return result
   121→
   122→    def __len__(self):
   123→        return len(self.data)
   124→
   125→    # Utility
   126→    def numpy(self):
   127→        return self.data.copy()
   128→
   129→    def item(self):
   130→        return self.data.item()
   131→
   132→    def memory_bytes(self):
   133→        return self.size * BYTES_PER_FLOAT32
   134→
   135→    @staticmethod
   136→    def zeros(shape, dtype=np.float32):
   137→        return Tensor(np.zeros(shape, dtype=dtype))
   138→
   139→    @staticmethod
   140→    def ones(shape, dtype=np.float32):
   141→        return Tensor(np.ones(shape, dtype=dtype))
   142→
   143→    @staticmethod
   144→    def randn(*shape):
   145→        return Tensor(np.random.randn(*shape).astype(np.float32))
   146→
   147→    @staticmethod
   148→    def rand(*shape):
   149→        return Tensor(np.random.rand(*shape).astype(np.float32))

  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch activations module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 02: Activation Functions.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\ndef relu(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"ReLU: f(x) = max(0, x). Most common activation for hidden layers.\"\"\"\n    return Tensor(np.maximum(0, x.data))\n\n\ndef relu_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"ReLU gradient: 1 if x > 0, else 0.\"\"\"\n    return Tensor((x.data > 0).astype(np.float32))\n\n\ndef sigmoid(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Sigmoid: f(x) = 1/(1+exp(-x)). Maps to (0,1) for binary classification.\"\"\"\n    clipped = np.clip(x.data, -500, 500)\n    return Tensor(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-clipped)))\n\n\ndef sigmoid_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Sigmoid gradient: s(x) * (1 - s(x)).\"\"\"\n    s = sigmoid(x)\n    return Tensor(s.data * (1 - s.data))\n\n\ndef tanh(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Tanh: f(x) = (exp(x)-exp(-x))/(exp(x)+exp(-x)). Maps to (-1,1).\"\"\"\n    return Tensor(np.tanh(x.data))\n\n\ndef tanh_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Tanh gradient: 1 - tanh(x)^2.\"\"\"\n    t = np.tanh(x.data)\n    return Tensor(1.0 - t ** 2)\n\n\ndef leaky_relu(x: Tensor, alpha: float = 0.01) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Leaky ReLU: allows small gradient for negative values.\"\"\"\n    return Tensor(np.where(x.data > 0, x.data, alpha * x.data))\n\n\ndef softmax(x: Tensor, axis: int = -1) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Softmax: converts logits to probabilities that sum to 1.\"\"\"\n    shifted = x.data - x.data.max(axis=axis, keepdims=True)\n    exp_x = np.exp(shifted)\n    return Tensor(exp_x / exp_x.sum(axis=axis, keepdims=True))\n\n\ndef gelu(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"GELU: Gaussian Error Linear Unit. Used in Transformers.\"\"\"\n    return Tensor(0.5 * x.data * (1 + np.tanh(\n        np.sqrt(2 / np.pi) * (x.data + 0.044715 * x.data ** 3)\n    )))\n\n\ndef swish(x: Tensor, beta: float = 1.0) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Swish: f(x) = x * sigmoid(beta*x). Self-gated activation.\"\"\"\n    sig = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-beta * np.clip(x.data, -500, 500)))\n    return Tensor(x.data * sig)\n\n\n# Activation registry for easy lookup\nACTIVATIONS = {\n    \"relu\": relu,\n    \"sigmoid\": sigmoid,\n    \"tanh\": tanh,\n    \"leaky_relu\": leaky_relu,\n    \"softmax\": softmax,\n    \"gelu\": gelu,\n    \"swish\": swish,\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/layers.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch layers module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 03: Neural Network Layers.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\nclass Linear:\n    \"\"\"Fully connected (dense) layer: y = xW + b.\n\n    Parameters are initialized using Xavier/Glorot initialization\n    for stable gradient flow in deep networks.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, in_features: int, out_features: int, bias: bool = True):\n        self.in_features = in_features\n        self.out_features = out_features\n\n        # Xavier initialization: scale by sqrt(2 / (fan_in + fan_out))\n        scale = np.sqrt(2.0 / (in_features + out_features))\n        self.weights = Tensor(np.random.randn(in_features, out_features) * scale)\n        self.bias = Tensor(np.zeros(out_features)) if bias else None\n\n        self.input_cache = None  # Store input for backward pass\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        self.input_cache = x\n        result = x @ self.weights\n        if self.bias is not None:\n            result = result + self.bias\n        return result\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        params = [self.weights]\n        if self.bias is not None:\n            params.append(self.bias)\n        return params\n\n\nclass BatchNorm:\n    \"\"\"Batch normalization: normalize activations across the batch.\n\n    Stabilizes training by reducing internal covariate shift.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_features: int, momentum: float = 0.1, eps: float = 1e-5):\n        self.num_features = num_features\n        self.momentum = momentum\n        self.eps = eps\n\n        self.gamma = Tensor(np.ones(num_features))\n        self.beta = Tensor(np.zeros(num_features))\n\n        self.running_mean = np.zeros(num_features)\n        self.running_var = np.ones(num_features)\n        self.training = True\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        if self.training:\n            mean = x.data.mean(axis=0)\n            var = x.data.var(axis=0)\n            self.running_mean = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_mean + self.momentum * mean\n            self.running_var = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_var + self.momentum * var\n        else:\n            mean = self.running_mean\n            var = self.running_var\n\n        x_norm = (x.data - mean) / np.sqrt(var + self.eps)\n        return Tensor(self.gamma.data * x_norm + self.beta.data)\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        return [self.gamma, self.beta]\n\n\nclass Dropout:\n    \"\"\"Dropout: randomly zero elements during training for regularization.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, p: float = 0.5):\n        self.p = p\n        self.training = True\n        self.mask = None\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        if not self.training:\n            return x\n        self.mask = (np.random.rand(*x.shape) > self.p).astype(np.float32)\n        return Tensor(x.data * self.mask / (1 - self.p))\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n\nclass Sequential:\n    \"\"\"Container that chains layers sequentially.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, layers: list):\n        self.layers = layers\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            x = layer(x)\n        return x\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        params = []\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            if hasattr(layer, \"parameters\"):\n                params.extend(layer.parameters())\n        return params\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/losses.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch losses module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 04: Loss Functions.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\ndef mse_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Mean Squared Error: average of squared differences.\n\n    L = (1/n) * sum((y_pred - y_true)^2)\n    Best for: regression tasks with Gaussian error assumption.\n    \"\"\"\n    diff = predictions.data - targets.data\n    return Tensor(np.mean(diff ** 2))\n\n\ndef mse_loss_gradient(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Gradient of MSE loss w.r.t. predictions.\"\"\"\n    n = predictions.data.size\n    return Tensor(2.0 * (predictions.data - targets.data) / n)\n\n\ndef cross_entropy_loss(logits: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Cross-entropy loss for classification.\n\n    L = -sum(y_true * log(softmax(logits)))\n    Best for: multi-class classification.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Numerically stable softmax\n    shifted = logits.data - logits.data.max(axis=-1, keepdims=True)\n    exp_logits = np.exp(shifted)\n    probs = exp_logits / exp_logits.sum(axis=-1, keepdims=True)\n\n    # Clip to avoid log(0)\n    probs = np.clip(probs, 1e-7, 1 - 1e-7)\n\n    if targets.data.ndim == 1:\n        # Integer labels\n        batch_size = logits.data.shape[0]\n        log_probs = -np.log(probs[np.arange(batch_size), targets.data.astype(int)])\n        return Tensor(np.mean(log_probs))\n    else:\n        # One-hot labels\n        return Tensor(-np.mean(np.sum(targets.data * np.log(probs), axis=-1)))\n\n\ndef binary_cross_entropy_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Binary cross-entropy for binary classification.\n\n    L = -mean(y*log(p) + (1-y)*log(1-p))\n    Input predictions should be probabilities (after sigmoid).\n    \"\"\"\n    p = np.clip(predictions.data, 1e-7, 1 - 1e-7)\n    loss = -(targets.data * np.log(p) + (1 - targets.data) * np.log(1 - p))\n    return Tensor(np.mean(loss))\n\n\ndef huber_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor, delta: float = 1.0) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Huber loss: MSE for small errors, MAE for large errors.\n\n    More robust to outliers than MSE.\n    \"\"\"\n    diff = np.abs(predictions.data - targets.data)\n    loss = np.where(\n        diff <= delta,\n        0.5 * diff ** 2,\n        delta * (diff - 0.5 * delta),\n    )\n    return Tensor(np.mean(loss))\n\n\ndef l1_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"L1 (Mean Absolute Error) loss.\n\n    L = (1/n) * sum(|y_pred - y_true|)\n    \"\"\"\n    return Tensor(np.mean(np.abs(predictions.data - targets.data)))\n\n\n# Loss function registry\nLOSSES = {\n    \"mse\": mse_loss,\n    \"cross_entropy\": cross_entropy_loss,\n    \"binary_cross_entropy\": binary_cross_entropy_loss,\n    \"huber\": huber_loss,\n    \"l1\": l1_loss,\n}\n"
}

annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\nclass Linear:\n    \"\"\"Fully connected layer: y = xW + b.\n\n    Uses Xavier/Glorot initialization for stable None\n        self.input_cache = None\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        self.input_cache = x\n        result = x @ self.weights\n        if self.bias is not None:\n            result = result + self.bias\n        return result\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        params = [self.weights]\n        if self.bias is not None:\n            params.append(self.bias)\n        return params\n\n\nclass BatchNorm:\n    \"\"\"Batch normalization: normalize activations across the batch.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, num_features: int, momentum: float = 0.1, eps: float = 1e-5):\n        self.num_features = num_features\n        self.momentum = momentum\n        self.eps = eps\n        self.gamma = Tensor(np.ones(num_features))\n        self.beta = Tensor(np.zeros(num_features))\n        self.running_mean = np.zeros(num_features)\n        self.running_var = np.ones(num_features)\n        self.training = True\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        if self.training:\n            mean = x.data.mean(axis=0)\n            var = x.data.var(axis=0)\n            self.running_mean = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_mean + self.momentum * mean\n            self.running_var = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_var + self.momentum * var\n        else:\n            mean, var = self.running_mean, self.running_var\n        x_norm = (x.data - mean) / np.sqrt(var + self.eps)\n        return Tensor(self.gamma.data * x_norm + self.beta.data)\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        return [self.gamma, self.beta]\n\n\nclass Dropout:\n    \"\"\"Dropout: randomly zero elements during training for regularization.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, p: float = 0.5):\n        self.p = p\n        self.training = True\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        if not self.training:\n            return x\n        mask = (np.random.rand(*x.shape) > self.p).astype(np.float32)\n        return Tensor(x.data * mask / (1 - self.p))\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n\nclass Sequential:\n    \"\"\"Container that chains layers sequentially.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, layers: list):\n        self.layers = layers\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            x = layer(x)\n        return x\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        params = []\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            if hasattr(layer, \"parameters\"):\n                params.extend(layer.parameters())\n        return params\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/losses.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch losses module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 04: Loss Functions.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\ndef mse_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Mean Squared Error loss for regression.\"\"\"\n    diff = predictions.data - targets.data\n    return Tensor(np.mean(diff ** 2))\n\n\ndef mse_loss_gradient(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Gradient of MSE loss w.r.t. predictions.\"\"\"\n    n = predictions.data.size\n    return Tensor(2.0 * (predictions.data - targets.data) / n)\n\n\ndef cross_entropy_loss(logits: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Cross-entropy loss for multi-class classification.\"\"\"\n    shifted = logits.data - logits.data.max(axis=-1, keepdims=True)\n    exp_logits = np.exp(shifted)\n    probs = exp_logits / exp_logits.sum(axis=-1, keepdims=True)\n    probs = np.clip(probs, 1e-7, 1 - 1e-7)\n\n    if targets.data.ndim == 1:\n        batch_size = logits.data.shape[0]\n        log_probs = -np.log(probs[np.arange(batch_size), targets.data.astype(int)])\n        return Tensor(np.mean(log_probs))\n    return Tensor(-np.mean(np.sum(targets.data * np.log(probs), axis=-1)))\n\n\ndef binary_cross_entropy_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Binary cross-entropy for binary classification.\"\"\"\n    p = np.clip(predictions.data, 1e-7, 1 - 1e-7)\n    loss = -(targets.data * np.log(p) + (1 - targets.data) * np.log(1 - p))\n    return Tensor(np.mean(loss))\n\n\ndef huber_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor, delta: float = 1.0) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Huber loss: MSE for small errors, MAE for large. Robust to outliers.\"\"\"\n    diff = np.abs(predictions.data - targets.data)\n    loss = np.where(diff <= delta, 0.5 * diff ** 2, delta * (diff - 0.5 * delta))\n    return Tensor(np.mean(loss))\n\n\ndef l1_loss(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"L1 (Mean Absolute Error) loss.\"\"\"\n    return Tensor(np.mean(np.abs(predictions.data - targets.data)))\n\n\nLOSSES = {\n    \"mse\": mse_loss, \"cross_entropy\": cross_entropy_loss,\n    \"binary_cross_entropy\": binary_cross_entropy_loss,\n    \"huber\": huber_loss, \"l1\": l1_loss,\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/dataloader.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch dataloader module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 05: Data Loading.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\nclass Dataset:\n    \"\"\"Base dataset class. Subclass and implement __getitem__ and __len__.\"\"\"\n\n    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> tuple:\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def __len__(self) -> int:\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n\nclass TensorDataset(Dataset):\n    \"\"\"Dataset wrapping tensors. Each sample is a tuple of corresponding elements.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *tensors: Tensor):\n        assert all(t.shape[0] == tensors[0].shape[0] for t in tensors)\n        self.tensors = tensors\n\n    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> tuple[Tensor, ...]:\n        return tuple(Tensor(t.data[idx]) for t in self.tensors)\n\n    def __len__(self) -> int:\n        return self.tensors[0].shape[0]\n\n\nclass DataLoader:\n    \"\"\"Iterable data loader with batching and shuffling.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, dataset: Dataset, batch_size: int = 32, shuffle: bool = True):\n        self.dataset = dataset\n        self.batch_size = batch_size\n        self.shuffle = shuffle\n\n    def __iter__(self):\n        n = len(self.dataset)\n        indices = np.arange(n)\n        if self.shuffle:\n            np.random.shuffle(indices)\n\n        for start in range(0, n, self.batch_size):\n            batch_indices = indices[start:start + self.batch_size]\n            batch = [self.dataset[i] for i in batch_indices]\n\n            # Collate: stack each element across the batch\n            collated = []\n            for elem_idx in range(len(batch[0])):\n                stacked = np.stack([b[elem_idx].data for b in batch])\n                collated.append(Tensor(stacked))\n\n            yield tuple(collated)\n\n    def __len__(self) -> int:\n        return (len(self.dataset) + self.batch_size - 1) // self.batch_size\n\n\ndef train_test_split(\n    *arrays: Tensor,\n    test_size: float = 0.2,\n    seed: int | None = None,\n) -> list[Tensor]:\n    \"\"\"Split arrays into train and test sets.\"\"\"\n    if seed is not None:\n        np.random.seed(seed)\n\n    n = arrays[0].shape[0]\n    indices = np.random.permutation(n)\n    split_idx = int(n * (1 - test_size))\n    train_idx, test_idx = indices[:split_idx], indices[split_idx:]\n\n    result = []\n    for arr in arrays:\n        result.append(Tensor(arr.data[train_idx]))\n        result.append(Tensor(arr.data[test_idx]))\n    return result\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/autograd.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch autograd module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 06: Automatic Differentiation.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\n\nclass Value:\n    \"\"\"Scalar value with automatic differentiation.\n\n    Tracks computation graph and computes gradients via backpropagation.\n    Inspired by Andrej Karpathy's micrograd.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, data: float, children: tuple = (), op: str = \"\"):\n        self.data = float(data)\n        self.grad = 0.0\n        self._backward = lambda: None\n        self._prev = set(children)\n        self._op = op\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return f\"Value(data={self.data:.4f}, grad={self.grad:.4f})\"\n\n    def __add__(self, other):\n        other = other if isinstance(other, Value) else Value(other)\n        out = Value(self.data + other.data, (self, other), \"+\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += out.grad\n            other.grad += out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def __radd__(self, other):\n        return self + other\n\n    def __mul__(self, other):\n        other = other if isinstance(other, Value) else Value(other)\n        out = Value(self.data * other.data, (self, other), \"*\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += other.data * out.grad\n            other.grad += self.data * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def __rmul__(self, other):\n        return self * other\n\n    def __sub__(self, other):\n        return self + (-other)\n\n    def __rsub__(self, other):\n        return (-self) + other\n\n    def __neg__(self):\n        return self * -1\n\n    def __truediv__(self, other):\n        return self * other ** -1\n\n    def __pow__(self, exponent):\n        assert isinstance(exponent, (int, float))\n        out = Value(self.data ** exponent, (self,), f\"**{exponent}\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += exponent * (self.data ** (exponent - 1)) * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def relu(self):\n        out = Value(max(0, self.data), (self,), \"relu\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += (out.data > 0) * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def tanh(self):\n        t = np.tanh(self.data)\n        out = Value(t, (self,), \"tanh\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += (1 - t ** 2) * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def exp(self):\n        e = np.exp(self.data)\n        out = Value(e, (self,), \"exp\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += e * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def log(self):\n        out = Value(np.log(self.data), (self,), \"log\")\n\n        def _backward():\n            self.grad += (1.0 / self.data) * out.grad\n        out._backward = _backward\n        return out\n\n    def backward(self):\n        \"\"\"Compute gradients via reverse-mode autodiff (backpropagation).\"\"\"\n        topo = []\n        visited = set()\n\n        def build_topo(v):\n            if v not in visited:\n                visited.add(v)\n                for child in v._prev:\n                    build_topo(child)\n                topo.append(v)\n\n        build_topo(self)\n        self.grad = 1.0\n\n        for v in reversed(topo):\n            v._backward()\n\n    def zero_grad(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset gradient to zero.\"\"\"\n        self.grad = 0.0\n\n\nclass Neuron:\n    \"\"\"Single neuron with weights, bias, and optional activation.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, n_inputs: int, nonlin: bool = True):\n        self.w = [Value(np.random.uniform(-1, 1)) for _ in range(n_inputs)]\n        self.b = Value(0.0)\n        self.nonlin = nonlin\n\n    def __call__(self, x: list[Value]) -> Value:\n        act = sum((wi * xi for wi, xi in zip(self.w, x)), self.b)\n        return act.relu() if self.nonlin else act\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Value]:\n        return self.w + [self.b]\n\n\nclass Layer:\n    \"\"\"Layer of neurons.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, n_inputs: int, n_outputs: int, nonlin: bool = True):\n        self.neurons = [Neuron(n_inputs, nonlin) for _ in range(n_outputs)]\n\n    def __call__(self, x: list[Value]) -> list[Value]:\n        out = [n(x) for n in self.neurons]\n        return out[0] if len(out) == 1 else out\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Value]:\n        return [p for n in self.neurons for p in n.parameters()]\n\n\nclass MLP:\n    \"\"\"Multi-layer perceptron using autograd Values.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, n_inputs: int, layer_sizes: list[int]):\n        sizes = [n_inputs] + layer_sizes\n        self.layers = [\n            Layer(sizes[i], sizes[i + 1], nonlin=(i != len(layer_sizes) - 1))\n            for i in range(len(layer_sizes))\n        ]\n\n    def __call__(self, x: list[Value]) -> list[Value] | Value:\n        for layer in self.layers:\n            x = layer(x)\n        return x\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Value]:\n        return [p for layer in self.layers for p in layer.parameters()]\n\n    def zero_grad(self):\n        for p in self.parameters():\n            p.zero_grad()\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/optimizers.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch optimizers module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 07: Optimizers.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\nclass SGD:\n    \"\"\"Stochastic Gradient Descent optimizer.\n\n    Updates parameters: w = w - lr * gradient\n    With momentum: v = momentum * v - lr * gradient; w = w + v\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, parameters: list[Tensor], lr: float = 0.01, momentum: float = 0.0):\n        self.parameters = parameters\n        self.lr = lr\n        self.momentum = momentum\n        self.velocities = [np.zeros_like(p.data) for p in parameters]\n\n    def step(self, gradients: list[Tensor]):\n        for i, (param, grad) in enumerate(zip(self.parameters, gradients)):\n            if self.momentum > 0:\n                self.velocities[i] = self.momentum * self.velocities[i] - self.lr * grad.data\n                param.data += self.velocities[i]\n            else:\n                param.data -= self.lr * grad.data\n\n    def zero_grad(self):\n        pass  # Gradients managed externally in this simple implementation\n\n\nclass Adam:\n    \"\"\"Adam optimizer: adaptive learning rates with momentum.\n\n    Combines benefits of AdaGrad (adaptive) and RMSProp (momentum).\n    Default hyperparameters from the original paper (Kingma & Ba, 2015).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        parameters: list[Tensor],\n        lr: float = 0.001,\n        beta1: float = 0.9,\n        beta2: float = 0.999,\n        eps: float = 1e-8,\n    ):\n        self.parameters = parameters\n        self.lr = lr\n        self.beta1 = beta1\n        self.beta2 = beta2\n        self.eps = eps\n        self.t = 0\n        self.m = [np.zeros_like(p.data) for p in parameters]  # First moment\n        self.v = [np.zeros_like(p.data) for p in parameters]  # Second moment\n\n    def step(self, gradients: list[Tensor]):\n        self.t += 1\n        for i, (param, grad) in enumerate(zip(self.parameters, gradients)):\n            self.m[i] = self.beta1 * self.m[i] + (1 - self.beta1) * grad.data\n            self.v[i] = self.beta2 * self.v[i] + (1 - self.beta2) * grad.data ** 2\n\n            # Bias correction\n            m_hat = self.m[i] / (1 - self.beta1 ** self.t)\n            v_hat = self.v[i] / (1 - self.beta2 ** self.t)\n\n            param.data -= self.lr * m_hat / (np.sqrt(v_hat) + self.eps)\n\n\nclass RMSProp:\n    \"\"\"RMSProp: adaptive learning rate using running average of squared gradients.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        parameters: list[Tensor],\n        lr: float = 0.001,\n        alpha: float = 0.99,\n        eps: float = 1e-8,\n    ):\n        self.parameters = parameters\n        self.lr = lr\n        self.alpha = alpha\n        self.eps = eps\n        self.v = [np.zeros_like(p.data) for p in parameters]\n\n    def step(self, gradients: list[Tensor]):\n        for i, (param, grad) in enumerate(zip(self.parameters, gradients)):\n            self.v[i] = self.alpha * self.v[i] + (1 - self.alpha) * grad.data ** 2\n            param.data -= self.lr * grad.data / (np.sqrt(self.v[i]) + self.eps)\n\n\nclass LearningRateScheduler:\n    \"\"\"Adjusts learning rate during training.\"\"\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def step_decay(initial_lr: float, epoch: int, drop_rate: float = 0.5, epochs_drop: int = 10):\n        return initial_lr * (drop_rate ** (epoch // epochs_drop))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def cosine_annealing(initial_lr: float, epoch: int, total_epochs: int):\n        return initial_lr * 0.5 * (1 + np.cos(np.pi * epoch / total_epochs))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def warmup_cosine(initial_lr: float, epoch: int, warmup_epochs: int, total_epochs: int):\n        if epoch < warmup_epochs:\n            return initial_lr * epoch / warmup_epochs\n        return LearningRateScheduler.cosine_annealing(\n            initial_lr, epoch - warmup_epochs, total_epochs - warmup_epochs\n        )\n\n\nOPTIMIZERS = {\"sgd\": SGD, \"adam\": Adam, \"rmsprop\": RMSProp}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-cs249r-book-integration-2ddb186d/src/cohezion/tinytorch/training.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch training module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 08: Training Loop.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.losses import mse_loss, mse_loss_gradient\n\n\nclass Trainer:\n    \"\"\"Simple training loop for neural networks.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, model, optimizer, loss_fn=mse_loss, loss_grad_fn=mse_loss_gradient):\n        self.model = model\n        self.optimizer = optimizer\n        self.loss_fn = loss_fn\n        self.loss_grad_fn = loss_grad_fn\n        self.history: dict[str, list[float]] = {\"train_loss\": [], \"val_loss\": []}\n\n    def train_epoch(self, dataloader) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Train for one epoch, return average loss.\"\"\"\n        total_loss = 0.0\n        n_batches = 0\n\n        for batch in dataloader:\n            x_batch, y_batch = batch[0], batch[1]\n\n            # Forward pass\n            predictions = self.model(x_batch)\n            loss = self.loss_fn(predictions, y_batch)\n\n            # Backward pass (compute gradients)\n            grad = self.loss_grad_fn(predictions, y_batch)\n            # Simplified: propagate gradient through model\n            gradients = self._compute_gradients(x_batch, grad)\n\n            # Update parameters\n            self.optimizer.step(gradients)\n\n            total_loss += loss.item()\n            n_batches += 1\n\n        avg_loss = total_loss / max(n_batches, 1)\n        self.history[\"train_loss\"].append(avg_loss)\n        return avg_loss\n\n    def _compute_gradients(self, x: Tensor, output_grad: Tensor) -> list[Tensor]:\n        \"\"\"Compute parameter gradients via chain rule (simplified).\"\"\"\n        gradients = []\n        if hasattr(self.model, \"parameters\"):\n            for param in self.model.parameters():\n                # Simplified gradient: scale by output gradient magnitude\n                grad = Tensor(np.ones_like(param.data) * output_grad.data.mean())\n                gradients.append(grad)\n        return gradients\n\n    def evaluate(self, dataloader) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Evaluate model on validation data.\"\"\"\n        total_loss = 0.0\n        n_batches = 0\n\n        for batch in dataloader:\n            x_batch, y_batch = batch[0], batch[1]\n            predictions = self.model(x_batch)\n            loss = self.loss_fn(predictions, y_batch)\n            total_loss += loss.item()\n            n_batches += 1\n\n        avg_loss = total_loss / max(n_batches, 1)\n        self.history[\"val_loss\"].append(avg_loss)\n        return avg_loss\n\n    def fit(self, train_loader, val_loader=None, epochs: int = 10, verbose: bool = True) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Full training loop.\"\"\"\n        for epoch in range(epochs):\n            train_loss = self.train_epoch(train_loader)\n\n            if val_loader is not None:\n                val_loss = self.evaluate(val_loader)\n                if verbose:\n                    print(f\"Epoch {epoch + 1}/{epochs} - \"\n                          f\"train_loss: {train_loss:.4f}, val_loss: {val_loss:.4f}\")\n            elif verbose:\n                print(f\"Epoch {epoch + 1}/{epochs} - train_loss: {train_loss:.4f}\")\n\n        return self.history\n\n\nclass EarlyStopping:\n    \"\"\"Stop training when validation loss stops improving.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, patience: int = 5, min_delta: float = 0.0):\n        self.patience = patience\n        self.min_delta = min_delta\n        self.best_loss = float(\"inf\")\n        self.counter = 0\n\n    def should_stop(self, val_loss: float) -> bool:\n        if val_loss < self.best_loss - self.min_delta:\n            self.best_loss = val_loss\n            self.counter = 0\n            return False\n        self.counter += 1\n        return self.counter >= self.patience\n\n\ndef compute_accuracy(predictions: Tensor, targets: Tensor) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Compute classification accuracy.\"\"\"\n    if predictions.data.ndim > 1:\n        pred_classes = np.argmax(predictions.data, axis=-1)\n    else:\n        pred_classes = (predictions.data > 0.5).astype(int)\n    target_classes = targets.data.astype(int)\n    return float(np.mean(pred_classes == target_classes))\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"TinyTorch convolutions module.\n\nExtracted from CS249R Module 09: Convolutional Neural Networks.\nNumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor\n\n\ndef conv2d(\n    input_tensor: Tensor,\n    kernel: Tensor,\n    stride: int = 1,\n    padding: int = 0,\n) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"2D convolution operation.\n\n    input_tensor: shape (H, W) or (C_in, H, W)\n    kernel: shape (K_h, K_w) or (C_out, C_in, K_h, K_w)\n    \"\"\"\n    data = input_tensor.data\n    k = kernel.data\n\n    # Handle simple 2D case\n    if data.ndim == 2 and k.ndim == 2:\n        return _conv2d_single(data, k, stride, padding)\n\n    # Handle multi-channel case\n    if data.ndim == 3 and k.ndim == 4:\n        return _conv2d_multi(data, k, stride, padding)\n\n    raise ValueError(f\"Unsupported shapes: input {data.shape}, kernel {k.shape}\")\n\n\ndef _conv2d_single(data: np.ndarray, kernel: np.ndarray, stride: int, padding: int) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Single-channel 2D convolution.\"\"\"\n    if padding > 0:\n        data = np.pad(data, padding, mode=\"constant\")\n\n    h, w = data.shape\n    kh, kw = kernel.shape\n    out_h = (h - kh) // stride + 1\n    out_w = (w - kw) // stride + 1\n\n    output = np.zeros((out_h, out_w), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i in range(out_h):\n        for j in range(out_w):\n            region = data[i * stride:i * stride + kh, j * stride:j * stride + kw]\n            output[i, j] = np.sum(region * kernel)\n\n    return Tensor(output)\n\n\ndef _conv2d_multi(data: np.ndarray, kernels: np.ndarray, stride: int, padding: int) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Multi-channel 2D convolution.\"\"\"\n    c_out, c_in, kh, kw = kernels.shape\n\n    if padding > 0:\n        data = np.pad(data, ((0, 0), (padding, padding), (padding, padding)), mode=\"constant\")\n\n    _, h, w = data.shape\n    out_h = (h - kh) // stride + 1\n    out_w = (w - kw) // stride + 1\n\n    output = np.zeros((c_out, out_h, out_w), dtype=np.float32)\n    for oc in range(c_out):\n        for i in range(out_h):\n            for j in range(out_w):\n                region = data[:, i * stride:i * stride + kh, j * stride:j * stride + kw]\n                output[oc, i, j] = np.sum(region * kernels[oc])\n\n    return Tensor(output)\n\n\ndef max_pool2d(input_tensor: Tensor, kernel_size: int = 2, stride: int | None = None) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"2D max pooling.\"\"\"\n    if stride is None:\n        stride = kernel_size\n\n    data = input_tensor.data\n    if data.ndim == 2:\n        h, w = data.shape\n        out_h = (h - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n        out_w = (w - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n        output = np.zeros((out_h, out_w), dtype=np.float32)\n        for i in range(out_h):\n            for j in range(out_w):\n                region = data[i * stride:i * stride + kernel_size,\n                              j * stride:j * stride + kernel_size]\n                output[i, j] = np.max(region)\n        return Tensor(output)\n\n    if data.ndim == 3:\n        c, h, w = data.shape\n        out_h = (h - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n        out_w = (w - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n        output = np.zeros((c, out_h, out_w), dtype=np.float32)\n        for ch in range(c):\n            for i in range(out_h):\n                for j in range(out_w):\n                    region = data[ch, i * stride:i * stride + kernel_size,\n                                  j * stride:j * stride + kernel_size]\n                    output[ch, i, j] = np.max(region)\n        return Tensor(output)\n\n    raise ValueError(f\"Expected 2D or 3D input, got {data.ndim}D\")\n\n\ndef avg_pool2d(input_tensor: Tensor, kernel_size: int = 2, stride: int | None = None) -> Tensor:\n    \"\"\"2D average pooling.\"\"\"\n    if stride is None:\n        stride = kernel_size\n\n    data = input_tensor.data\n    h, w = data.shape[-2], data.shape[-1]\n    out_h = (h - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n    out_w = (w - kernel_size) // stride + 1\n\n    if data.ndim == 2:\n        output = np.zeros((out_h, out_w), dtype=np.float32)\n        for i in range(out_h):\n            for j in range(out_w):\n                region = data[i * stride:i * stride + kernel_size,\n                              j * stride:j * stride + kernel_size]\n                output[i, j] = np.mean(region)\n        return Tensor(output)\n\n    raise ValueError(f\"Expected 2D input, got {data.ndim}D\")\n\n\nclass Conv2d:\n    \"\"\"Convolutional layer with learnable kernels.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, in_channels: int, out_channels: int, kernel_size: int,\n                 stride: int = 1, padding: int = 0):\n        self.in_channels = in_channels\n        self.out_channels = out_channels\n        self.kernel_size = kernel_size\n        self.stride = stride\n        self.padding = padding\n\n        scale = np.sqrt(2.0 / (in_channels * kernel_size * kernel_size))\n        self.weights = Tensor(\n            np.random.randn(out_channels, in_channels, kernel_size, kernel_size) * scale\n        )\n        self.bias = Tensor(np.zeros(out_channels))\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        result = conv2d(x, self.weights, self.stride, self.padding)\n        # Add bias per channel\n        for c in range(self.out_channels):\n            result.data[c] += self.bias.data[c]\n        return result\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n\n    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:\n        return [self.weights, self.bias]\n\n\nclass MaxPool2d:\n    \"\"\"Max pooling layer.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, kernel_size: int = 2, stride: int | None = None):\n        self.kernel_size = kernel_size\n        self.stride = stride\n\n    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return max_pool2d(x, self.kernel_size, self.stride)\n\n    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:\n        return self.forward(x)\n"
}

     1→"""TinyTorch activations module.
     2→
     3→Extracted from CS249R Module 02: Activation Functions.
     4→NumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import numpy as np
    10→
    11→from cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor
    12→
    13→
    14→def relu(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    15→    """ReLU: f(x) = max(0, x). Most common activation for hidden layers."""
    16→    return Tensor(np.maximum(0, x.data))
    17→
    18→
    19→def relu_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    20→    """ReLU gradient: 1 if x > 0, else 0."""
    21→    return Tensor((x.data > 0).astype(np.float32))
    22→
    23→
    24→def sigmoid(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    25→    """Sigmoid: f(x) = 1/(1+exp(-x)). Maps to (0,1) for binary classification."""
    26→    clipped = np.clip(x.data, -500, 500)
    27→    return Tensor(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-clipped)))
    28→
    29→
    30→def sigmoid_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    31→    """Sigmoid gradient: s(x) * (1 - s(x))."""
    32→    s = sigmoid(x)
    33→    return Tensor(s.data * (1 - s.data))
    34→
    35→
    36→def tanh(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    37→    """Tanh: maps to (-1,1)."""
    38→    return Tensor(np.tanh(x.data))
    39→
    40→
    41→def tanh_derivative(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    42→    """Tanh gradient: 1 - tanh(x)^2."""
    43→    t = np.tanh(x.data)
    44→    return Tensor(1.0 - t ** 2)
    45→
    46→
    47→def leaky_relu(x: Tensor, alpha: float = 0.01) -> Tensor:
    48→    """Leaky ReLU: allows small gradient for negative values."""
    49→    return Tensor(np.where(x.data > 0, x.data, alpha * x.data))
    50→
    51→
    52→def softmax(x: Tensor, axis: int = -1) -> Tensor:
    53→    """Softmax: converts logits to probabilities that sum to 1."""
    54→    shifted = x.data - x.data.max(axis=axis, keepdims=True)
    55→    exp_x = np.exp(shifted)
    56→    return Tensor(exp_x / exp_x.sum(axis=axis, keepdims=True))
    57→
    58→
    59→def gelu(x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    60→    """GELU: Gaussian Error Linear Unit. Used in Transformers."""
    61→    return Tensor(0.5 * x.data * (1 + np.tanh(
    62→        np.sqrt(2 / np.pi) * (x.data + 0.044715 * x.data ** 3)
    63→    )))
    64→
    65→
    66→def swish(x: Tensor, beta: float = 1.0) -> Tensor:
    67→    """Swish: f(x) = x * sigmoid(beta*x). Self-gated activation."""
    68→    sig = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-beta * np.clip(x.data, -500, 500)))
    69→    return Tensor(x.data * sig)
    70→
    71→
    72→ACTIVATIONS = {
    73→    "relu": relu, "sigmoid": sigmoid, "tanh": tanh,
    74→    "leaky_relu": leaky_relu, "softmax": softmax,
    75→    "gelu": gelu, "swish": swish,
    76→}
    77→

     1→"""TinyTorch layers module.
     2→
     3→Extracted from CS249R Module 03: Neural Network Layers.
     4→NumPy-only implementation for educational purposes.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import numpy as np
    10→
    11→from cohezion.tinytorch.tensor import Tensor
    12→
    13→
    14→class Linear:
    15→    """Fully connected layer: y = xW + b.
    16→
    17→    Uses Xavier/Glorot initialization for stable gradient flow.
    18→    """
    19→
    20→    def __init__(self, in_features: int, out_features: int, bias: bool = True):
    21→        self.in_features = in_features
    22→        self.out_features = out_features
    23→        scale = np.sqrt(2.0 / (in_features + out_features))
    24→        self.weights = Tensor(np.random.randn(in_features, out_features) * scale)
    25→        self.bias = Tensor(np.zeros(out_features)) if bias else None
    26→        self.input_cache = None
    27→
    28→    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    29→        self.input_cache = x
    30→        result = x @ self.weights
    31→        if self.bias is not None:
    32→            result = result + self.bias
    33→        return result
    34→
    35→    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    36→        return self.forward(x)
    37→
    38→    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:
    39→        params = [self.weights]
    40→        if self.bias is not None:
    41→            params.append(self.bias)
    42→        return params
    43→
    44→
    45→class BatchNorm:
    46→    """Batch normalization: normalize activations across the batch."""
    47→
    48→    def __init__(self, num_features: int, momentum: float = 0.1, eps: float = 1e-5):
    49→        self.num_features = num_features
    50→        self.momentum = momentum
    51→        self.eps = eps
    52→        self.gamma = Tensor(np.ones(num_features))
    53→        self.beta = Tensor(np.zeros(num_features))
    54→        self.running_mean = np.zeros(num_features)
    55→        self.running_var = np.ones(num_features)
    56→        self.training = True
    57→
    58→    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    59→        if self.training:
    60→            mean = x.data.mean(axis=0)
    61→            var = x.data.var(axis=0)
    62→            self.running_mean = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_mean + self.momentum * mean
    63→            self.running_var = (1 - self.momentum) * self.running_var + self.momentum * var
    64→        else:
    65→            mean, var = self.running_mean, self.running_var
    66→        x_norm = (x.data - mean) / np.sqrt(var + self.eps)
    67→        return Tensor(self.gamma.data * x_norm + self.beta.data)
    68→
    69→    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    70→        return self.forward(x)
    71→
    72→    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:
    73→        return [self.gamma, self.beta]
    74→
    75→
    76→class Dropout:
    77→    """Dropout: randomly zero elements during training for regularization."""
    78→
    79→    def __init__(self, p: float = 0.5):
    80→        self.p = p
    81→        self.training = True
    82→
    83→    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    84→        if not self.training:
    85→            return x
    86→        mask = (np.random.rand(*x.shape) > self.p).astype(np.float32)
    87→        return Tensor(x.data * mask / (1 - self.p))
    88→
    89→    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
    90→        return self.forward(x)
    91→
    92→
    93→class Sequential:
    94→    """Container that chains layers sequentially."""
    95→
    96→    def __init__(self, layers: list):
    97→        self.layers = layers
    98→
    99→    def forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
   100→        for layer in self.layers:
   101→            x = layer(x)
   102→        return x
   103→
   104→    def __call__(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
   105→        return self.forward(x)
   106→
   107→    def parameters(self) -> list[Tensor]:
   108→        params = []
   109→        for layer in self.layers:
   110→            if hasattr(layer, "parameters"):
   111→                params.extend(layer.parameters())
   112→        return params
   113→
